#### ccf.products.refusal.RefusalEnvelope

- Surface: ccf.products.refusal.RefusalEnvelope
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — every fabric refusal type reached no wire at all; the
  product resolver seam is typed to return a servable product and admits no
  refusal in its union, so a refusal could not cross a door even in principle
- After: a frozen `CCFContract` carrying `kind`, `reason`, `key`, `detail`,
  `fields` (the refusal type's own declared fields, canonically rendered),
  `request`, and `pre_assembly_lineage`
- Why: `D-28` / spec Amendment 1 §A ruled one canonical envelope defined in
  `src/` before any door changes, carrying every refusal kind **without
  flattening** — losing *which kind* a refusal was makes a client's
  `refusal.reason` mush and kills the auditable-refusal guarantee at the seam
- Migration: none. Nothing consumed a refusal across a seam before, so there
  is no prior shape to migrate from. Producers of refusals are unaffected —
  the envelope reads refusal types, it does not change them

*Recorded by `17-02-refusal-envelope.md`.*

#### ccf.products.refusal.RequestIdentity

- Surface: ccf.products.refusal.RequestIdentity
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — no door echoed the question back, and no refusal type
  carries the question it refused
- After: a frozen `CCFContract` carrying `product_id`, `product_version`,
  `parameters` and `temporal_boundary`
- Why: `D-28`(b) — a refused result must still say *what was asked*, which is
  the pre-assembly half of `D-09`'s field split; the refusal types are values
  about what was wrong and hold none of it
- Migration: none. Constructed by the caller that holds the request

*Recorded by `17-02-refusal-envelope.md`.*

#### ccf.products.refusal.envelope_for

- Surface: ccf.products.refusal.envelope_for
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `envelope_for(refusal, *, request, pre_assembly_lineage=None)`, total
  over every type in `REFUSAL_KINDS`, raising `TypeError` on anything else and
  `ValueError` when a lineage is offered alongside a refusal the fabric reaches
  before touching the graph
- Why: the translation has to exist in exactly one place for the kind to be
  preserved consistently; dispatch is on the **exact** type rather than
  `isinstance` because an `isinstance` chain reports an unknown subclass as its
  nearest known ancestor, producing a well-formed envelope carrying the wrong
  kind
- Migration: none — new callable

*Recorded by `17-02-refusal-envelope.md`.*

#### ccf.products.refusal.REFUSAL_KINDS

- Surface: ccf.products.refusal.REFUSAL_KINDS
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — no enumeration of the fabric's refusal vocabulary existed
  anywhere
- After: a closed `Mapping[type[CCFContract], str]` of the sixteen shipped
  refusal types to their wire tokens
- Why: `kind` must come from one mapping rather than from literals at call
  sites, so the vocabulary is closable; `tests/products/test_refusal_envelope.py`
  asserts its key set equals a structural AST walk of `src/` by **set
  equality**, so a refusal type added and translated nowhere fails the build
- Migration: none. Adopters read it; a new SDK refusal type will appear in it

*Recorded by `17-02-refusal-envelope.md`.*

#### ccf.products.refusal.NON_PRODUCT_REFUSALS

- Surface: ccf.products.refusal.NON_PRODUCT_REFUSALS
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: a `frozenset[str]` naming the three `…Refusal` contracts under `src/`
  that are not product refusals — `RegistrationRefusal` (a Control Plane
  registration verdict), `EffectRefusal` (a harness effect-capture verdict) and
  `GrantRefusal` (a skilling-governance verdict)
- Why: an omission and an exclusion look identical from the outside. Declaring
  the exclusions lets the exhaustiveness gate close this half by set equality,
  so a fourth such class is a **failure demanding a ruling** rather than a
  silent pass
- Migration: none

*Recorded by `17-02-refusal-envelope.md`.*

#### ccf.products.refusal.NON_VALUE_VIOLATIONS

- Surface: ccf.products.refusal.NON_VALUE_VIOLATIONS
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: a `frozenset[str]` naming the four `…Violation` classes under `src/`
  that are exceptions rather than values — `TrustPolicyViolation`,
  `SnapshotScopeViolation`, `ProductEntitlementViolation` and
  `SingleWriterViolation`
- Why: two of the sixteen kinds are named `…Violation` rather than `…Refusal`,
  so the gate must walk both name families and partition them by resolved base.
  Without this second allowlist the `…Violation` half would stay enumerated by
  hand, and a new `CCFContract`-based `…Violation` would be neither discovered
  nor excluded — shipping untranslated with the gate green, which is precisely
  the defect `D-28`(a) exists to prevent
- Migration: none. Contract-level principal and entitlement handling at the
  client seam is deferred by the v0 not-list, which is why
  `ProductEntitlementViolation` is recorded here rather than translated

*Recorded by `17-02-refusal-envelope.md`.*

#### ccf.products.resolver.ServedProductResolverProtocol

- Surface: ccf.products.resolver.ServedProductResolverProtocol
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — no `src/`-resident seam yielded a gate-admitted product.
  The nearest one, `ccf.agents.assembler.ContextAssemblerProtocol`, sits one
  level below the vessel: its outcome carries a payload and no contract, no
  gates and no replay record. The two seams that do yield a gated product both
  live in `runtime/`, so an in-process client had no implementable path
- After: a `@runtime_checkable` Protocol with one keyword-only `__call__`
  taking `tenant`, `product_class`, `version`, `perspective` and `request` —
  copied argument for argument from the egress seam — and returning
  `ResolverOutcome`
- Why: spec Amendment 1 §B (`D-29`) ruled the resolver Protocol is declared in
  `src/`, because the alternative shapes are both refused: importing the
  `runtime/` seam would be the first `src` → `runtime` edge in the tree, and
  having the client run the gates itself would put an SDK-owned verdict on the
  serve path that `D-IMPL-102` limb 3 declined
- Migration: none for existing door resolvers — the argument list is identical,
  so a deployment adapts one by widening its return type rather than reshaping
  its call

*Recorded by `17-03-served-product-resolver.md`.*

#### ccf.products.resolver.ResolverOutcome

- Surface: ccf.products.resolver.ResolverOutcome
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — the shipped seams return a servable product and admit no
  refusal in their union, so a fabric refusal produced behind one had nowhere
  to go, and a replay reference could not reach a caller at all
- After: a named union alias, `ServedProduct | RefusalEnvelope`
- Why: the two widenings research measured to be missing. `ServedProduct`
  rather than `ServableProduct` carries `replay_ref`, which the ruled client
  result exposes and which no shipped door can reach today; the refusal half
  gives every fabric refusal a way across the seam, which is what
  `RefusalEnvelope` was defined in wave 1 to be. A named alias rather than an
  inline union because a `match` statement, a decoder and a docstring must all
  spell the same set
- Migration: none — nothing returned this type before

*Recorded by `17-03-served-product-resolver.md`.*

#### ccf.products.resolver.ProductNotResolvable

- Surface: ccf.products.resolver.ProductNotResolvable
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent from `src/`. The equivalent absence signal,
  `ccf_runtime.mcp.egress.ProductNotServable`, lives in `runtime/` and is
  unreachable from `src/`
- After: a `LookupError` subclass raised when a resolver holds no product at
  the address it was called with
- Why: a distinct type rather than an import, because importing the door's one
  would be exactly the `src` → `runtime` edge Amendment 1 §B ruled out. Raised
  rather than returned, on the split `ProductRequestViolation` already draws: a
  question the class does not answer is a bad question and comes back as a
  refusal value, while an address the resolver was never wired to serve is a
  broken deployment and comes back as an exception
- Migration: none — a deployment catching `ProductNotServable` at a door is
  unaffected; this type is raised only by an in-process resolver

*Recorded by `17-03-served-product-resolver.md`.*

#### ccf.products.resolver.GovernedResolver

- Surface: ccf.products.resolver.GovernedResolver
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. The SDK shipped every *part* of assemble → gate → record →
  serve and never the composition, so every deployment wrote it — which is why
  the rejected first example wrote it in front of a reader who wanted to ask
  one question
- After: a frozen, keyword-only dataclass conforming to
  `ServedProductResolverProtocol`, composing `refuse_request` → graph view at
  the boundary → `build_operator_registry` → `ProjectionEngine.execute` →
  `register_product_class` → `run_gates` → `build_replay_record` → `serve`
- Why: `D-29` ruled the reference implementation ships in `src/` beside the
  Protocol. The hard constraint attached to that ruling is that the
  composition is **pure mechanism**: thirteen constructor arguments, none with
  a default, four of them manifest keys read off the booted fabric's own
  artifacts — so every threshold, class name, policy key, TTL figure and
  artifact-class token stays a Control Plane artifact or a value the
  deployment authored
- Migration: none — a deployment keeps its own resolver if it has one. This is
  a reference implementation carrying no privileged status: the SDK invokes it
  never, and a deployment substituting its own loses no capability

*Recorded by `17-03-served-product-resolver.md`.*

#### ccf.products.resolver.ProjectionPlanFactoryProtocol

- Surface: ccf.products.resolver.ProjectionPlanFactoryProtocol
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — a deployment composing assemble → gate → serve by hand
  constructed its plan inline, so there was no named seam for "how this
  deployment builds Π for one question"
- After: a `@runtime_checkable` Protocol with one keyword-only `__call__`
  taking `required` and `request` and returning a `ProjectionPlan`
- Why: a *factory* rather than a plan, because a plan that interprets a
  caller's parameters has to see them. Handing the resolver one long-lived
  plan would freeze the parameters at wiring time — the *configurable rather
  than callable* defect the request surface was built to correct — or force
  every plan to be mutable, which replay cannot tolerate
- Migration: none — new seam, no prior shape

*Recorded by `17-03-served-product-resolver.md`.*

#### ccf.products.resolver.AssemblyDebtScorerProtocol

- Surface: ccf.products.resolver.AssemblyDebtScorerProtocol
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — debt was assessed by deployment code at whatever call site
  the deployment's own spine chose, with no named seam at the resolver
- After: a `@runtime_checkable` Protocol with one keyword-only `__call__`
  taking `view` and returning a `DebtAssessment`
- Why: the SDK ships the machinery for debt and never the values, and the
  choice this seam hides is larger than a threshold — measuring freshness over
  the rows a question *read* rather than over the rows that *survived being
  read* yields a different number, and which is right depends on what the
  product means. It takes the view rather than the payload because debt is
  measured against the assembly, not declared beside it
- Migration: none — new seam, no prior shape

*Recorded by `17-03-served-product-resolver.md`.*

#### ccf.client.wire.WireRequest

- Surface: ccf.client.wire.WireRequest
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — no shape existed for a question in transit. The request's
  `parameters` reached no wire at any door, and neither did the temporal
  boundary a caller asked against
- After: a frozen `CCFContract` carrying `product_id`, `parameters` and
  `temporal_boundary`, with a field validator that refuses any parameter value
  which does not survive the wire unchanged
- Why: `D-11` names the shape an adapter carries but never interprets. The
  parameter guard is what makes the outcome's echo cross-check sound: because
  `parameters` is annotated `Any`, the canonical serializer's correct rendering
  of a `Decimal` or an instant is parsed back as text, the decoded echo then
  differs from what was sent, and the decoder would report a `TransportError`
  on a wire that worked perfectly. Refusing the value at construction — where
  the message can name the wire-stable spelling — is the only alternative to a
  transport failure that never happened
- Migration: none — new type. Callers reaching it are adapter authors; a
  parameter that previously would have been a `Decimal` is spelled with
  `format(value, "f")`, and an instant with `canonical_timestamp`

*Recorded by `17-04-client-wire-codec.md`.*

#### ccf.client.wire.WireOutcome

- Surface: ccf.client.wire.WireOutcome
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — a served product crossed a door as bare canonical bytes with
  no envelope, and a refusal crossed no door at all
- After: a frozen `CCFContract` carrying `request`, `product_json`,
  `content_address`, `replay_ref` and `refusal`, with a model validator
  enforcing **exactly two states** — served or refused, never both and never
  neither — and requiring the content address on a served outcome while
  forbidding it on a refusal
- Why: `D-11` makes this the canonical serialized form of either half.
  `product_json` is text and never a byte string, which is a ruling rather than
  a detail: the canonical serializer's normalizer admits no byte string in any
  branch, so a `WireOutcome` spelled that way could not be serialized at all.
  Two states are enforced at construction rather than documented, because three
  states would mean every consumer writes its own answer to "what does neither
  mean" and they would not all write the same one
- Migration: none — new type. Note for anyone extending it: the content address
  is an outcome-level sibling of the vessel and never a member of it, for the
  reason `canonical_response` already records — the vessel's bytes *are* the
  product, so carrying the address inside them would change the bytes and
  therefore change the address they claim

*Recorded by `17-04-client-wire-codec.md`.*

#### ccf.client.wire.TransportAdapter

- Surface: ccf.client.wire.TransportAdapter
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — there was no transport seam for the client role, so nothing
  named what a transport must implement
- After: a `@runtime_checkable` Protocol with exactly one method,
  `ask(request: WireRequest) -> WireOutcome`
- Why: `D-11` — one Protocol, one method. An adapter surface wide enough to
  express policy is an adapter surface on which transports diverge, so the seam
  is deliberately one method. Its contract carries the inversion that matters:
  a transport failure never has a `WireOutcome`, it raises
- Migration: none — new Protocol. Structural typing, so an adapter conforms by
  shape and inherits nothing

*Recorded by `17-04-client-wire-codec.md`.*

#### ccf.client.errors.TransportError

- Surface: ccf.client.errors.TransportError
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. `ccf_runtime.peering.typed_http.WireTransportError` covers the
  peering wire and is unrelated to the client role; the client taxonomy did not
  exist
- After: a `RuntimeError` subclass meaning *the wire never spoke* — a connection
  that would not open, a timeout, an authentication failure, an address that
  would not resolve, or bytes that do not decode into a well-formed outcome
- Why: `D-10` reserves exceptions for the wire failing and for caller
  negligence, and `D-07` makes a refusal a value. Collapsing the two lets an
  adapter report "the fabric said no" when nothing was ever asked, which is
  unrecoverable downstream because both shapes look well-formed. Declared in
  this plan rather than the one that owns the rest of the taxonomy because the
  codec must raise it and the codec ships first; `BindingError` joins it there
  and subclasses it, so `except TransportError` stays the one catch-all
- Migration: none — new exception type

*Recorded by `17-04-client-wire-codec.md`.*

#### ccf.client.codec.encode_outcome

- Surface: ccf.client.codec.encode_outcome
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — each door serialized for itself, so "every transport returns
  the same shape" was a convention with no code path enforcing it
- After: `encode_outcome(outcome: WireOutcome) -> bytes`, which is
  `canonical_bytes` of the outcome and defines no second canonical form
- Why: `D-12` puts `Result` construction in exactly one place, and `D-13` sends
  the in-process adapter through this same codec rather than past it — so
  in-process is a true degenerate wire and a parity bug has exactly one place to
  live
- Migration: none — new callable

*Recorded by `17-04-client-wire-codec.md`.*

#### ccf.client.codec.decode_outcome

- Surface: ccf.client.codec.decode_outcome
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `decode_outcome(raw, *, sent)`, taking the received bytes **and** the
  request that was sent, returning a `WireOutcome` and raising `TransportError`
  on anything malformed, on a content address that disagrees with the
  recomputation, or on an echoed request that disagrees with the one sent
- Why: `D-20` makes the codec the single point of parity failure. The sent
  request is a required parameter rather than an optional extra so the echo
  cross-check cannot be forgotten: reconstruction alone is cheaper but cannot
  detect a transport that answered a *different question* than the one asked,
  and only comparing what came back against what was asked turns that into a
  detectable failure. The address is always recomputed and never trusted from
  the wire
- Migration: none — new callable

*Recorded by `17-04-client-wire-codec.md`.*

#### ccf.client.codec.verify_content_address

- Surface: ccf.client.codec.verify_content_address
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. The HTTP door published a content address in a header and the
  MCP doors published none, but no shipped code *checked* a received address
  against the bytes it described — the header existed so a consumer could
  perform that check itself
- After: `verify_content_address(product_json, claimed) -> str`, which recomputes
  `sha256` over the received text, raises `TransportError` on disagreement, and
  returns the recomputed value
- Why: two reasons, both load-bearing. The check has to exist somewhere on the
  decode path, and naming it makes it a function the round-trip path and its
  `D-22` sabotage control can *both* reach — a control that re-implements the
  comparison proves only that the copy fires. It is exported rather than
  private because an adapter author verifying a wire, and a consumer holding
  canonical bytes and an address, both want exactly this one line, and a second
  hand-rolled spelling of it is how two canonical forms start
- Migration: none — new callable. Note that it is not part of
  `ccf.client.__all__`: `D-03` fixes the *package's* public surface at four
  names, and this is the adapter author's surface, reached by importing
  `ccf.client.codec` directly

*Recorded by `17-04-client-wire-codec.md`.*

#### ccf.client.ProductClient

- Surface: ccf.client.ProductClient
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — `ccf.client.__all__` was empty, and there was no client role
  at all. A consumer reached a Context Product by importing the producer's own
  boot machinery, which is how the rejected Phase 16 example ended up holding a
  path to the producer's Control Plane config
- After: one of the four names `D-03` fixes the package surface at, re-exported
  from `ccf.client.client`
- Why: `D-01` puts the client abstraction and the result contract in the SDK, so
  transports become adapters *behind* a contract rather than each re-inventing
  one. Four names is the measurable form of `D-02`'s "the producer side stays
  invisible to the client", and `tests/client/test_surface.py` asserts the set
  by equality with controls that watch the assertion fail on a fifth name and on
  a missing one
- Migration: none — new surface. `from ccf.client import ProductClient` is the
  whole import a consumer needs

*Recorded by `17-05-client-surface.md`.*

#### ccf.client.Result

- Surface: ccf.client.Result
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: re-exported from `ccf.client.result`; the second of the four ruled names
- Why: `D-01` — a Context Product is valuable because of the contract it
  returns, so one result shape crosses every transport
- Migration: none — new surface

*Recorded by `17-05-client-surface.md`.*

#### ccf.client.TransportError

- Surface: ccf.client.TransportError
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: declared in `ccf.client.errors` by `17-04` but not exported from the
  package, whose `__all__` was empty
- After: re-exported from the package; the third of the four ruled names
- Why: `D-10` reserves it for *the wire never spoke*, and a caller catching it
  should not have to know which submodule declared it
- Migration: none — `from ccf.client.errors import TransportError` still works

*Recorded by `17-05-client-surface.md`.*

#### ccf.client.BindingError

- Surface: ccf.client.BindingError
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: re-exported from the package; the fourth of the four ruled names
- Why: `D-05`/`D-10`. It subclasses `TransportError`, so `except TransportError`
  stays the one catch-all for every way the wire never spoke, while a caller who
  wants to distinguish a misconfiguration from an outage catches the narrower
  type first
- Migration: none — new surface

*Recorded by `17-05-client-surface.md`.*

#### ccf.client.result.Result

- Surface: ccf.client.result.Result
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — a served product crossed a door as bare canonical bytes and a
  refusal crossed no door at all, so there was no shape carrying "the answer or
  the governed no"
- After: a class carrying either half, with five pre-assembly properties
  (`refused`, `refusal`, `product_id`, `parameters`, `temporal_boundary`)
  readable on both, and six post-assembly properties that **raise** on a refused
  result. Exactly one of the two halves is enforced at construction
- Why: `D-07` makes a refusal a value rather than an exception — the gates ran
  and the governed answer was "no, and here is why", which is the fabric
  working. `D-09` keeps everything that existed *before* assembly readable, so
  the "no" stays auditable at the seam. Deliberately **not** a Pydantic
  contract, and that is a design consequence rather than an inconsistency: the
  guard requires attribute access to be a computation, while a declared field is
  a value that would be readable as `None` on a refused result before any code
  of this module ran — the exact defect `D-08` forbids. Validation is not
  deferred by that choice, because the boundary this type sits behind is
  `WireOutcome`, which is frozen and `extra=forbid`
- Migration: none — new type. Note for anyone extending it: a seventh public
  property must join `GUARDED_FIELDS` or `PRE_ASSEMBLY_FIELDS`, because
  `tests/client/test_result_guard.py` asserts the two partition the class's
  public properties exactly

*Recorded by `17-05-client-surface.md`.*

#### ccf.client.result.Refusal

- Surface: ccf.client.result.Refusal
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent under this name. `ccf.products.refusal.RefusalEnvelope` shipped
  in `17-02` and is what a refusal has always been
- After: the client's name for that same class — an alias, not a copy and not a
  subclass
- Why: the spec's four names are `ProductClient`, `Result` (*with its*
  `Refusal`), `TransportError` and `BindingError` — so `Refusal` rides on
  `Result` rather than standing beside it, and is deliberately **not** a fifth
  package export. It is the same type because the envelope already *is* the
  client-facing reading: it promotes `reason`, `key` and `detail`, carries
  `kind` unflattened, and attaches the pre-assembly request identity and lineage
  `D-09` requires. A parallel shape with the same members would need a gate
  asserting a rename is still a rename
- Migration: none — `RefusalEnvelope` is unchanged and both names resolve to it

*Recorded by `17-05-client-surface.md`.*

#### ccf.client.result.GUARDED_FIELDS

- Surface: ccf.client.result.GUARDED_FIELDS
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — nothing named which members of a delivered product exist only
  after assembly
- After: the six names `D-08` guards: `payload`, `debt`, `content_address`,
  `replay_ref`, `lineage`, `expires_at`
- Why: exported rather than private because the guard's message enumerates it,
  the per-field tests parameterize over it, and `17-07`'s conformance suite
  reads it so the guard leg is written once for every adapter that ever ships.
  Three readers of one tuple cannot drift into disagreeing about what is
  guarded; three copies would
- Migration: none — new constant

*Recorded by `17-05-client-surface.md`.*

#### ccf.client.result.PRE_ASSEMBLY_FIELDS

- Surface: ccf.client.result.PRE_ASSEMBLY_FIELDS
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: the five members readable on either half — `refused`, `refusal`,
  `product_id`, `parameters`, `temporal_boundary`
- Why: `D-09`'s assembly boundary is the rule that decides which fields survive
  a refusal, and a rule stated only in prose cannot be checked. Declaring both
  sides makes "every public property is on one side of the boundary" an
  assertion, so a seventh property cannot appear next year on neither
- Migration: none — new constant

*Recorded by `17-05-client-surface.md`.*

#### ccf.client.errors.BindingError

- Surface: ccf.client.errors.BindingError
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. `17-04` declared `TransportError` and recorded that the
  binding half would join it here
- After: a `TransportError` subclass raised when binding resolution produces
  nothing usable — no address at all, an address with no scheme prefix, or a
  scheme no adapter is registered for
- Why: `D-05`, `D-10`. A subclass rather than a sibling for the caller's sake:
  from where they stand, an address that would not resolve and a connection that
  would not open are the same event, so one `except` clause covers both
- Migration: none — new exception type

*Recorded by `17-05-client-surface.md`.*

#### ccf.client.errors.RefusedResultError

- Surface: ccf.client.errors.RefusedResultError
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — there was no result to read past, so there was nothing for
  caller negligence to mean
- After: a `RuntimeError` subclass raised when a post-assembly field is read off
  a refused `Result`
- Why: `D-08`'s inversion — the exception does not fire when the fabric says no,
  it fires when the caller ignores the fabric saying no. Deliberately **not** an
  `AttributeError`, and that is load-bearing rather than stylistic: the
  three-argument `getattr(result, "payload", None)` and `hasattr` both convert
  an `AttributeError` straight back into the silent `None` the guard exists to
  prevent. Deliberately **not** a `TransportError` either, because a retry loop
  wrapped around `except TransportError` would re-issue a question whose answer
  already arrived, forever. It is importable from this module but is not among
  the package's four names, because a caller reaching for it by name is writing
  `except` around a field read instead of branching on `result.refused`
- Migration: none — new exception type

*Recorded by `17-05-client-surface.md`.*

#### ccf.client.binding.BINDING_ENV_VAR

- Surface: ccf.client.binding.BINDING_ENV_VAR
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — no environment variable named where a client talks to
- After: the literal `"CCF_BINDING"`, as a named constant
- Why: `D-05` step 2. A constant rather than a repeated literal so the refusal
  message, the tests and any adapter's documentation all say the same string
- Migration: none — new constant

*Recorded by `17-05-client-surface.md`.*

#### ccf.client.binding.AdapterFactory

- Surface: ccf.client.binding.AdapterFactory
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `Callable[[str], TransportAdapter]` — the type of a registry value,
  receiving the address after the scheme prefix
- Why: `D-06`. Named so an adapter plan registering a scheme has a type to
  conform to rather than a shape to infer from an existing entry. The factory
  receives the remainder rather than the whole binding, so `inprocess:./deployment`
  hands `./deployment` and `http://host/path` hands `//host/path` — which is
  what a URL parser expects when it is handed a scheme separately
- Migration: none — new alias

*Recorded by `17-05-client-surface.md`.*

#### ccf.client.binding.SCHEME_REGISTRY

- Surface: ccf.client.binding.SCHEME_REGISTRY
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: a plain `dict[str, AdapterFactory]`, **empty** on this plan
- Why: `D-06` — a plain dict inside `ccf.client`, mechanism rather than values:
  it holds no product identity, no threshold and no policy. Empty is the current
  truth rather than a placeholder, because the in-process adapter is a later
  plan in this phase and registers `inprocess` when it lands. That makes the
  empty case real today, which is why an unresolvable scheme refuses loudly
  rather than falling through to a default — a lookup that quietly does nothing
  when it can resolve nothing is a gate that goes silent exactly when a
  deployment is least configured. There is deliberately no entry-point lookup,
  no registration decorator and no metadata scan; each is a fourth resolution
  step wearing a different hat, and `tests/client/test_binding.py` asserts
  against the source that none is here
- Migration: none — new registry. An adapter registers its scheme at import
  time by assigning into this dict

*Recorded by `17-05-client-surface.md`.*

#### ccf.client.binding.resolve_binding

- Surface: ccf.client.binding.resolve_binding
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `resolve_binding(binding=None, *, environ=None) -> str` — the explicit
  kwarg, else `CCF_BINDING`, else `BindingError`
- Why: `D-05`. Three steps and no fourth: no bindings file, no per-product
  environment-variable naming, no discovery chain. The reason is measured rather
  than aesthetic — the example this phase exists to replace was 722 lines, and a
  resolution chain is how that happens. `environ` is a parameter so the second
  step is testable without mutating the process, and never a third source of
  truth
- Migration: none — new callable

*Recorded by `17-05-client-surface.md`.*

#### ccf.client.binding.split_binding

- Surface: ccf.client.binding.split_binding
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `split_binding(binding) -> tuple[str, str]`, splitting on the **first**
  colon only and refusing a missing scheme, an empty scheme or an empty address
- Why: `D-06` makes the scheme prefix the only place a transport is named, so
  the parse has one definition. Splitting on the first colon only is what keeps
  `http://host/path` intact and `mcp+stdio` one scheme name rather than two. A
  malformed binding is refused rather than half-resolved, because a client
  holding half an address fails later, further from what was actually wrong
- Migration: none — new callable

*Recorded by `17-05-client-surface.md`.*

#### ccf.client.binding.adapter_for_binding

- Surface: ccf.client.binding.adapter_for_binding
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `adapter_for_binding(binding=None, *, environ=None) -> TransportAdapter`
  — resolution and scheme dispatch in one call
- Why: one call so the client has one thing to do, and — the reason that
  mattered when writing the tests — so precedence between the kwarg and the
  environment is observable as *which adapter got built with which address*
  rather than as which string got stored. `17-RESEARCH.md` §7 names the
  distinction: a stored attribute proves storage, not dispatch
- Migration: none — new callable

*Recorded by `17-05-client-surface.md`.*

#### ccf.client.client.ProductClient

- Surface: ccf.client.client.ProductClient
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `for_product(product_id, binding=None)` and
  `ask(parameters, as_of=None) -> Result`, sync-only, holding the single
  `Result` decoder
- Why: `D-04` fixes the API at those two calls, and `D-24` fixes it at one
  synchronous `ask` — async would double the exported surface and the first
  audience for this package is a reader. `D-12` puts `Result` construction in
  exactly one place, here, which is what makes "every transport returns the same
  shape" a code path an adapter cannot avoid rather than a convention it is
  asked to follow; `tests/client/test_surface.py` asserts the one-site property
  with an AST scan over `src/`, `runtime/` and `examples/`, and its control
  passes the same scan a `tmp_path` root with two sites and watches it fail.
  `as_of=None` means *use the server's decision instant*, documented on `ask()`
  as `D-04` requires
- Migration: none — new class. `__init__` takes a `TransportAdapter` directly,
  for adapter authors and tests; `for_product` is the ordinary path

*Recorded by `17-05-client-surface.md`.*

#### ccf.client.codec.check_outcome

- Surface: ccf.client.codec.check_outcome
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — the three post-parse checks lived inside `decode_outcome`'s
  body, reachable only by handing it bytes
- After: `check_outcome(outcome, *, sent) -> ContextProductContract | None`,
  performing the echo cross-check, the address recomputation and the vessel
  parse, returning the parsed vessel or `None` on a refusal.
  `decode_outcome` now calls it and is otherwise unchanged
- Why: `D-11` types the adapter seam as returning a `WireOutcome` **object**
  rather than bytes, so an adapter that never went near this module can still
  hand the client a well-formed outcome that answers a different question or
  carries an address disagreeing with its own bytes. Extracting the checks lets
  `ProductClient` run *the same function* on everything it is handed, which
  turns `D-12`'s "a code path adapters cannot avoid" into a property of the
  checks as well as of `Result` construction. The alternative — the client
  re-encoding and re-decoding every outcome to force it through
  `decode_outcome` — buys the same guarantee at three times the serialization
  cost. The vessel is returned rather than discarded so the caller that needs it
  does not parse the same text twice
- Migration: none — new callable, and `decode_outcome`'s contract is unchanged.
  An adapter author who already holds a `WireOutcome` and wants the checks calls
  this instead of round-tripping through bytes

*Recorded by `17-05-client-surface.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.BINDING_DOCUMENT_NAME

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.BINDING_DOCUMENT_NAME
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — a deployment root had no way to tell a client anything, and
  an in-process binding address named a directory nothing could interpret
- After: the constant `"binding.yaml"`, referenced by every refusal the loader
  raises so a rename is one edit rather than a search
- Why: `D-IMPL-141` (PROPOSED). `ccf_config.yaml` is a closed-world contract —
  `ccf/deployment/config.py::_RESERVED_SECTIONS` fixes seven sections plus
  `operators`, and the module already refuses a `signal_batch` section by
  explicit decision — so a deployment that must contribute Python needs some
  other document to say where it is
- Migration: none — new surface, and inert unless a deployment authors the file

*Recorded by `17-06-inprocess-adapter.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.REQUIRED_KEYS

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.REQUIRED_KEYS
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `("config", "resolver", "question")` — the keys a binding document must
  state
- Why: exported so the tests parameterize over the real tuple rather than a
  copy of it. A fourth required key therefore cannot arrive without a test for
  its absence, which is the drift a hand-maintained duplicate would allow
- Migration: none — new surface

*Recorded by `17-06-inprocess-adapter.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.OPTIONAL_KEYS

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.OPTIONAL_KEYS
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `("connection",)` — a deployment whose backend needs no connection
  settings omits it, and omission is a positive statement rather than a missing
  value
- Why: the unknown-key refusal enumerates both tuples by name, so a reader told
  their key is not a key is also told what the keys are
- Migration: none — new surface

*Recorded by `17-06-inprocess-adapter.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.BindingDocument

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.BindingDocument
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: a frozen dataclass carrying one deployment root's wiring, with **every
  path already resolved and already contained** — so a consumer never re-checks
  and never holds a second, weaker copy of the containment rule
- Why: `root` is the value the in-process boot cache keys on. Resolving it here
  rather than in the adapter is what makes `./deployment` and its absolute
  spelling one cache entry, which is `D-30`'s actual failure mode: a second
  `load_config_and_boot` against one graph raises `SingleWriterViolation`
- Migration: none — new surface

*Recorded by `17-06-inprocess-adapter.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.ResolverCall

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.ResolverCall
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: the two things a deployment supplies that the SDK cannot invent — a
  typed `ProductRequest` and the `Perspective` it is answered under
- Why: `ServedProductResolverProtocol` takes five arguments and a `WireRequest`
  carries three fields. Three of the five the SDK holds already — `tenant` off
  the booted fabric, `product_class` and `version` off the request's own
  `schema_name`/`schema_version`, so a deployment is never asked to state its
  class address twice. The remaining two it cannot invent: `subject`, `intent`
  and `freshness` are domain vocabulary, `asked_at` is the producer's answer to
  `D-04`'s "`as_of=None` means the server's decision instant", and the client
  surface exposes no principal in v0 (`D-IMPL-129`). The SDK inventing either
  would be a half-life table in library code under another name
- Migration: none — new surface

*Recorded by `17-06-inprocess-adapter.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.QuestionFactoryProtocol

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.QuestionFactoryProtocol
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: a `@runtime_checkable` Protocol for the factory a deployment names
  under `question:` — the shape
  `examples/s1-00-companies-over-revenue/deployment.py::request` already has,
  widened by the perspective
- Why: a Protocol rather than a base class (CLAUDE.md §11) — a deployment
  conforms by being callable this way. The decorator verifies only the presence
  of `__call__`, which every function has, so the loader checks the factory's
  **return value** against `ResolverCall` as well; that check is real, and the
  Protocol alone would be a tautology
- Migration: none — new surface

*Recorded by `17-06-inprocess-adapter.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.load_binding_document

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.load_binding_document
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: reads and validates the document at a deployment root, refusing an
  unknown top-level key **by name** in the `DeploymentConfig` posture
- Why: deployment wiring that silently selected a default would be a deployment
  running configuration nobody authored, and it would surface as a wrong answer
  rather than as a failed boot. Containment is enforced rather than trusted: a
  path is refused both as written (absolute, or carrying `..`) and as resolved
  (a plain name that is a symlink out), which is the two-check rule
  `ManifestEntry` already holds a manifest to. `T-17-19` accepts that loading a
  reference runs the deployment's Python — the root is its own trust boundary —
  but not that a document reaches outside the deployment it describes
- Migration: none — new surface

*Recorded by `17-06-inprocess-adapter.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.load_reference

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.load_reference
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: resolves a `<module-path>:<attribute>` reference against a deployment
  root, loading the module under a name derived from the **resolved root**
- Why: a bare module name would be a real collision rather than a theoretical
  one — the conformance suite boots more than one deployment in a process and
  `sys.modules` is process-wide, so a second root carrying its own `resolver.py`
  would silently get the first root's code. The root goes on `sys.path` only for
  the duration of the load, so a deployment module can import its own siblings
- Migration: none — new surface

*Recorded by `17-06-inprocess-adapter.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.conforming_resolver

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.conforming_resolver
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: refuses a resolver factory whose product does not satisfy
  `ServedProductResolverProtocol`
- Why: the Protocol is `@runtime_checkable`, which is the whole reason it
  carries the decorator — this is a real structural check at the boundary a
  surface is handed a resolver, rather than a shape mismatch discovered inside
  a call that has already been accepted
- Migration: none — new surface

*Recorded by `17-06-inprocess-adapter.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.inprocess.SCHEME

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.inprocess.SCHEME
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — `SCHEME_REGISTRY` shipped empty in `17-05`, deliberately, so
  every lookup refused and a client with no transport installed said so loudly
- After: the constant `"inprocess"`, and the key this module registers itself
  under at import
- Why: written once so the registry entry, the refusal messages and the tests
  all say the same string. `D-05` — a scheme reaches the registry by its
  adapter module being imported, and there is still no plugin lookup and no
  discovery chain behind it
- Migration: none — new surface. `CCF_BINDING="inprocess:<root>"` is the whole
  configuration change a deployment makes

*Recorded by `17-06-inprocess-adapter.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.inprocess.InProcessAdapter

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.inprocess.InProcessAdapter
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — no `TransportAdapter` existed, so `ProductClient` had a seam
  and nothing to put behind it
- After: the first transport. Boots the deployment its address names **lazily**
  on the first `ask()`, caches the booted fabric on the **resolved absolute**
  root, calls the deployment's resolver, and encodes the answer through the
  canonical serializer
- Why: three rulings at once. `D-16` — the boot is invisible, so a developer
  types one command and nothing about a manifest or an artifact key reaches
  them. `D-13` — the adapter holds a live product and serializes it **anyway**,
  with no fast path, which makes in-process a true degenerate wire, gives a
  parity bug exactly one place to live, and *physically* prevents a live
  producer-side object from crossing the seam. `D-30` — the cache is not an
  optimization: a second `load_config_and_boot` against one graph raises
  `SingleWriterViolation`, measured, so caching is what makes a second `ask()`
  and a second client legal at all, and keying on the resolved root is what
  keeps `./deployment` and its absolute spelling one entry
- Migration: none — new surface. Nothing in `ccf.client`'s four-name package
  surface changed, and a client developer never names this class

*Recorded by `17-06-inprocess-adapter.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.inprocess.reset_boot_cache

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.inprocess.reset_boot_cache
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: closes every cached fabric and empties the cache. **For tests only**,
  and deliberately not re-exported from `ccf.client`
- Why: `D-03` fixes that package's surface at four names, and a fifth for a test
  affordance would put a lifecycle control in a client developer's namespace to
  buy a test something the test can reach directly. It **closes** rather than
  clearing, and that is the whole reason it is a function: a cleared cache whose
  handles were never closed leaves the writers live, so the next boot against
  the same graph raises `SingleWriterViolation` — a helper that made the next
  test fail for a reason belonging to the previous one
- Migration: none — new surface, and no `close()` was added to `ProductClient`
  (`D-30`): two clients on one binding **share** the cached fabric, so a
  per-client close would retire a handle the other is still using

*Recorded by `17-06-inprocess-adapter.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.http_api.errors.ERROR_PRODUCT_PARAMETERS_MALFORMED

- Surface: ccf_runtime.http_api.errors.ERROR_PRODUCT_PARAMETERS_MALFORMED
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `"product-parameters-malformed"` — the typed code the product route
  refuses a non-JSON-object `?parameters=` with
- Why: `D-08`, one vocabulary and two renderings: the code is the contract and
  the prose is human context, so no client parses refusal text. Refusal rather
  than a fallback to the empty mapping, because a door that quietly read
  malformed text as *no parameters* would answer a question the caller did not
  ask while reporting success
- Migration: none — a new constant, reachable only from a call that supplies the
  new parameter

*Recorded by `17-10-both-doors-carry-parameters.md`.*

#### ccf.products.door.DoorOutcome

- Surface: ccf.products.door.DoorOutcome
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. A door had exactly one thing it could say — the vessel's
  canonical bytes — so a refusal had no wire shape and a replay reference had
  nowhere to ride. `WireOutcome.replay_ref` had already named that absence:
  *"No shipped door emits this today, which is a defect the client seam exists
  to fix rather than a feature request"*
- After: a frozen, `extra=forbid` contract carrying `product_json`,
  `replay_ref` and `refusal`, with a model validator refusing an outcome that is
  both halves or neither. The MCP tool returns its canonical JSON
- Why: `D-IMPL-148`. It lives in `src/` beside the types it carries, for the
  reason `ccf.products.refusal` gives for its own placement: it is emitted by a
  door in `runtime/` and decoded by a client in `src/ccf/client`, and `src/`
  cannot import `runtime/` — so the emission and the decode agree by
  construction rather than by two implementations being written to the same
  paragraph. The two halves are discriminated **structurally**: exclusive named
  members, and underneath them `RefusalEnvelope` and `ContextProductContract`
  are both `extra=forbid` with disjoint required fields, so neither can validate
  as the other
- Migration: a client reading the MCP product **tool** parses
  `DoorOutcome.model_validate_json(text)` and reads `product_json` where it
  previously read the whole return. The MCP **resource** is unchanged and needs
  no client change. There is no shipped adapter for this tool yet — the
  `mcp+stdio` adapter is plan `17-10` Task 3 — so no SDK consumer is affected

*Recorded by `17-10-both-doors-carry-the-replay-reference.md`.*

#### ccf.products.door.ServedAnswer

- Surface: ccf.products.door.ServedAnswer
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. Each door read `.product` off whatever its resolver returned
  and discarded everything beside it
- After: a frozen dataclass carrying the vessel and the replay reference, or the
  reference's honest absence. Not a contract, deliberately: it never crosses a
  wire
- Why: two served shapes reach a door — the narrower `ServableProduct` the
  `runtime/` seams declare, and the `ServedProduct` that
  `ccf.products.resolver.GovernedResolver` returns — and only the second carries
  a reference. One function reading both is what stops three doors growing three
  `isinstance` ladders and then disagreeing about what "served" means
- Migration: none — a new type, reachable only from the new function below

*Recorded by `17-10-both-doors-carry-the-replay-reference.md`.*

#### ccf.products.door.served_answer

- Surface: ccf.products.door.served_answer
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `served_answer(resolved) -> ServedAnswer`. Total over the two served
  shapes; raises `TypeError` naming the type on anything else
- Why: `replay_ref=None` must mean *this deployment's resolver produced no
  reference* — which is the honest report for the narrower vessel — and never
  *a reference was dropped at the door*. Concentrating that read in one place is
  what makes the distinction checkable
- Migration: none — additive

*Recorded by `17-10-both-doors-carry-the-replay-reference.md`.*

#### ccf.products.door.door_refusal

- Surface: ccf.products.door.door_refusal
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. A refusal returned by a deployment's resolver reached
  `canonical_bytes(x.product)` and produced an `AttributeError` — an opaque 500
  on HTTP and an `isError` with pydantic's wording on MCP
- After: `door_refusal(resolved, *, asked, product_class, version) ->
  RefusalEnvelope | None`. A ready-made `RefusalEnvelope` passes through
  **untouched**, so the pre-assembly lineage a gate refusal genuinely had is
  preserved rather than re-enveloped away; a bare refusal value that
  `REFUSAL_KINDS` names is translated against the question the door carried;
  anything else returns `None`
- Why: `D-IMPL-148`, and the *"handle both"* the plan asks for. **No third
  channel exists, and that is a measurement rather than an omission**: every
  type in `REFUSAL_KINDS` is a `CCFContract` — a value — so none of them can be
  raised, and no shipped exception in the tree carries one. A door has nothing
  to catch and translate
- Migration: none — additive. A deployment whose resolver already returns
  `ServableProduct` sees no behavioural change at all

*Recorded by `17-10-both-doors-carry-the-replay-reference.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.http_api.routes.REPLAY_REF_HEADER

- Surface: ccf_runtime.http_api.routes.REPLAY_REF_HEADER
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — the HTTP product door discarded the replay reference exactly
  as the MCP door did. `grep replay_ref runtime/ccf_runtime/http_api/routes/product.py`
  returned nothing
- After: `"X-CCF-Replay-Ref"`, the header a served product publishes the
  versioned URI of its replay record under
- Why: `D-IMPL-148` Addendum A's *"both, not one"*. A **header** rather than a
  body member, on the identical argument `CONTENT_ADDRESS_HEADER` is one: the
  body *is* the product, so a reference carried inside it would change the bytes
  and therefore change the address they claim. The MCP tool publishes the same
  string in an envelope because it has no header channel — the asymmetry is a
  property of the transports, not a difference in what the doors carry
- Migration: none — a new response header. Existing clients ignore it. It is
  **absent rather than empty** when the resolver produced no reference, so that
  "no record was named" and "the record is named by nothing" stay distinguishable

*Recorded by `17-10-both-doors-carry-the-replay-reference.md`.*

#### ccf.products.door.PRODUCT_REQUEST_TOOL

- Surface: ccf.products.door.PRODUCT_REQUEST_TOOL
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: the name was bound in `ccf_runtime.mcp.egress` and only there. Nothing
  in `src/` could refer to the tool it names, because `src/` cannot import
  `runtime/`
- After: the string is bound in `ccf.products.door` and
  `ccf_runtime.mcp.egress` imports it and re-exports it under the same name, so
  every existing `ccf_runtime.mcp.egress.PRODUCT_REQUEST_TOOL` citation and
  import resolves unchanged
- Why: the *emitting* side is a door in `runtime/` and the *calling* side is
  `ccf.client.adapters.mcp_stdio` in `src/`. This is precisely the argument
  `ccf.products.door` already makes for holding `DoorOutcome` — a fact spelled on
  both sides of a boundary neither can cross is two implementations written to
  the same paragraph rather than one fact. The tool's *name* is SDK mechanism and
  not a deployment value (CINV-01): a deployment chooses whether to expose the
  tool and what prose it advertises, and the SDK chooses what it is called,
  exactly as it chooses the shape of what the tool returns
- Migration: none. The name is still exported from `ccf_runtime.mcp.egress` and
  still holds the identical string, so no import, citation or wire call moves

*Recorded by `17-10-the-mcp-stdio-adapter.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.mcp_stdio.McpStdioAdapter

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.mcp_stdio.McpStdioAdapter
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. `inprocess` was the only shipped transport, so every
  cross-adapter comparison in the conformance suite degenerated to a
  self-comparison and the phrase *"same `consume.py`, different `CCF_BINDING`,
  identical `Result`"* had one binding to be true of
- After: a `TransportAdapter` that launches the deployment's own MCP server
  program as a child process, calls the parameterised product tool over stdio,
  and re-shapes the door's `DoorOutcome` as a `WireOutcome`. The address form is
  `mcp+stdio:<program>` and the deployment is the program's own directory —
  stdio means the client launches the server, so a client that starts a
  deployment necessarily knows where it is
- Why: `D-06` and `D-15`. A conformance suite parameterised over adapters is
  worth nothing with one adapter bound, and CLAUDE.md §7.3's portability
  commitment is *demonstrated, not asserted*. With this adapter bound, one
  question at one instant is shown to produce a byte-identical content address
  and the identical replay reference through two genuinely different transports
- Migration: none — additive. A deployment reaches it by setting `CCF_BINDING`
  to an `mcp+stdio:` address and importing
  `ccf.client.adapters.mcp_stdio`, the same way `inprocess` is reached. It ships
  **no** server program and cannot: `build_egress_server` requires five
  deployment values, so the program is the deployment's to write

*Recorded by `17-10-the-mcp-stdio-adapter.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.mcp_stdio.SCHEME

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.mcp_stdio.SCHEME
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: the string `mcp+stdio`, bound once so the registry entry, the adapter's
  refusal messages and the tests all say the same thing
- Why: the sibling of `ccf.client.adapters.inprocess.SCHEME` and for its stated
  reason — a scheme spelled at each use site is a scheme two of them can drift on
- Migration: none — additive

*Recorded by `17-10-the-mcp-stdio-adapter.md`.*

#### ccf.products.debt_scorer.AssemblyDebtScorer

- Surface: ccf.products.debt_scorer.AssemblyDebtScorer
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. `AssemblyDebtScorerProtocol` was declared in
  `ccf.products.resolver` and nothing in `src/` or `runtime/` implemented it,
  so every deployment wrote the `GraphView` → `DebtFactors` adapter itself and
  made the seam's row-selection choice silently, in its own tree. The Phase 17
  client fixture's `resolver.py` is the measured cost
- After: a frozen, keyword-only dataclass conforming to
  `AssemblyDebtScorerProtocol`, taking a `HalfLifeTable`, a `DebtProfile` and a
  `DebtFactorScorerProtocol` for each of `C` and `S` — none defaulted. It maps
  one `Observation` per entity record, typed by that record's own
  `entity_class` and dated by its `observed_at`, and scores `F` through the
  shipped `FreshnessScorer` at the view's own `as_of`
- Why: CLAUDE.md §11 — *"Reference implementations ship alongside Protocols"* —
  and Amendment A.1 of
  `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-16-reference-plan-factory-and-debt-scorer.md`,
  which rules that the Protocol's stated objection overstates the case: a
  documented, overridable default is not a choice imposed on every deployment,
  it is what a reference implementation is. The row-selection choice the
  Protocol names is made **explicitly** here — the rows that *survived* being
  read, with its reason, its observable consequence and its override path in
  the class docstring — rather than avoided
- Migration: none. A deployment keeps its own scorer if it has one; this one is
  supplied by the deployment, never selected by the SDK. A deployment adopting
  it supplies the two artifacts it already registered plus its own `C` and `S`

*Recorded by `17-11-reference-debt-scorer.md`.*

#### ccf.products.debt_scorer.DeclaredFactor

- Surface: ccf.products.debt_scorer.DeclaredFactor
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. `DebtFactors` records that only `F` has an SDK reference
  scorer — `C` and `S` are supplied by the caller, because the SDK would
  otherwise have to invent a required-field vocabulary and a contradiction
  detector — but there was no shipped way to *state* an unmeasured factor, so a
  deployment either wrote a scorer or passed a bare number that reached the
  delivered product indistinguishable from a measurement
- After: a `DebtFactorScorerProtocol` implementation that reports a value the
  deployment declared, reading neither `observations` nor `now`, and carrying
  its own `scorer_id` / `scorer_version` so the assertion is pinned into the
  Decision Replay record under `D-IMPL-47`
- Why: the honest form of "we did not measure this". An examiner reading a
  replay record can tell a declared factor from a measured one without reading
  any code, which an unpinned bare number does not allow. `value` has no
  default on the `D-IMPL-38` discipline: a defaulted factor would put a number
  the SDK invented inside a delivered product's content address
- Migration: none — new name, no prior shape

*Recorded by `17-11-reference-debt-scorer.md`.*

#### ccf.products.debt_scorer.DECLARED_FACTOR_SCORER_ID

- Surface: ccf.products.debt_scorer.DECLARED_FACTOR_SCORER_ID
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `"ccf.reference.declared-factor"`, the identity `DeclaredFactor` pins
  into replay
- Why: exported for the reason `ccf.halflife.debt.FRESHNESS_SCORER_ID` is —
  the id a replay record carries and the id a consumer matches against must
  come from one place, or the two drift and a replay silently resolves nothing
- Migration: none

*Recorded by `17-11-reference-debt-scorer.md`.*

#### ccf.products.debt_scorer.DECLARED_FACTOR_SCORER_VERSION

- Surface: ccf.products.debt_scorer.DECLARED_FACTOR_SCORER_VERSION
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `"1.0.0"` — the version of `DeclaredFactor`'s *computation*, which is
  "report what was declared". Not a calibration and not an artifact value; it
  moves only if that computation changes
- Why: `D-IMPL-47` requires the scorer version *in force* to be pinned into
  replay, because a factor value is only reproducible alongside the computation
  that produced it. Exported beside the id for the same reason the id is
- Migration: none

*Recorded by `17-11-reference-debt-scorer.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.DATA_KEYS

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.DATA_KEYS
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: the thirteen keys a binding document states as data — `config`,
  `question`, `perspective`, `product_class_key`, `request_contract_key`,
  `operator_parameter_keys`, `policy_bundle_key`, `replay_record_class`,
  `query_pin_class`, `max_ttl_seconds`, `product_ttl_seconds`,
  `artifact_store`, `debt`. All required
- Why: spec §3 measured that the request vocabulary and the perspective are
  data — `Perspective` is two plain strings, `subject`/`intent`/`freshness` are
  plain `str` — so the factories that used to produce them were factories
  producing constants
- Migration: a deployment on the rejected shape moves its resolver factory's
  arguments into the document verbatim and deletes the factory

*Recorded by `17-12-binding-document-data-only.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.REFERENCE_KEYS

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.REFERENCE_KEYS
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `("plan",)` — a **one-element** tuple naming the single permitted
  `<module-path>:<attribute>` reference, the deployment's projection
- Why: Amendment A.3 grants exactly one Python module and A.4 requires the
  loader to refuse any other reference by name. The tuple is one element on
  purpose: granting a second exception becomes a visible edit to a named
  constant that a reviewer can see, rather than a widened condition inside a
  branch. `tests/client/test_binding_document.py` asserts its length
- Migration: none for a conforming deployment. A deployment wanting a second
  reference does not get one; that is the point

*Recorded by `17-12-binding-document-data-only.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.RULED_OUT_KEYS

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.RULED_OUT_KEYS
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. A removed key would have fallen through to the unknown-key
  refusal
- After: a mapping from a rejected key to what to do instead — `resolver` and
  `connection_factory` today — refused **before** the unknown-key check, with a
  message that says the key was *ruled out* and cites the spec
- Why: an unknown key and a ruled-out key are different mistakes with different
  remedies. Told *"not a binding-document key"*, an author of the rejected
  `D-IMPL-141` shape re-spells it and tries again; told *"ruled out"* with the
  ruling named, they read it
- Migration: delete the key. The message names the replacement

*Recorded by `17-12-binding-document-data-only.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.looks_like_a_reference

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.looks_like_a_reference
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. Nothing checked whether a *value* was reference-shaped; only
  key names were policed
- After: a predicate returning `True` for a value naming a `.py` module path, a
  colon and a non-empty attribute. The loader walks every data position —
  recursively, so nested mappings and sequences are covered — and refuses any
  match, naming the dotted position
- Why: **this is the half of the narrowing that key-name refusal cannot
  reach.** A reference smuggled through a key the schema *does* permit would
  widen the exception with no decision recorded anywhere, which is exactly the
  precedent Amendment A.4 forbids. The shape checked is *names a Python file*
  rather than *contains a colon*, because a declared `asked_at` carries three
  colons and is data — the narrow predicate is what makes the check usable
  rather than a source of false refusals
- Migration: none. A conforming document has one reference and it is under
  `plan:`

*Recorded by `17-12-binding-document-data-only.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.QuestionDeclaration

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.QuestionDeclaration
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. The question arrived from a deployment factory as a
  `ResolverCall`
- After: a frozen, keyword-only dataclass carrying `subject`, `intent`,
  `freshness` and `asked_at`, parsed from the document. `asked_at` is refused
  unless it is a zoned ISO 8601 instant — a zone-less instant is a silent
  default (`D-IMPL-38`), and which zone it silently meant is the difference
  between two different answers
- Why: the deployment's answer to `D-04`'s *"`as_of=None` means the server's
  decision instant"* is a value, not a computation
- Migration: move the three tokens and the default instant out of the question
  factory and into `question:`

*Recorded by `17-12-binding-document-data-only.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.DebtDeclaration

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.DebtDeclaration
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. Every deployment wrote its own debt scorer; the client fixture
  carried a 55-line one
- After: a frozen, keyword-only dataclass carrying `half_life_key`,
  `debt_profile_key`, and the `completeness` and `semantic_consistency` this
  deployment **declares**. The declared values are `Decimal`, parsed from
  strings and never through a `float`
- Why: `17-11` shipped `AssemblyDebtScorer` but measured that the SDK has no
  reference scorer for `C` or `S`, so the values stay the deployment's. Declared
  through `DeclaredFactor`, they land in replay under their own scorer pin
  (`D-IMPL-47`), so an examiner can tell an assertion from a measurement without
  reading code. A library default would be `D-IMPL-38`'s *a value nobody chose*
  landing inside a delivered product's content address
- Migration: a deployment that measures `C` or `S` supplies its own conforming
  scorer through its own resolver; this document's declared values are the
  no-measurement path

*Recorded by `17-12-binding-document-data-only.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.conforming_plan_factory

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.conforming_plan_factory
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: refuses a `plan:` reference that does not satisfy
  `ProjectionPlanFactoryProtocol`, which is `@runtime_checkable` and therefore a
  real structural check rather than a comment
- Why: `plan:` is the **single** thing a deployment may hand the SDK as code, so
  it is also the single thing worth verifying is the code it claimed
- Migration: none

*Recorded by `17-12-binding-document-data-only.md`.*

#### ccf.products.resolver.build_governed_resolver

- Surface: ccf.products.resolver.build_governed_resolver
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. `GovernedResolver` took `debt_scorer` as a required argument
  with no default, so **every** deployment supplied one — and since the SDK
  shipped no implementation until `17-11`, every deployment also *wrote* one.
  `17-12` moved the composition into
  `ccf.client.adapters.inprocess::_boot`, where it was one transport's private
  wiring rather than a seam a second transport could reach
- After: a keyword-only function taking the three fabric objects, the ten data
  values and the single permitted `plan:` factory, and returning a
  `GovernedResolver`. When no `debt_scorer` is passed it composes
  `ccf.products.debt_scorer.AssemblyDebtScorer` from the two Control Plane keys
  the caller named plus the two declared factors, each wrapped in
  `DeclaredFactor` so it lands in the Decision Replay record under its own
  `ScorerPin` (`D-IMPL-47`). Passing an explicit scorer overrides the reference
  entirely. Supplying **both**, or **neither**, is refused with a `TypeError`
  naming what is missing
- Why: §6 of
  `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-16-reference-plan-factory-and-debt-scorer.md`
  requires that *"a deployment that provides only data never has to know the
  Protocols exist"*, and CLAUDE.md §11 requires a reference implementation
  beside every Protocol. Shipping `AssemblyDebtScorer` without selecting it
  satisfied neither: a deployment still had to name it. **The helper rather
  than the class** because the default is not a value but a *construction*
  needing four further inputs — defaulting on the class would have grown it
  from thirteen arguments to seventeen, four of them dead whenever an explicit
  scorer is supplied, in order to give one argument a default
- Migration: none — new name, no prior shape. A deployment that constructs
  `GovernedResolver` directly keeps working unchanged and keeps supplying its
  own scorer. A deployment that wants the SDK's reference switches its
  construction call to this function and supplies `half_life_key`,
  `debt_profile_key`, `declared_completeness` and `declared_semantic_consistency`
  instead of a scorer

*Recorded by `17-13-resolver-defaults.md`.*

#### ccf.products.resolver.DEBT_SCORER_INPUTS

- Surface: ccf.products.resolver.DEBT_SCORER_INPUTS
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `("half_life_key", "debt_profile_key", "declared_completeness",
  "declared_semantic_consistency")` — the four values
  `build_governed_resolver` needs to construct the reference scorer
- Why: exported for the reason `ccf.client.result.GUARDED_FIELDS` is exported —
  the helper's two refusal messages enumerate it, and the tests parameterize
  over it, so a fifth input added to the reference scorer gets its own control
  on the day it is declared rather than the day somebody remembers. A
  hand-written list beside each of three call sites is three lists that rot
  independently
- Migration: none

*Recorded by `17-13-resolver-defaults.md`.*

#### ccf.products.door.WireRequest

- Surface: ccf.products.door.WireRequest
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: defined in `ccf.client.wire`, and reachable only from there
- After: defined here, verbatim — the class docstring, both field validators
  (`_parameters_survive_the_wire`, `_temporal_boundary_survives_the_wire`) and
  the module-private `_unstable_parameter` moved unedited. `ccf.client.wire`
  re-exports it under the same name, so `from ccf.client.wire import
  WireRequest` is unchanged; `WireRequest.__module__` now reads
  `ccf.products.door`
- Why: the door has to *name* the type it echoes (`C-01`), and a producer-side
  shape module cannot import the client package without reversing the layering
  `ccf.products.door`'s own module docstring establishes — the shape is emitted
  from `runtime/` and decoded in `src/ccf/client`, and `src/` cannot import
  `runtime/`. `19-RESEARCH.md` §2.2 evaluated the alternative (`door.py`
  importing `ccf.client.wire`) and measured it cycle-free, and it is still
  rejected on layering grounds
- Migration: none for an importer. Code asserting on `WireRequest.__module__`
  or on a fully-qualified repr sees `ccf.products.door`

*Recorded by `19-01-one-outcome-shape-and-the-encoded-question.md`.*

#### ccf.products.door.door_question

- Surface: ccf.products.door.door_question
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `door_question(asked_json: str) -> WireRequest`. One
  `WireRequest.model_validate_json` call, re-raising `pydantic.ValidationError`
  as itself
- Why: `C-01` — the echo must be filled from what the door **parsed off its own
  wire**, never from an object it was handed. The parameter is text for exactly
  that reason: a door given an already-constructed `WireRequest` would be
  echoing the caller's own copy back to the caller
- Migration: none — a new name

*Recorded by `19-01-one-outcome-shape-and-the-encoded-question.md`.*

#### ccf.products.door.door_answer

- Surface: ccf.products.door.door_answer
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. Each door assembled its own `DoorOutcome` and serialized it
- After: `door_answer(resolved, *, echo, resolved_request, product_class,
  version) -> bytes` — the one emission site for the shape. `echo` is echoed
  verbatim; `resolved_request` is what the door answers against and what
  `door_refusal` names in a refusal's request identity. The two are
  deliberately **not** the same parameter: `WireRequest.temporal_boundary` may
  be `None` and `ProductRequest.asked_at` never is (`C-02`, `C-15`). The vessel
  is serialized exactly **once** and `content_address` is `sha256` of those
  same bytes, on the discipline
  `ccf_runtime.http_api.routes::canonical_response` records
- Why: `C-01` and `C-06`. A producer-computed address is what gives the
  consumer's recomputation something independent to disagree with; two
  serializations would be two chances to disagree
- Migration: none — a new name. Returns `bytes` because the caller puts them on
  a wire without a second rendering; the MCP tool decodes to UTF-8 at the seam,
  losslessly, because canonical bytes are UTF-8-decodable by construction

*Recorded by `19-01-one-outcome-shape-and-the-encoded-question.md`.*

#### ccf.client.codec.DecodedOutcome

- Surface: ccf.client.codec.DecodedOutcome
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: a frozen dataclass with two attributes — `outcome: WireOutcome` and
  `vessel: ContextProductContract | None` — returned by `decode_outcome`
- Why: `check_outcome` **already parses** the served vessel in order to
  validate it, and until now `decode_outcome` discarded that return while
  `ProductClient._result_from` sourced the vessel from its own second
  `check_outcome` call. With the checks moved onto the one decode path, a
  caller that re-parsed `product_json` to get its own copy would be running a
  second contract validation on a second decode path — the defect `D-12`
  exists to prevent, one field over. A frozen dataclass rather than a bare
  tuple, on the precedent `ccf.products.door.ServedAnswer` already sets for *a
  value and the thing that authorized it*: a positional pair at a published
  surface reads as an accident at the first call site that unpacks it wrong
- Migration: none — a new name

*Recorded by `19-02-the-adapter-seam-carries-bytes.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.http_api.routes.REQUEST_ECHO_HEADER

- Surface: ccf_runtime.http_api.routes.REQUEST_ECHO_HEADER
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. This door published two `X-CCF-*` siblings —
  `CONTENT_ADDRESS_HEADER` and `REPLAY_REF_HEADER` — and no statement of what
  it had been asked
- After: `Final[str] = "X-CCF-Request-Echo"`, carrying the caller's own
  `ccf.products.door.WireRequest`, canonically serialized and base64url-
  wrapped
- Why: `C-09`. *One shape* is scoped to doors that **have** an envelope; this
  door's body *is* the vessel, so the echo joins the siblings rather than
  displacing them. base64url rather than canonical JSON verbatim because a
  header value has to survive every intermediary and canonical JSON carries
  `"` and may carry non-ASCII — and because the second-rendering objection
  `ccf.client.wire` records is about the **vessel**, whose address is a
  function of its bytes, while nothing anywhere hashes this echo
- Migration: none — a new name, and a new header a consumer may ignore

*Recorded by `19-06-the-http-door-publishes-the-question.md`.*

#### src/ccf/deployment/\_\_init\_\_.py

- Public surface: false
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — there was no home for adopter-facing wiring, so an
  ergonomics helper would have landed wherever it was first needed
- After: an empty, algorithmic-tagged package root whose docstring records the
  SDK-extension citation (CLAUDE.md §6.4/§10), the CINV-03 reading
  ("algorithmic" is no model call, not no I/O), and the D-15A abstraction line
- Why: the phase's defining risk is an ergonomic helper silently deleting a
  whitepaper invariant. The package that will hold every helper states the line
  in its own docstring, and the standing guard has a real, non-vacuous target
  from the moment it is written rather than being retrofitted later
- Migration: none — the package exports nothing yet

*Recorded by `15-02-deployment-package.md`.*

#### cinv02-deployment-gates

- Surface: tools/lints/harness_imports.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: added
- Before: two rules under one AST walk — `cinv02-harness` and
  `cinv02-graph-writer`
- After: a third rule, `cinv02-deployment-gates`, refusing any import of
  `ccf.products.gates` from `ccf.deployment` or any of its submodules, in both
  the `import ccf.products.gates` and `from ccf.products import gates`
  spellings, with no allowlist
- Why: `run_gates` compares the product's declared
  `lineage.operators_invoked` against the engine's `engine_resolved`, and that
  comparison is a real check only because the two values arrive from two
  independent sources. A wiring helper that ran the gate is one step from
  deriving both from one input, at which point the gate compares a value against
  itself — green, tautological, and lineage strict equality (whitepaper §7.6,
  INV-CH7-47) deleted with nothing failing to say so. Gate execution stays in
  adopter code
- Migration: none for existing code — the rule denies one importer and the
  package it denies is new. Anything else may still name `ccf.products.gates`;
  the opposite-direction regression guard in
  `tests/lints/test_deployment_gates_ban.py` proves the ban did not widen

*Recorded by `15-02-deployment-package.md`.*

#### tools/measure_examples.py

- Surface: tools/measure_examples.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — the examples tree's mechanical line counts existed only as
  a narrative table in `15-RESEARCH.md`, itself a correction of a ROADMAP
  estimate that was wrong in both directions
- After: an AST measurement tool with an exported `serialize()` and a
  `--check` mode, writing one JSON fragment per example module to
  `docs/surface/examples/before/`; `removable` (mechanical wiring, which
  rewrites delete) and `relocatable` (assertions and prints, which D-09 merely
  moves to `tests/`) are separate keys that are never summed
- Why: D-11 makes the surface audit a phase-close report and D-12 forbids a
  numeric target, so the audit's honesty rests entirely on the phase-close
  figure being a re-measurement by the same code that produced the phase-start
  figure, and on relocation being reported apart from removal — D-12's stated
  risk is a target hit by moving lines rather than by removing work
- Migration: none — this is a repository tool, not part of the installed SDK.
  Plans that rewrite an example write an `after/` fragment beside the
  `before/` one; the `before/` baseline is written once, by this plan, and is
  never regenerated

*Recorded by `15-03-audit-baseline.md`.*
