#### docs/schemas/

- Surface: docs/schemas/
- Public surface: false
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — an adopter authoring an artifact document had no schema
  to point an editor at, and marshalling errors surfaced at boot
- After: one committed JSON Schema per entry in `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES`
  (eleven today), each annotated `x-ccf-class-id`, regenerated by
  `make schemas` and held to the live models by a bytes-equality gate in the
  always-on `test` job that fails on drifted, missing, **and orphaned** files
- Why: D-11's generate-at-build posture has one standing exception, D-12's
  committed `openapi.json`, for contracts external consumers read. The
  schemas are the same case one step earlier: they are what adopters author
  *against*, in an editor, before anything runs, and a schema that exists
  only at build time cannot be the target of a `$schema` header in a
  checked-in document. Classes the SDK does not own get no committed schema
  — the SDK never invents one for a namespace that is not its own.
- Migration: run `make schemas` after adding or changing an `ArtifactClass`;
  a stale tree is a red build, not a silent difference

*Recorded by `15-12-schema-export.md`.*

#### make schemas

- Surface: make schemas
- Public surface: false
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: regenerates the committed `docs/schemas/` tree from
  `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES`; `tools/dump_schemas.py --check` is the
  write-nothing form the gate uses
- Why: the single documented command surface (D-10) — every target CI runs, a
  developer runs the same way. The failure message names this target, because
  a failure nobody can act on is a failure that gets muted.
- Migration: none

*Recorded by `15-12-schema-export.md`.*

#### make schema-headers

- Surface: make schema-headers
- Public surface: false
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — nothing checked that a `$schema` header was *correct*
- After: `tools/check_schema_headers.py` verifies, per artifact document,
  that the header resolves from the document's **own directory** (as an
  editor resolves it, not as a repo-root-relative test would), that the
  target is a committed schema annotated with a real SDK class id matching
  its file name, that the named class's contract **actually accepts the
  document**, and that any deployment manifest in the directory declares the
  same `class_id` the header names. Auto-arming: a directory joins the
  checked set as soon as it carries its first header, so each example-rewrite
  plan gates its own headers at its own wave. `DIR=<path>` narrows it;
  `--require-all` is the tree-wide form for the phase close.
- Why: an editor hint has a failure mode no runtime has — it can be wrong for
  months and nothing breaks, because nothing reads it but the adopter's
  editor, which will happily annotate a document with confident nonsense from
  a real schema for the wrong class. Existence of the target is therefore not
  the property worth gating; correctness is. Validating through the SDK's own
  `load_artifact` makes the check exactly as strong as the validator the
  adopter's fabric runs at boot, and costs no new dependency.
- Migration: run `make schema-headers DIR=<your artifacts dir>` when adding
  headers

*Recorded by `15-12-schema-export.md`.*

#### examples/00-quickstart/artifacts/

- Surface: examples/00-quickstart/artifacts/
- Public surface: false
- Change type: added
- Before: no schema annotation of any kind
- After: each document carries an explicit
  `# ccf-schema: deferred(15-13) …` first line
- Why: **no SDK artifact class accepts any of these four documents today** —
  verified, not assumed — because each is a multi-artifact bundle and the
  contracts are `extra=forbid`. A `$schema` header on such a file would
  annotate every key belonging to the other artifacts as an error, so the
  "hint" would be a wall of false positives in the adopter's editor: worse
  than no header. Plan 15-13 splits these into typed per-class documents with
  a manifest and writes the real headers then, which is also when they become
  reviewable alongside the reshaped documents. The deferral is explicit,
  greppable, names the plan that closes it, and is *counted* — a document with
  neither a header nor a marker fails the gate, so nothing falls through
  silently.
- Migration: none — the markers disappear at 15-13

*Recorded by `15-12-schema-export.md`.*

#### docs/schemas/ccf.declared-artifact.json

- Surface: docs/schemas/ccf.declared-artifact.json
- Public surface: false
- Change type: added

- After: the committed JSON Schema of the open `DeclaredArtifact` envelope, emitted by `tools/dump_schemas.py` **without** joining `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES`

#### docs/schemas/ccf.agent-definition.json, ccf.prompt-artifact.json, ccf.task-definition.json, ccf.skill-catalog-entry.json, ccf.skill-grant.json, ccf.workflow-definition.json

- Surface: docs/schemas/
- Public surface: false
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — these six SDK-owned classes had no committed schema, so a hand-authored agent definition, prompt artifact or skill catalog entry had no honest `$schema` target and could only pass `make schema-headers` with a deferral marker
- After: committed JSON Schemas, emitted by `tools/dump_schemas.py` from a second set, `author_versioned_classes()`, alongside the `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` set and the envelope
- Why: all six carry D-IMPL-28 two-field versioning and register through `register_versioned`, whose D-05 bump check holds the author's `artifact_version` claim against content identity. `load_and_boot` registers through `register_typed`, which does not run that check — so they are deliberately **not** manifest-addressable, and adding them to `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` would have silently dropped the governance they exist to carry. They are nonetheless SDK-owned schemas an adopter authors by hand, so they are committed without becoming addressable, on the same reasoning that admitted the `DeclaredArtifact` envelope in 15-13
- Migration: point your agent definitions, prompt artifacts and skill catalog entries at their own committed schema, and keep registering them through `register_versioned` rather than through a manifest

*Recorded by `15-14-examples-01-02.md`.*

#### GET /backends

- Surface: GET /backends
- Public surface: false
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `{"backends": ["<name>", ...]}` on 200; on 503 the API's existing
  typed-refusal shape, `{"detail": {"error_code": "backend-registry-conflict",
  "message": "..."}}`. The answer is deployment-local — it describes the
  process serving the request, which under `ccf --local` is the CLI's own
  process and over HTTP is the server's.

  manifest diff, so it is classified `false` — classifying it `true` would
  fail the phase-close cross-check in the direction that catches
  misclassification. D-IMPL-22's noun enumeration is amended to ten as the
  mechanical consequence of adding it; `tests/http_api/test_routes.py` reads
  that ruling text to hold the mounted prefixes to it.

#### src/ccf/agents/assembler.py

- Surface: src/ccf/agents/assembler.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: added
- Before: the `ccf.agents` package's permitted `ccf` import surface
  (`ALLOWED_CCF_PREFIXES` in `tests/agents/test_consumption_discipline.py`)
  admitted no module under `ccf.projection`, and AGNT-01 held package-wide
- After: a **per-module** widening, `MODULE_CCF_PREFIXES`, admitting
  `ccf.projection.plan`, `ccf.projection.acquisition` and `ccf.products.request`
  for `assembler.py` alone — plus a new fourth claim asserting that a widened
  module names none of `FABRIC_CAPABILITY_NAMES`
- Why: the file's own rule is that a new fabric import *"must be argued into
  the allowlist, in review — never slipped in"*, so here is the argument. Every
  symbol the module actually binds from those modules is a **frozen value
  contract, never a capability**: `Perspective` is specialization axis 3 itself
  (its docstring says *"the agent framework binds to it in I.2 rather than
  replacing it"*), `ProjectionOutput` is a `plan_id` plus a payload,
  `AcquiredContent` is the content-addressed item the Alternation claim cannot
  be stated without naming, and `ccf.products.request` reaches no fabric layer
  at all. The widening is **bounded rather than trusted**, which is the part
  that matters: admitting the module that *holds* `ContentAcquirer` and
  `ProjectionPlan` does not admit reaching for either, and the new claim runs
  over every module in the package, so a capability arriving through one of
  these doors is a red build. It is per-module rather than package-wide so the
  prefix does not silently become admissible for `agent.py`
- Migration: none — a test-scope invariant. An adopter is unaffected

*Recorded by `15.5-10-assembler.md`.*

#### The width ceiling, mechanized

- Surface: tests/mcp/test_egress_parameters.py::assert_same_verdicts
- Public surface: false
- Change type: added
- Before: nothing held the two doors' parameter surfaces to each other. They
  drifted into identical hard-wirings independently and were repaired
  independently in plan `16-23` and `D-IMPL-108`
- After: `assert_same_verdicts(cases, ask_mcp, ask_http)` runs **one shared
  case table** against both real doors and requires identical verdicts, where a
  verdict is what `refuse_request` says about the request the door actually
  carried. Five cases: served, served-with-optional, empty, undeclared, and
  wrong-typed. The assertion **refuses a table in which no case is served** —
  without that guard the table would have passed against the two unrepaired
  doors, which carried `{}` and therefore agreed perfectly while carrying
  nothing
- Why: a per-door table lets the two drift, which is how the doors got here.
  `D-22`: the two controls pass a deliberately-wider fake door and a coercing
  fake door into the **real** assertions rather than into copies of them
- Migration: none

*Recorded by `17-10-both-doors-carry-parameters.md`.*

  place. Pydantic copies a model's default on every validation, so no call
  receives this instance
- Why: a literal `{}` in the signature would carry the same schema and none of

- Migration: none — a module-level constant, not exported

*Recorded by `18-99-close-review-blocking-fixes.md`.*

## Reshaped

Surfaces that still exist under the same name but whose shape moved. These are the entries an existing integration must read.

293 entries — 110 public Python surface, 183 not public Python API.

### Public Python surface

#### ccf.assembly.worker.EntityResolutionSpec

- Surface: ccf.assembly.worker.EntityResolutionSpec
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: a `CCFContract` with `entity_rules` and `relationship_rules` and no
  version field of any kind
- After: inherits `ccf.controlplane.artifact_base.VersionedArtifact`; `semver`
  is required and has **no default**
- Why: D-IMPL-77. The class's own docstring already called it "a Control Plane
  artifact, versioned like any other" — the field's absence made that sentence
  aspirational, and was one of exactly three things keeping the spec away from
  the `parse_artifact` seam. A **wrapper** holding `semver` beside the spec was
  rejected: `ccf.boot.boot` would then receive a wrapper where it expects a
  spec, changing `boot()`'s signature — the one contract D-05 committed to
  preserving. The wrapper trades a data change for a contract change, which is
  the more expensive of the two. No default, because a default lane is a version
  nobody chose and the registry's D-05 bump check would then compare content
  hashes against a lane the author never asserted
- Migration: **the content hash changes.** `semver` participates in
  `content_hash(spec)`, so every `resolution_spec_version` pin minted after this
  change differs from one minted before it, and every Decision Replay record
  carrying such a pin is pinned to a new value. Existing replay records remain
  valid against the artifact versions they name — nothing is invalidated and no
  record changes shape — but a re-registration of the *same* resolution rules
  now produces a different pin than it did before. Constructing an
  `EntityResolutionSpec` without `semver` is now a validation error: state the
  lane, and prefer reading it from the artifact rather than inlining one
  (CLAUDE.md §6.4)

*Recorded by `15-07-resolution-spec.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ManifestEntry

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ManifestEntry
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `path`, `key`, `class_id`, `role` — four required fields, and nothing said
  which registration path the entry travelled. `load_and_boot` called `register_typed`
  for every `role: register` entry unconditionally
- After: a fifth required field, `registration: RegistrationMode`, with **no default**
- Why: D-IMPL-85 (Dave, 2026-08-05). Either default would have the SDK deciding the
  *governance path* by silence for every entry that omitted it — a deployment would
  inherit or lose the D-05 bump check with no document saying so, which is exactly the
  convention-mistaken-for-enforcement failure this phase exists to close (T-15.1-02-01).
  The argument is a sibling of `role`'s, not a copy of it: `role`'s no-default reasoning
  is about a replay-dimension-2 *boundary*, and this one is about which governance
  obligations *run*. Rejected: optional-defaulting-to-`typed`, which is the cheap
  migration and the silent choice at once. The cost was accepted and measured with the
  ruling — 163 entries across 11 manifests plus the deployment test fixtures, one
  mechanical wave, and no external adopters to carry it (private repo, pre-1.0)
- Migration: **every manifest entry must now declare `registration`.** The mechanical
  edit is one line per entry, directly under `role`:

*Recorded by `15.1-02-registration-mode.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.pins.ArtifactPin

- Surface: ccf.deployment.pins.ArtifactPin
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `key`, `version`, `class_id` — the pin recorded what was in force and under
  which contract, and said nothing about how it got there
- After: a fourth required field, `registration: RegistrationMode`, carrying the mode
  the entry actually travelled
- Why: D-IMPL-83 (Dave, 2026-08-05). Bump evidence had to be reachable from the returned
  pins, and `ResolvedPins` is under an explicit no-growth constraint pinned by
  `tests/deployment/test_manifest.py::test_resolved_pins_has_exactly_one_public_method`.
  The reading adopted: that constraint forbids **methods that select**, not **fields
  that record** — `class_id` is already a recorded fact of the same kind — so the mode
  lands as a field and `ResolvedPins` gains no accessor. The audit log remains the
  **primary** evidence: `register_versioned` already audits every outcome with
  `action="register_versioned"` and `resolved=True|False`, which is how
  `test_the_author_versioned_definitions_kept_their_bump_check` reads it today. This
  field is the convenience for a reader holding the pins and no audit log. Rejected:
  audit-log-only, which leaves the evidence unreachable from the returned pins; and a
  `ResolvedPins` accessor, which contradicts the no-growth constraint directly
- Migration: **the pin set's content hash changes** — the field is additive to a
  `CCFContract`, so anything that recorded a pin-set hash before this plan records a
  different one after it. Determinism is unaffected:
  `test_two_loads_of_one_manifest_produce_an_identical_pin_set` still passes because
  both loads change identically. Code constructing an `ArtifactPin` directly must supply
  the field; code reading `version_for` is untouched

*Recorded by `15.1-02-registration-mode.md`.*

#### ccf.boot.boot

- Surface: ccf.boot.boot
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `boot(*, database_path=None, postgres_dsn=None, postgres_schema=…,
  registry, …)` — exactly one of `database_path` / `postgres_dsn` selected
  the substrate
- After: a `memory: bool = False` keyword joins the selector set; the
  exclusivity check now reads exactly one of {`database_path`,
  `postgres_dsn`, `memory=True`}, refused in the same
  the-fabric-does-not-infer-its-substrate ValueError style. The memory
  branch constructs `MemoryGraphStore` and a `MemoryGraphWriter`, so every
  `memory=True` boot is its own graph
- Why: explicit selection, never inference (the `_open_backend` posture) —
  the memory backend must be a statement, not a fallback a missing
  connection argument degrades to
- Migration: none for existing callers — the new keyword defaults to
  `False` and both existing selectors are untouched

*Recorded by `15.2-06-memory-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.DeploymentManifest

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.DeploymentManifest
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `backend: Literal["sqlite", "postgres"]` — every deployment
  manifest had to name a substrate the environment supplies a connection
  value for
- After: `backend: Literal["sqlite", "postgres", "memory"]`. A `memory`
  manifest needs no connection field and no environment argument — the D-08
  kind-only posture completed for a substrate with nothing to connect to.
  `load_and_boot` maps `backend: memory` to `boot(memory=True)`, and its
  preflight refuses a `memory` declaration accompanied by `database_path`
  or `postgres_dsn` exactly as it refuses the wrong backend's argument. The
  re-export `ccf.deployment.DeploymentManifest` reshapes identically. The
  committed `docs/schemas/ccf.deployment-manifest.json` regenerated
  (ERGO-12)
- Why: D-06 — a fabric that boots from a manifest must be able to name the
  always-on floor; SUBQ-08's memory leg runs through the same one call every
  other deployment uses
- Migration: none for existing manifests — both existing kinds are
  untouched; new manifests may declare `backend: memory` and supply no
  connection argument

*Recorded by `15.2-06-memory-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.projection.engine.ProjectionEngine

- Surface: ccf.projection.engine.ProjectionEngine
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: constructor `(reader, registry, acquirer)`; `execute()` performed
  one `reader.as_of()` everything-read and ran all operators in-process
- After: constructor gains keyword-only `survivor_cap: int | None = None`
  (D-10 — mechanism only; the value is Control Plane configuration, wired in
  plan 15.2-08) and a `last_survivor_set_sizes: tuple[int, ...]` telemetry
  property (per-execution, reset at `execute()` start, deliberately outside
  the replay record, report-only). `execute()` — public signature unchanged
  — now splits the plan and issues one `reader.query()` per pushable
  segment, re-applying each pushed segment's operators over its answer
  (`GQ-19`) and threading survivors as `allowed_entities` (`GQ-15`)
- Why: the D-IMPL-87 resolution's execution design: per-segment questions
  instead of the everything-read, with the resurrection defect closed at
  the seam and the cap decision Control-Plane-valued (D-10)
- Migration: none — every product, provenance chain, and gate outcome is
  byte-identical to the pre-segmentation path (pinned for both plan shapes
  in `tests/projection/test_segmentation.py`); constructing with the
  default `survivor_cap=None` preserves the no-cap posture

*Recorded by `15.2-07-read-intent-segmentation.md`.*

#### ccf.projection.engine.ExecutionResult

- Surface: ccf.projection.engine.ExecutionResult
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: no record of the substrate questions an execution asked
- After: gains sentinel-defaulted `executed_queries: tuple[GraphQuery, ...]
  = ()` — the issued queries in execution order, appended at each
  `reader.query()` call site (`GQ-20`); a cap firing is visible as an
  absent entry
- Why: plan 15.2-08 threads the per-round-trip `content_hash` pins from
  this field into the Decision Replay record (D-11) without reshaping the
  loop; the engine records what ran, never what the plan predicted
- Migration: none — the field defaults to the empty tuple; existing
  consumers of `ExecutionResult` are unchanged

*Recorded by `15.2-07-read-intent-segmentation.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.serve.serve

- Surface: ccf.replay.serve.serve
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `serve(product, record, replay_writer)` — append the record, then
  deliver
- After: gains keyword-only `query_pin_writer: QueryPinWriter | None = None`
  and `executed_queries: tuple[GraphQuery, ...] = ()`; when the seam is
  handed the executed queries their canonical bytes are persisted **before**
  the replay-record append, after an element-for-element consistency check
  against the record's `graph_query_sequence`
- Why: D-11's ordering commitment — no moment exists at which an appended
  record pins bytes the store does not hold (T-15.2-28)
- Migration: none — both parameters default to the pre-15.2-08 posture, so
  every existing call site is byte-for-byte unchanged; the governed path
  passes `execution.executed_queries` plus a `QueryPinWriter`

*Recorded by `15.2-08-replay-query-pins.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.record.ContentGenerationPath

- Surface: ccf.replay.record.ContentGenerationPath
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: dimension 5 recorded the plan, steps, product address, and the
  workflow step sequence; nothing recorded the substrate questions asked
- After: gains sentinel-defaulted `graph_query_sequence: tuple[str, ...] =
  NO_GRAPH_QUERIES` — one `content_hash` per issued `GraphQuery`, in
  execution order; a Similarity-bearing execution carries two pins, a
  cap-firing execution carries one — the record shows what ran
- Why: D-11 (D-IMPL-87 lineage) — replay recomputes the sequence and
  compares hashes; the pins derive from the engine's executed-queries record
  (`GQ-20`), never plan-side prediction (T-15.2-15)
- Migration: none — the sentinel default; stored records lacking the field
  rebuild to it and their stored content addresses still verify (D-IMPL-89)

*Recorded by `15.2-08-replay-query-pins.md`.*

- Surface: ccf.replay.record.PolicyBindingsSnapshot

- Change type: reshaped
- Before: dimension 2 recorded the bindings and the HITL actions block
- After: gains sentinel-defaulted `survivor_cap: int | None = None` — the

- Why: the pushed/in-process split depends on plan + data + this value

- Surface: ccf.projection.engine.ExecutionResult
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: carried `executed_queries` (15.2-07) but not the cap in force
- After: gains sentinel-defaulted `survivor_cap: int | None = None` — the
  engine's constructor value echoed onto the result, whether or not any

- Why: the dimension-2 pin needs the value in force to travel the same

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.DeploymentManifest

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.DeploymentManifest
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: no Control Plane home existed for the D-10 cap value; only direct
  `ProjectionEngine` construction could set it
- After: gains `survivor_cap: int | None = None` — deployment-wide,
  schema-gated (docs/schemas/ccf.deployment-manifest.json regenerated);
  absent parses to `None` = no cap configured
- Why: D-10 — the value is Control Plane configuration and the mechanism
  ships regardless (CLAUDE.md §11); a cap change becomes a versioned
  manifest change with an audit trail (T-15.2-27); deployment-wide because
  the manifest is the versioned document with existing schema machinery, and
  a per-product-class override can arrive later without a contract break
- Migration: none — existing manifests parse unchanged

*Recorded by `15.2-08-replay-query-pins.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.manifest.BootedFabric

- Surface: ccf.deployment.manifest.BootedFabric
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `handle`, `pins`, `tenant_id`, `artifacts`
- After: gains `survivor_cap: int | None` — the manifest's value, verbatim;
  the adopter passes it to `ProjectionEngine(survivor_cap=...)` at
  construction
- Why: value-threading only — `load_and_boot` constructs no engine (the
  D-IMPL-86 correction's boundary), so the governed route ends at the boot

  to the engine themselves
- Migration: none — `load_and_boot` is the only constructor

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ProductClassDeclaration

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ProductClassDeclaration
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the declaration carried no statement of which source-attestation
  modes the class accepts — every class implicitly consumed whatever source
  the deployment wired
- After: gains `accepted_policy_binding_modes: frozenset[PolicyBindingMode]
  = {"assembly-stamped"}` and `accepted_lineage_modes: frozenset[LineageMode]
  = {"graph-supplied"}` — unordered accepted-mode **sets** (not ordered
  minima; no strength ordering between `assembly-stamped` and
  `admission-verified` is invented), defaulting to the tier-1 posture;
  `docs/schemas/ccf.product-class.json` regenerated
- Why: D-09 — a product class must be able to demand provenance; the tier-1
  defaults make the demand every pre-attestation class already implicitly
  made, so consuming a tier-2 source becomes an explicit authored opt-in
  (deny-by-default) and nothing existing changes behavior
- Migration: none — existing YAML validates unchanged (the defaults);
  a class that should consume a mounted admitted product declares
  `accepted_policy_binding_modes: [assembly-stamped, admission-verified]`
  and `accepted_lineage_modes: [graph-supplied, product-carried]`

*Recorded by `15.2-11-attestation-enforcement.md`.*

- Surface: ccf.products.contract.RegisteredProductClass
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: carried the class's registration-time governance (schema lane,

- After: gains `accepted_policy_binding_modes` / `accepted_lineage_modes`

- Why: `run_gates` receives the registered class, so carrying the accepted

- Migration: none — both fields default to the tier-1 posture; every

*Recorded by `15.2-11-attestation-enforcement.md`.*

#### ccf.products.gates.run_gates

- Surface: ccf.products.gates.run_gates
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the canonical three-gate sequence over
  `(candidate, engine_resolved, now, registry, product_class)`
- After: gains keyword-only `source_attestation: SourceAttestation | None =
  None` — the pinned attestation of the execution that produced the
  candidate (the governed path passes `execution.source_attestation`);
  when present and below the class's accepted modes the additive gate
  returns `AttestationRefusal` after the canonical three, and no
  `ServableProduct` exists. The three pre-existing gates' code, docstrings,
  order, and refusal precedence are byte-untouched; the return union is
  unchanged
- Why: the candidate contract cannot carry its source's attestation (a
  product does not attest its own source; the execution record does), so
  the pin arrives as the plan-anticipated keyword-only parameter with a
  `None` default — the only shape that leaves every pre-attestation call
  site byte-for-byte unchanged
- Migration: none for existing callers (`None` passes); the governed
  serving path adds `source_attestation=execution.source_attestation`

*Recorded by `15.2-11-attestation-enforcement.md`.*

#### ccf.projection.engine.ProjectionEngine

- Surface: ccf.projection.engine.ProjectionEngine
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: no pre-execute attestation surface; `execute()` did not record
  the source's attestation
- After: gains `check_source_attestation(*, product_class) ->
  AttestationRefusal | None` — the caller-visible pre-execute check: zero
  queries (it touches only the reader's `attestation` property), refusal
  as a returned value, the same `refuse_attestation` comparison the gate
  path runs. `execute()`'s signature and return annotation are
  byte-identical to plan 15.2-08's
- Why: the D-09 early layer — a below-modes source is refused before the
  first byte moves; the check is advisory in position but not in effect,
  because the unconditional pin plus the gate path make `ServableProduct`
  unconstructible for a below-modes source regardless
- Migration: none — new method; serving code calls it before `execute()`
  in the same match-composition the gates use

*Recorded by `15.2-11-attestation-enforcement.md`.*

#### ccf.projection.engine.ExecutionResult

- Surface: ccf.projection.engine.ExecutionResult
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: carried `executed_queries` and `survivor_cap` (15.2-07/08) but
  not the source's attestation
- After: gains sentinel-defaulted `source_attestation: SourceAttestation |
  None = None` — the reader's attestation as observed at the read seam,
  threaded by `execute()` **unconditionally**; `None` is the positive
  "no attestation was reported" claim (a pre-phase reader), which the gate
  passes
- Why: the dimension-1 pin needs the value in force to travel the same
  channel every other execution fact travels (the 15.2-08 precedent); the
  unconditional threading is what makes even a bypassing caller's read
  path repudiation-proof (T-15.2-25)
- Migration: none — the field defaults to `None`; `execute()`'s signature
  is byte-identical

*Recorded by `15.2-11-attestation-enforcement.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.record.ContextFabricSnapshot

- Surface: ccf.replay.record.ContextFabricSnapshot
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: dimension 1 recorded the four D-IMPL-06 constituents and the
  derived snapshot id; nothing recorded how the source vouched for the
  state read
- After: gains sentinel-defaulted `source_attestation: SourceAttestation |
  None = None`, with `None` documented as the positive assertion "recorded
  before source attestation existed"; `build()` gains the matching
  keyword-only parameter. The field is deliberately **not** a
  `graph_snapshot_id` constituent — the id identifies graph state, so
  re-derived ids stay comparable across records written before and after
  the field
- Why: D-09 — the attestation in force at decision time is a dimension-1
  fact (what state/source the decision drew from); pinned verbatim, so
  even a read path that skipped every check leaves its provenance posture
  in the record
- Migration: none — the sentinel default; stored records lacking the field
  rebuild to it and their stored content addresses still verify
  (D-IMPL-89)

  in the same the-fabric-does-not-infer-its-substrate ValueError style.
  The neo4j branch imports the backend lazily, so boot stays importable —
  and every other branch stays bootable — without the extra

  and the manifest Literal in-phase, each in its owning plan

#### ccf.boot.boot

- Surface: ccf.boot.boot
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `boot(*, database_path=…, postgres_dsn=…, postgres_schema=…,
  memory=…, neo4j_uri=…, neo4j_partition=…, mongo_uri=…, mongo_partition=…,
  registry, …)` — five selectable backends
- After: two keyword-only parameters added, `fluree_url: str | None = None` and
  `fluree_partition: str | None = None`, and the exactly-one exclusivity check
  now counts six selectors and names `fluree_url (Fluree v4)` in its refusal.
  Unlike the neo4j and mongo branches, the Fluree branch imports its backend at
  **module top**: its wire client is a core pin, so the import shape is the
  Postgres one
- Why: selection is by explicit argument and never by inference from the shape
  of a string — a fabric that guessed its substrate could boot against a
  development file in production and still pass every test. Adding a substrate
  is therefore adding one more member of an existing enumeration
- Migration: none — both parameters default to `None` and every existing call
  is unaffected. A Fluree deployment passes `boot(fluree_url=…)`

*Recorded by `15.3-02-fluree-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.manifest.load_and_boot

- Surface: ccf.deployment.manifest.load_and_boot
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `load_and_boot(manifest_path, *, registry, database_path=…,
  postgres_dsn=…, neo4j_uri=…, mongo_uri=…, tenant_id=…)`
- After: one keyword-only parameter added, `fluree_url: str | None = None`,
  threaded to both `_preflight_backend` and `boot`. Preflight refuses a
  declared `fluree` backend with no URL **before any artifact is parsed or
  registered**, and refuses a URL supplied alongside a different declared
  backend
- Why: the D-08 kind-only posture — the manifest names the substrate, the
  environment supplies how to reach it, and the two are reconciled loudly
  before any Control Plane state exists. A Fluree URL can carry userinfo and the
  deployment may also hold a bearer token, so neither may reach a
  content-hashed audit artifact (threat T-15.2-05)
- Migration: none — the parameter defaults to `None`. A manifest declaring
  `backend: fluree` must supply `fluree_url` at the call site

*Recorded by `15.3-02-fluree-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.boot.boot

- Surface: ccf.boot.boot
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: sixteen keyword arguments, eleven of them per-database
  (`database_path`, `postgres_dsn`, `postgres_schema`, `memory`, `neo4j_uri`,
  `neo4j_partition`, `mongo_uri`, `mongo_partition`, `fluree_url`,
  `fluree_partition`, `fluree_bearer_token`), with the substrate selected by
  asking which of them was not `None`. Omitting all of them selected the
  development substrate by default
- After: eight keyword arguments — `backend`, `connection`, `secrets`,
  `registry`, `resolution_spec_key`, `resolution_spec_version`,
  `policy_bundle_key`, `policy_bundle_version`. `backend` is required and has
  no default; `connection` is a mapping validated against the resolved
  adapter's own contract; `secrets` is a mapping of config field to
  `ccf.graph.registry.ResolvedSecret`. No parameter names a database
- Why: the composition root named all six substrates, so adding a seventh cost
  an edit in core and a substrate name in core was unavoidable. After
  D-IMPL-93 an adapter declares itself and `boot` resolves the one a deployment
  named, which is what lets a third-party adapter ship as a separate
  installable distribution with no line changing in this repository. `backend`
  is required rather than defaulted because a fabric that could infer its own
  substrate could boot against a development store in production and still pass
  every test
- Migration: replace the per-database keyword with `backend=` plus
  `connection=`, using this table. `postgres_schema` became `schema_name`
  because a Pydantic field named `schema` shadows an attribute on `BaseModel`;
  the store's own keyword is unchanged.
  | Retired `boot()` keyword | New location |
  |---|---|
  | `database_path` | `backend="sqlite"`, `connection={"database_path": ...}` — name unchanged |
  | `postgres_dsn` | `backend="postgres"`, `connection={"dsn": ...}` |
  | `postgres_schema` | `backend="postgres"`, `connection={"schema_name": ...}` |
  | `memory=True` | `backend="memory"`, no connection mapping at all |
  | `neo4j_uri` | `backend="neo4j"`, `connection={"uri": ...}` |
  | `neo4j_partition` | `backend="neo4j"`, `connection={"partition": ...}` |
  | `mongo_uri` | `backend="mongo"`, `connection={"uri": ...}` |
  | `mongo_partition` | `backend="mongo"`, `connection={"partition": ...}` |
  | `fluree_url` | `backend="fluree"`, `connection={"url": ...}` |
  | `fluree_partition` | `backend="fluree"`, `connection={"partition": ...}` |
  | `fluree_bearer_token` | `secrets={"bearer_token": ResolvedSecret(...)}` |
  Two worked cases follow, and then the three refusals to expect while
  migrating.

*Recorded by `15.4-05-boot.md`.*

#### ccf.boot.FabricHandle

- Surface: ccf.boot.FabricHandle
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: constructed with `reader`, `worker`, `registry` and `shutdown`, and
  exposing the first three. It carried capabilities and no state a caller could
  misuse
- After: additionally constructed with `adapter` and exposing
  `handle.adapter`, a `ccf.graph.registry.AdapterPin` carrying the adapter's
  name, its version, and the sorted names of the credential references in force
- Why: from D-IMPL-93 onward the adapter behind the graph seam can be
  third-party code, so which adapter answered and at which version is part of
  what produced the answer — whitepaper §7.10.1 dimension 2. The pin has no
  field that can hold a credential: `secret_refs` carries reference *names*,
  which is what makes the credential path auditable without recording a
  credential. `FabricHandle` still has no writer attribute, private or
  otherwise
- Migration: none for a reader. A caller that constructs a `FabricHandle`
  directly — which nothing in the SDK does outside `boot` — must now pass
  `adapter`

*Recorded by `15.4-05-boot.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.manifest.load_and_boot

- Surface: ccf.deployment.manifest.load_and_boot
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: eight keyword arguments, six of them per-database — `database_path`,
  `postgres_dsn`, `neo4j_uri`, `mongo_uri`, `fluree_url`,
  `fluree_bearer_token` — checked against a hand-maintained mapping from
  substrate name to required argument that lived in core and was edited once
  per substrate forever
- After: four — `manifest_path`, `registry`, `connection`, `tenant_id`.
  `connection` is one mapping keyed by the field names on the declared
  adapter's own connection contract, validated against it. The credential no
  longer travels as an argument at all: the manifest names it, under
  `backend_secrets`, and boot resolves the named environment variable
- Why: the manifest layer held the last two substrate enumerations in core —
  the six-name `Literal` and the required-argument table — so adding a seventh
  adapter cost an edit here. Each adapter now declares its own connection
  contract (D-07) and the registry answers which adapters a deployment has, so
  this function enumerates nothing. Two accepted costs, recorded rather than
  worked around: the exported JSON Schema loses its `backend` enum, so an
  adopter's editor no longer completes the name — plan 15.4-09's `ccf backends`
  command replaces that by answering the better question, "what is installed
  here" rather than "what did someone hard-code"; and an unknown name is now
  caught at load rather than at parse, paid back by a refusal that can list what
  *is* registered. Keeping the retired keywords as deprecated pass-throughs was
  considered and rejected: it would have left adapter names in the public
  surface indefinitely, which is what D-IMPL-93 property (2) exists to remove
- Migration: replace the per-database keyword with a `connection` mapping using
  this table, and move any credential into the manifest. A key that changed name
  and was not migrated does not read as a rename — it reads as
  `extra_forbidden` on a key you are sure exists, which is why the table is
  here rather than one fragment away.
  | Retired `load_and_boot` keyword | New location |
  |---|---|
  | `database_path` | `connection={"database_path": ...}` — name unchanged |
  | `postgres_dsn` | `connection={"dsn": ...}` |
  | `neo4j_uri` | `connection={"uri": ...}` |
  | `mongo_uri` | `connection={"uri": ...}` |
  | `fluree_url` | `connection={"url": ...}` |
  | `fluree_bearer_token` | a `backend_secrets` entry in the manifest, resolved from the environment |
  The mapping additionally reaches every other field an adapter's contract
  declares, which the old keyword list could not: `connection={"schema_name":
  ...}` where a keyword `postgres_schema` never existed here, and
  `connection={"partition": ...}` for the adapters that take one. `schema_name`
  rather than `schema` because a Pydantic field named `schema` shadows an
  attribute on `BaseModel`; the store's own keyword is unchanged.
  Before and after, for a caller with no credential:
  `load_and_boot(MANIFEST, registry=registry, database_path=str(path))` becomes
  `load_and_boot(MANIFEST, registry=registry, connection={"database_path": str(path)})`.
  For a caller with one, the argument disappears entirely: delete
  `fluree_bearer_token=os.environ["CCF_FLUREE_TOKEN"]` from the call and add
  `backend_secrets: [{field: bearer_token, env_var: CCF_FLUREE_TOKEN}]` to the
  manifest. The value still never enters the manifest and still is never
  persisted; what the manifest gains is the auditable record of *which*
  reference was in force.
  Three refusals to expect while migrating, none of which reproduces a value
  that was supplied: an adapter name nothing registers raises
  `ccf.graph.registry.UnknownBackend` listing what `declared_names()` returns; a
  connection key the adapter does not declare raises
  `ccf.graph.registry.InvalidBackendConfig` naming the adapter and the key; a
  `backend_secrets` entry whose variable is unset or empty raises `ValueError`
  naming the adapter, the field and the variable name.

*Recorded by `15.4-06-manifest.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.DeploymentManifest

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.DeploymentManifest
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `backend` was a `Literal` over six fixed names, and a deployment's
  credentials appeared nowhere in the document
- After: `backend` is a plain `str` constrained by `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$` — a shape
  rule, not a vocabulary — with membership checked at load against
  `ccf.graph.registry.declared_names()`. A new optional `backend_secrets` holds
  `ccf.controlplane.artifacts.SecretReference` entries, with a validator
  refusing a repeated `field`
- Why: six database names in a core contract is exactly what CLAUDE.md §11
  forbids, and generating the list rather than typing it would not have changed
  that. Which adapters exist is a property of the deployment that parses the
  document, not of the document — so the document carries the name and the
  deployment answers whether it has one. `backend_secrets` closes the other
  half: a credential must never enter a content-hashed artifact, but the
  *reference* to one can, and that is what makes "which credential was in
  force" answerable from replay for the first time
- Migration: **none — no existing manifest needs an edit**, and there is a
  mechanical reason rather than a hopeful one. Every name the retired `Literal`
  admitted is a name the pattern admits and the registry registers, so every
  manifest in the tree parses byte-unchanged. Manifests are content-addressed by
  their file bytes, so no existing replay pin moves either. What changes is what
  a manifest *may* now say: a name a third-party adapter registers, and a
  `backend_secrets` block. The committed schema at
  `docs/schemas/ccf.deployment-manifest.json` is regenerated in the same commit,
  so an adopter authoring against it sees the pattern in place of the enum and
  sees `backend_secrets` on the same day the code accepts it.

*Recorded by `15.4-06-manifest.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.record.build_replay_record

- Surface: ccf.replay.record.build_replay_record
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: ten keyword parameters; dimension 2 carried no adapter pin and the
  builder had no channel to receive one
- After: one further optional keyword, `adapter: AdapterPin | None = None`.
  Supplied, its three values land on dimension 2 with `secret_refs` sorted;
  omitted or `None`, the three positive sentinels apply and every field the
  builder wrote before this extension is byte-for-byte what it was
- Why: a parameter rather than a fact read off `execution`, unlike the 15.2-08
  and 15.2-11 extensions, because the adapter is a property of the **fabric**
  and the execution object has never seen the fabric — it carries what the
  engine ran, not what the deployment booted against. D-06 additionally keeps
  dimension-2 assembly in adopter code
- Migration: pass `adapter=booted.handle.adapter` to start pinning. Nothing is
  required of an existing caller: omitting the keyword is supported and
  records the positive `no-adapter` claim rather than falling silent

*Recorded by `15.4-08-replay.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.mcp.egress.build_egress_server

- Surface: ccf_runtime.mcp.egress.build_egress_server
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `(*, server_name, resolver)` — the `FastMCP` constructor received
  neither `token_verifier` nor `auth`, so this door was unauthenticated, and it
  bound no authorizer of any kind
- After: four further required keyword-only parameters — `token_verifier`,
  `auth_settings`, `entitlement_source`, `perspective_resolver`. The verifier
  pair reaches the `FastMCP` constructor; the governance pair is consulted
  inside the product resource before the resolver is reached
- Why: an entitlement check downstream of no authentication asks "may this
  principal receive this class?" about a principal nobody verified, so
  authentication had to come first — and `FastMCP` installs its authentication
  middleware only at construction and only when both halves are present (D-26),
  so it cannot be retrofitted onto the returned object. The governance pair is
  required rather than optional-and-inert because this door is receiving its
  first authentication *and* its first authorization at once: `hitl.py`'s
  optional posture exists because it retrofits authorization onto a door that
  had already shipped, whereas an optional seam here would ship exactly the gap
  `docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_REVISION.md` §11.2 measured and would make
  deny-by-default a deployment's choice rather than the SDK's
- Migration: every existing caller breaks, and deliberately so — there is no
  configuration of this builder that answers everyone. Supply (1) a
  `TokenVerifier`: `SourceBackedTokenVerifier(source=...)` over a
  `TokenSourceProtocol`; `FileTokenSource` is the development reference and a
  production deployment substitutes its own identity infrastructure. (2) an
  `AuthSettings` carrying the deployment's issuer, resource-server identity and
  required scopes. (3) an `entitlement_source`:
  `ControlPlaneProductEntitlementSource(control_plane=..., clock=...)` over a
  registry holding a `ccf.product-entitlement-policy` registered under
  `product_entitlement_key(tenant_id=...)`. (4) a `perspective_resolver`:
  `(*, grant, tenant, requested_role) -> Perspective` deriving the role from the
  verified grant — this surface's URI has three segments and no role among
  them, so `requested_role` is always `None` here. Worked example:
  `examples/04-cross-process/producer_child.py` with
  `artifacts/egress-door.yaml`

*Recorded by `15.5-06-egress-entitlement.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.mcp.experience.build_experience_server

- Surface: ccf_runtime.mcp.experience.build_experience_server
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: authenticated (`token_verifier` and `auth_settings` were already
  required and already reached the `FastMCP` constructor), but its
  `context_product` resource was entitlement-free and perspective-free exactly
  like the other two doors
- After: two further required keyword-only parameters — `entitlement_source`
  and `perspective_resolver`, imported from
  `ccf_runtime.authz.product_entitlement` and never re-declared here
- Why: this is the surface external agent runtimes reach; egress is the
  fabric-internal one. A fabric whose external door is less governed than its
  internal one has its threat model backwards, and this phase's founding
  complaint is *"an MCP server in front of a database"* — shipping the SDK's own
  front door ungated would be that complaint, self-inflicted. The seams are
  imported rather than re-declared because three doors with three readings of
  "entitled" is the drift `run_gates`/`run_federated_gates` share their
  comparison functions specifically to prevent
- Migration: authentication is unchanged; existing callers add the two
  governance seams on the same terms as the egress door above. The shared home
  is `ccf_runtime.authz.product_entitlement`, not a sibling MCP module: the two
  MCP roles may not import each other (`test_mcp_role_separation.py`), so the
  seams live in a package neither owns — the resolution
  `ccf_runtime.authz.protocol` already models for the two HITL doors. Worked
  example: `examples/external/00-hello-context/serve.py` with the `entitlement`
  block in `artifacts/experience.yaml`

*Recorded by `15.5-06-egress-entitlement.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.http_api.routes.product.build_router

- Surface: ccf_runtime.http_api.routes.product.build_router
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `build_router(deps)` — one GET route with no principal anywhere on it
- After: `build_router(deps, *, entitlement_source=None,
  perspective_resolver=None)`, and the route gains an optional `role` query
  parameter and 403 responses carrying the shared typed constants. Supplying
  exactly one of the two seams raises `ValueError`
- Why: the seams are optional *here* and required on the two MCP doors, and the
  asymmetry is deliberate: authentication on this surface is `create_app`'s
  `auth=` hook, which is itself optional by ruling (D-IMPL-73, the
  acknowledged insecure default), so a required seam would make a still-supported
  posture unbuildable. `create_app` warns instead. The role is a query
  parameter rather than a fourth path segment because the three path segments
  are the product's *address* and a role is the caller's request about capacity
- Migration: existing callers are unaffected — both parameters default to
  `None` and the unbound route is byte-identical in behaviour to the recorded
  T-07-24 posture. A deployment that wants delivery gated supplies **both**
  seams and binds `auth=`; supplying one alone is refused at build time, and
  supplying both without `auth=` emits `CcfDeadEntitlementWarning`

*Recorded by `15.5-06-egress-entitlement.md`.*

- After: two further optional keyword-only parameters, `product_entitlement`

#### ccf.mcp.client.McpResourceClient

- Surface: ccf.mcp.client.McpResourceClient
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `(*, url, bridge=None)` — no way to present a credential, so an
  authenticated MCP server could only be read by dropping to
  `run_with_session` and re-implementing the three operations this class
  already offers
- After: an optional `http_client_factory` parameter, threaded to
  `run_with_session` unchanged by all three async operations
- Why: the egress door is now authenticated, and every SDK MCP server is. The
  seam already existed one layer down (`run_with_session` has taken an
  `HttpClientFactory` since D-26); this exposes it on the class consumers
  actually use. A *factory* rather than a client instance for the reason the
  type's own docstring gives: an `httpx.AsyncClient` binds to the event loop it
  is used on, and the loop here is the bridge's background one
- Migration: none — the parameter is optional and defaults to `None`, so an
  unauthenticated endpoint needs nothing new. A caller reading an authenticated
  server passes `http_client_factory=lambda: httpx.AsyncClient(headers={...})`;
  `examples/04-cross-process/run.py` is the worked example

*Recorded by `15.5-06-egress-entitlement.md`.*

#### ccf.products.contract.RegisteredProductClass

- Surface: ccf.products.contract.RegisteredProductClass
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: eight fields — `schema_name`, `schema_version`, `ttl_anchor`,
  `assembly_mode`, `max_ttl_key`, `max_ttl_version`,
  `accepted_policy_binding_modes`, `accepted_lineage_modes`. Of the four §6.3
  elements the module docstring placed here, only `assembly_mode` existed
- After: six further fields, every one defaulted — `freshness_sla`,
  `discovery`, `addressability`, `constituent_validity_floors`,
  `payload_schema_key` and `payload_schema_version`. Between this class and
  `ContextProductContract` all nine §6.3 elements are now borne by named
  fields, asserted by `tests/products/test_contract_elements.py` with nothing
  pending
- Why: D-IMPL-106 (freshness), D-IMPL-98 (discovery and addressability, ruled
  *not* declaration-only), D-IMPL-31 (the floors) and D-IMPL-97 ruling 1 (the
  declared payload schema). Class-level throughout, because a product does not
  carry a copy of its class's governance — it is governed by it — and because
  that is the placement under which no recorded content address moves
- Migration: **nothing is required of an existing adopter.** Every new field
  defaults to a named positive sentinel, so a `register_product_class` call
  written before this plan constructs unchanged and the resulting class states
  what it always stated. To adopt: pass `freshness_sla=FreshnessSLA(...)` for
  the per-signal currency expectation; `discovery=ProductDiscovery(...)` to
  publish the class to a registry; `addressability=ProductAddress(...)` for the
  address it answers at; `constituent_validity_floors=...` to declare the
  D-IMPL-31 floors ahead of 15.5-12's refusal; and
  `payload_schema_key=` / `payload_schema_version=` to pin a declared payload
  schema, which must already be registered in the Control Plane

*Recorded by `15.5-07-contract-elements.md`.*

#### ccf.products.contract.register_product_class

- Surface: ccf.products.contract.register_product_class
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: eight keyword parameters; resolved and type-checked the class's
  MAX_TTL and nothing else
- After: six further keyword parameters carrying the fields above, all
  defaulted. When a payload-schema pin is declared, the schema is **resolved at
  registration** and refused — `ProductClassRegistrationError`, raised and
  naming both sides — if the pin is half-declared, names nothing registered, or
  names an artifact that is not a payload schema
- Why: D-IMPL-102 limb 1, *schema validity at registration*: a deployment that
  pinned a schema that is not there finds out while wiring the fabric rather
  than when a request is about to be bound to it, which is the MAX_TTL
  reasoning (D-IMPL-39) applied to the second piece of pinned governance. The
  well-formedness half is structural rather than a check that could be
  forgotten — a malformed schema cannot be constructed at all — so what remains
  for registration is that the pinned artifact *is* one
- Migration: none for an existing caller; every added parameter is
  keyword-only with a default. A caller adopting the payload schema registers
  the schema first and passes the `(key, version)` the registration minted.
  **No fourth gate follows**: `ccf.products.gates.run_gates` is byte-untouched
  and the three shipped validation gates stay at three (D-IMPL-102 limb 3)

*Recorded by `15.5-07-contract-elements.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ProductClassDeclaration

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ProductClassDeclaration
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `semver`, `schema_name`, `schema_version`, `ttl_anchor`,
  `assembly_mode`, `max_ttl_key`, `required_operator_keys`,
  `accepted_policy_binding_modes`, `accepted_lineage_modes`
- After: five further defaulted fields mirroring the registered class —
  `freshness_sla`, `discovery`, `addressability`,
  `constituent_validity_floors` and `payload_schema_key`.
  `docs/schemas/ccf.product-class.json` regenerates accordingly, in the same
  commit, under the bytes-equality drift gate
- Why: the authored twin of `RegisteredProductClass`. **`payload_schema_version`
  is deliberately absent**, on exactly the rule that keeps `max_ttl_version`
  absent: that pin is a content hash minted by *registering* the schema, and an
  artifact declaring it would be authoring a hash of something it does not
  contain. Authored key, derived version — the second instance of the rule
  rather than a new one
- Migration: **no committed `ccf.product-class` document needs editing.** All
  five fields default, on the `accepted_policy_binding_modes` precedent, so an
  artifact authored before this plan parses unchanged and means what it meant.
  To adopt, add any of `freshness_sla:`, `discovery:`, `addressability:`,
  `constituent_validity_floors:` or `payload_schema_key:` to the document; an
  editor pointed at the regenerated schema will complete them

#### ccf.skills.catalog.SkillArgumentProperty

- Surface: ccf.skills.catalog.SkillArgumentProperty
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: two required fields, `type` and `description`, and no way to describe
  anything below the top level
- After: three further fields, all defaulted to "not declared" — `properties`
  (an object-typed property's own members), `items` (an array-typed property's
  element spec) and `required` (which of its own members must be present).
  Keyword agreement is validated: `properties` requires `type == "object"`,
  `items` requires `type == "array"`, an array's element spec may not itself be
  an array, and a nested `required` name must be declared
- Why: D-IMPL-103, Dave 2026-08-11, branch (i). Flattening v0 payloads to one
  level was measured as not surviving without loss — three declared fields in
  `examples/s1-01-hello-context-product` lose a typed fact, and flattening a
  beneficial-owner list into parallel arrays preserves the values while
  degrading the pairing to an index convention the schema cannot state
- Migration: **none — this is a widening, not a replacement.** Every existing
  depth-1 property is still valid with the two fields it always had, and an
  unset nesting field is not a declaration: a leaf renders exactly the two keys
  it rendered before

*Recorded by `15.5-07-contract-elements.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ProductClassDeclaration

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ProductClassDeclaration

- Change type: reshaped
- Before: as plan 15.5-07 left it — `semver`, `schema_name`, `schema_version`,

  *registering* the contract, and an artifact declaring it would be authoring a

#### ccf.agents.definitions.ContextContract

- Surface: ccf.agents.definitions.ContextContract
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `consumes: tuple[ConsumedProduct, ...]` alone, with a docstring
  reserving the emission side: *"Emission bindings (which projection an
  assembler emits to) extend this contract when the ContextAssembly
  archetype's transport lands; the shape is additive by design"*
- After: `consumes` unchanged, plus `emits: tuple[EmittedProduct, ...] =
  EMITS_NOTHING`. `docs/schemas/ccf.agent-definition.json` regenerates
  accordingly, in the same commit, under the bytes-equality drift gate
- Why: that reserved sentence, followed literally — the binding extends axis 2
  rather than becoming a parallel artifact beside it, because both directions
  are one axis: what an agent's fabric relationship *is*. `emits` defaults to
  the named positive `EMITS_NOTHING` claim so every shipped definition and
  every fixture parses byte-unchanged. What is deliberately **not** checked
  here is the coherence: the schema knows the shape of a document, and only
  the factory holds the archetype bundles and the deployment's registered
  classes — a check that cannot see both sides cannot name both sides
- Migration: **an emission binding is two lines in an agent definition, and
  adopting it is optional.** In the `context_contract:` block that today reads
  only `consumes:`, add:

#### ccf.agents.factory.AgentFactory

- Surface: ccf.agents.factory.AgentFactory
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: constructor took `control_plane`, `callable_registry`, `mcp_skills`
  and `peering_skills`; `construct` ran four steps
- After: one further defaulted keyword, `product_classes: Mapping[str,
  RegisteredProductClass] | None = None`, and a fifth construction step that
  refuses an incoherent or unregistered axis-2 emission binding
- Why: both refusals are the class of check `register_product_class` already
  makes for `MAX_TTL` (D-IMPL-39) — a deployment finds out **while wiring the
  fabric**, rather than when a product is about to be assembled. They are
  raised, never returned or warned, and they name both sides without repairing
  either. `product_classes` is constructor-injected on the `callable_registry`
  precedent — the factory holds no registry of its own — and keyed by
  `schema_name`, the shape `AdmissionGate` already takes for the same
  population. `None` is the positive statement *this deployment registered no
  product classes*, and it is fail-closed: an emitting definition is then
  refused, naming the empty set
- Migration: **existing call sites are unaffected.** The new keyword defaults,
  and a definition that declares no emission binding reaches neither refusal —
  every shipped `AgentFactory(control_plane=…)` construction is untouched, and
  all 162 tests under `tests/agents` pass with no fixture edited. Wire
  `product_classes` only when you declare an emission binding

*Recorded by `15.5-10-assembler.md`.*

#### ccf.halflife.debt.DebtAssessment

- Surface: ccf.halflife.debt.DebtAssessment
- Public surface: true

- Before: three fields — `debt`, `signal`, `factors` — defined in this module
- After: a re-export of `ccf.halflife.assessment.DebtAssessment`, carrying a
  fourth field `scorers`, defaulted to `NO_SCORER_PINS`. A `float` `debt` is
  refused at construction, and a second pin for one factor is refused
- Why: D-IMPL-47's pin (see `ScorerPin`) and D-IMPL-100's placement (see the
  module split under `ccf.halflife.assessment.DebtAssessment`)
- Migration: **none for construction** — the new field is defaulted, so all six
  construction sites in this tree build unchanged. **The canonical bytes of an
  assessment change**, because `scorers` is serialized whether or not it is
  populated. Anything that content-addressed a `DebtAssessment` directly, or a
  contract embedding one, re-records; inside this repository that is exactly the
  `ccf.hitl` escalation records below

*Recorded by `15.5-11-debt-delivery.md`.*

#### ccf.halflife.debt.assess_debt

- Surface: ccf.halflife.debt.assess_debt
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `assess_debt(factors, *, profile) -> DebtAssessment`
- After: `assess_debt(factors, *, profile, scorers=NO_SCORER_PINS) ->
  DebtAssessment`. The pins are **carried, never consulted**: passing them
  changes neither `debt` nor `signal`, asserted by test
- Why: D-IMPL-47 requires the scorer in force to travel *with* the value, and
  constructing the value and its pin in one place is what stops the two from
  being assembled separately and disagreeing. **D-IMPL-105 is what the new
  parameter is not**: no entitlement, principal or grant argument joins this
  signature, and a structural test asserts the parameter set so that a later
  addition is a decision somebody makes rather than one that happens. Under a
  per-principal figure the completeness number would move when a source was
  withheld, giving a caller an oracle over the entitlement configuration —
  quieter than a refusal, because nothing in the response says a consultation
  was declined
- Migration: **none.** The parameter is keyword-only with a default, so all nine
  call sites across three files are unchanged

*Recorded by `15.5-11-debt-delivery.md`.*

#### ccf.products.contract.ContextProductContract

- Surface: ccf.products.contract.ContextProductContract
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: nine fields, none of them saying anything about how much to trust the
  product's contents
- After: one further field, `debt: DebtAssessment | None`, defaulted to `None`
- Why: D-IMPL-100. Debt is carried **inside** the delivered bytes rather than
  beside them, because every shipped delivery path serializes the vessel and
  none reads `ServedProduct.replay_ref` — see the door table above. `None` is
  the positive assertion *this product was assembled with no debt assessment*,
  in the `NO_HITL_ACTIONS` voice: it is what every product shipped before this
  field existed was in fact asserting, and it is what keeps all 42 existing
  construction sites saying exactly what they always said
- Migration: **nothing is required to keep constructing products**, and
  **something is required if you recorded addresses outside the replay record**
  — see "The content address moves" above. To adopt, pass
  `debt=assess_debt(factors, profile=profile, scorers=...)` at assembly; the
  value then reaches every door and lands on the record with no further wiring.
  A custom `ReplayEnvironment` must read it back — see
  `ccf.replay.engine.ReplayEnvironment` below

*Recorded by `15.5-11-debt-delivery.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.record.ContentGenerationPath

- Surface: ccf.replay.record.ContentGenerationPath
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: six fields, ending at the 15.2-08 query pins
- After: one further field, `debt: DebtAssessment | None`, defaulted to `None`
- Why: dimension 5 and not dimension 1, for two reasons pointing the same way.
  D_c's routing band *is* a generation-path fact — a Forced-LLM signal invokes a
  semantic stage and a Fail-Closed signal declines the decision outright — and
  debt is a property of the delivered product, so it belongs beside
  `product_content_address`: the field a rehydrator reads in order to reproduce
  the address sits next to the address it must reproduce. Dimension 1 stays
  exactly the four D-IMPL-06 constituents plus the attestation pin
- Migration: none for construction. `None` is the positive assertion *this
  decision carried no debt assessment*, on the `source_attestation` precedent,
  so a record written before this seam parses and verifies unchanged

*Recorded by `15.5-11-debt-delivery.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.record.build_replay_record

- Surface: ccf.replay.record.build_replay_record
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the record's dimension 5 said nothing about debt
- After: dimension 5's `debt` is **derived from the `product` argument the
  builder was already handed** — no new parameter
- Why: derivation rather than threading is what keeps all 30 consumers,
  including two conformance modules, byte-for-byte unchanged; and it makes a
  record that pins a debt its product does not carry *unrepresentable* rather
  than merely discouraged. It is the same rule that put the 15.2-08 seam facts
  on `ExecutionResult` rather than in the signature: a fact already present is
  read, never re-passed
- Migration: **none.** No call site changes. A caller that wants debt on the
  record puts it on the product

#### ccf.replay.record.ContentGenerationPath

- Surface: ccf.replay.record.ContentGenerationPath
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: seven fields, ending at the 15.5-11 debt landing
- After: one further field, `request: ProductRequest | None`, defaulted to
  `None`
- Why: D-IMPL-111 limb (b). **Dimension 5 and not dimension 4.** Dimension 4
  answers *"for whom, with what authority?"* (`docs/CCF_Whitepaper_1-2.md:3266`)
  — principal, role, agent identity, grants, delegation — and a request asserts
  none of those. What a request *does* determine is the generation path: its
  `parameters` reach plan selection through D-IMPL-49's `selection_params` slot,
  its `subject` seeds the traversal, and its `asked_at` is the instant the
  caller asked as of. So it sits beside `graph_query_sequence`, which it
  explains. `None` is the positive assertion *this decision recorded no
  request*, in the `debt` and `source_attestation` voice: a record written
  before this seam and one whose caller dropped the question would otherwise be
  the same artifact
- Migration: none for construction; see "The record's content address moves"
  above for the one thing that is required

*Recorded by `15.5-13-request-in-replay.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.record.build_replay_record

- Surface: ccf.replay.record.build_replay_record
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `build_replay_record(*, execution, product, perspective,
  policy_bindings, assembly_mode, recorded_at, agent_conditioning=None,
  harness_trace=None, hitl_actions=None, workflow_step_sequence=None,
  adapter=None)`
- After: one further keyword-only parameter, `request: ProductRequest | None =
  None`, recorded verbatim on dimension 5
- Why: a **parameter** rather than a derivation, which is the opposite of the
  choice D-IMPL-100 made for `debt` — and for a reason that points the same way.
  Debt is a property of the product, and the product was already a parameter, so
  reading it off made a record pinning a debt its product does not carry
  unrepresentable. The *question* is a property of the **call**: neither the
  execution nor the product has ever seen it, the engine is handed a plan, a
  perspective, a boundary and a seed set, and L5 importing L6's request surface
  to carry one would invert the layering (CLAUDE.md §10). A question
  reconstructed from its answer is not the question that was asked
- Migration: **none.** All 32 call sites across 28 files are byte-for-byte
  unchanged under the positive sentinel — the pattern this phase has now used
  six times, priced at 0 forced repairs in
  `docs/CONTEXT_PRODUCT_RULING_EVIDENCE.md` § Q1

*Recorded by `15.5-13-request-in-replay.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.mcp.egress.ProductResolverProtocol

- Surface: ccf_runtime.mcp.egress.ProductResolverProtocol
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `__call__(*, tenant: str, product_class: str, version: str) -> ServableProduct`
- After:
  `__call__(*, tenant, product_class, version, perspective: Perspective, request: ProductRequest | None) -> ServableProduct`
- Why: D-IMPL-108, closing DI-15.5-03. The door resolves and authorizes a
  `Perspective` and must be able to hand it to the path that shapes the
  product, or the authorization is a refusal check and nothing more.
  **Neither new argument is defaulted, and `perspective` is not nullable** —
  that is the substance rather than a style choice. A resolver silently
  constructing its own perspective is indistinguishable from one honouring the
  door's, and a parameter defaulting to `None` would recreate exactly that
  ambiguity one layer down. `request` is required-with-no-default but typed
  `ProductRequest | None`, because a surface can honestly have **no way to
  ask**: the `ccf-product://` template is three path segments with no query
  among them, and manufacturing a subject, an intent, a freshness figure and
  an instant would be the SDK holding deployment values (CINV-01) and reading
  a clock — the literal-instead-of-argument defect D-IMPL-98 exists to remove,
  reintroduced one layer up. That is the same voice `requested_role: str | None`
  already carries on `PerspectiveResolverProtocol`.
  **Reclassified from `behavior` to `reshaped` by plan `15.6-10`**, under the
  authorization `D-IMPL-110` recorded by name and `D-IMPL-122` implemented.
  Editing a landed fragment is otherwise forbidden; this edit exists only
  because that ruling anticipated it. **The original `behavior` classification
  was correct when it was made** — `reshaped` fails
  `tests/test_surface_completeness.py` when the manifest shows no diff line to
  point at, and at the time it could show none: `inspect.signature` on a
  `Protocol` resolves `__init__`, so this seam's widening from three arguments
  to five was byte-invisible in `docs/surface/v0.2-surface.json`. `15.6-10`
  taught the tool to read a Protocol's `__call__`, the manifest now shows this
  name under `reshaped`, and the honest classification and the representable one
  have converged
- Migration: **required, and mechanical.** Every resolver a deployment supplies
  gains two keyword-only parameters. Eleven definitions across `runtime/`,
  `examples/` and `tests/` were updated in this plan's commits; an adopter's
  own is the same edit. The minimum conforming change is to accept both and
  ignore them, which type-checks and runs — but a resolver that ignores
  `perspective` is asserting *the conditioning this door authorized does not
  affect what I serve*, which is a claim worth making deliberately. The
  reference deployments do not: `examples/04-cross-process/producer_child.py`
  and `examples/external/00-hello-context/serve.py` both refuse to serve when
  the authorized perspective is not the one their product was assembled under

*Recorded by `15.5-15-resolver-seam.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.mcp.experience.ExperienceProductResolverProtocol

- Surface: ccf_runtime.mcp.experience.ExperienceProductResolverProtocol
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the same three-string signature as its egress twin
- After: the same five-argument signature as its egress twin
- Why: the two moved together deliberately. The seams are structurally
  identical so that one deployment resolver can serve both roles, and a
  deployment supplying one resolver to both doors must not discover that only
  one of them forwards what it authorized. The two modules remain separate and
  import neither each other nor a sibling —
  `tests/invariants/test_mcp_role_separation.py` still asserts it, unrelaxed.
  **Reclassified from `behavior` to `reshaped` by plan `15.6-10`** on the same
  authorization and for the same reason as its egress twin above: the original
  classification was correct when made, because the manifest could show no diff
  line for a `Protocol` until `15.6-10` taught it to read `__call__`
- Migration: as above

*Recorded by `15.5-15-resolver-seam.md`.*

#### ccf.products.contract.RegisteredProductClass

- Surface: ccf.products.contract.RegisteredProductClass
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: fourteen fields, ending at the payload-schema pin
- After: two further fields — `request_contract_key` and

- Why: D-IMPL-98's request-contract pin, mirrored onto the registered value the
  way every other piece of pinned governance already is. Plan `15.5-08` shipped
  the refusal that guards it but could not wire it, because this file was owned
  by concurrent plan `15.5-11` that wave
- Migration: **none.** Both fields are defaulted to the positive sentinel, so
  every existing registration keeps saying exactly what it always said.
  **No content address moves**, and that is asserted rather than assumed:
  `RegisteredProductClass` is reachable from no addressed contract — the check
  walks `ContextProductContract`'s field graph — and `ContextProductContract`'s
  own field set is pinned by test. `make schemas` produces no diff, because
  this type is not an artifact class
