#### ccf.products.contract.register_product_class

- Surface: ccf.products.contract.register_product_class
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the payload-schema pin was resolved at registration; the
  request-contract pin was not resolved anywhere
- After: two further keyword-only parameters, both defaulted, and one call to
  `ccf.products.request.refuse_request_contract_pin` — so a class that pinned a
  contract that is not there, or another class's, or another lane's, finds out
  **while wiring the fabric** rather than in front of a caller whose question
  it can no longer answer
- Why: D-IMPL-102 limb 1's reasoning applied to D-IMPL-98's surface, and the
  MAX_TTL voice throughout: raised, never returned or warned, naming both
  sides. Until this plan the refusal existed, was fully tested, and was reached
  by nobody
- Migration: **none.** Both parameters are defaulted. A deployment adopting the
  pin registers its `ProductRequestContract` and passes the `(key, version)`
  pair, exactly as it does for a payload schema

*Recorded by `15.5-15-resolver-seam.md`.*

#### ccf.halflife.decay.Observation

- Surface: ccf.halflife.decay.Observation
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: two fields — `observation_type` and `observed_at`. An observation could
  say what kind of thing it was and when it was made, and nothing about what it
  was an observation *of*
- After: one further field, `subject: str`, defaulted to
  `ccf.halflife.decay.NO_SUBJECT_IDENTITY`. An opaque identifier the deployment
  chooses; the SDK never enumerates it, never parses it and never compares it.
  It is **carried, never consumed** — no function in `ccf.halflife.decay` reads
  it, and `ccf.halflife.debt.FreshnessScorer.score` returns the same figure for
  the same types and instants whether it is populated or not, asserted by
  `tests/halflife/test_debt.py::test_the_freshness_figure_is_unmoved_by_the_subject_identity`
- Why: `D-IMPL-112` gap 1 — a debt figure an analyst can act on has to be able to
  point at the observations whose decay it summarises, and `D-IMPL-99` ruled that
  unbuildable because the join key existed on no structure in this tree.
  `D-IMPL-117` scopes what the SDK owes for it: the data structure and the API to
  populate and extract, and none of the judgement about which observations
  matter — so the field is an opaque string and the choice of what to put in it
  belongs to the context product creator. `D-IMPL-120` ruled the field
  **optional** (`qc-optional`) rather than required, measured against the three
  shipped example artifacts: required cost 6 code files **plus all 3 artifacts,
  each of which raises**; optional costs 3 code files and **0 artifacts**.
  Whitepaper §7.7 binds λ_c to the observation *type* and says nothing about
  subject identity, so the field is an SDK extension and its docstring is labelled
  as one (CLAUDE.md §11)
- Migration: **none.** The field is defaulted, so every existing construction site
  and every `Observation.model_validate` over a two-key mapping keeps working and
  keeps saying exactly what it always said — asserted by
  `tests/halflife/test_decay.py::test_a_two_key_observation_mapping_still_validates`,
  and demonstrated by the three example artifacts
  (`examples/00-quickstart/artifacts/decision.yaml`,
  `examples/01-agent-basics/artifacts/debt-evaluation.yaml`,
  `examples/08-e2e-demo/artifacts/decision.yaml`), which are **unchanged by this
  plan**. **The canonical bytes of an `Observation` do change**, because `subject`
  is serialized whether or not it is populated: anything that content-addressed an
  `Observation` directly re-records. Nothing in this repository does, and no
  product address moves, because `Observation` is an input to debt computation and
  not a member of `ContextProductContract`. To adopt, pass `subject=` at
  construction — `examples/s1-01-hello-context-product/deployment.py` carries
  `record.claim.entity_uri`, a value its loop variable already held

*Recorded by `15.6-05-observation-identity.md`.*

#### ccf.products.contract.ContextProductContract

- Surface: ccf.products.contract.ContextProductContract
- Public surface: true
- Before: ten fields, ending at 15.5-11's `debt` — which said how much to trust
  the product's contents and nothing about what that judgement rested on
- After: one further field, `debt_evidence: DebtEvidence`, defaulted to
  `NO_DEBT_EVIDENCE`. A **sibling** of `debt`, not a member of it
- Change type: reshaped
- Why: `D-IMPL-118`, which chose `qa-toplevel` over the cheaper nested placement
  and recorded the reason — the field buys *"its own identity on the vessel
  rather than [being] a rider on debt"*. On the vessel rather than beside it for
  15.5-11's door reason, unchanged: all three shipped delivery paths serialize
  `servable.product` and none reads `ServedProduct.replay_ref`, so a placement
  beside the vessel would reach an in-process caller and no wire consumer at all
- Migration: **nothing is required to keep constructing products** — the
  sentinel default keeps every existing site saying what it always said — and
  **something is required if you recorded addresses outside the replay record**;
  see "The content address moves" above. A custom `ReplayEnvironment` **must**
  be updated regardless of whether you populate the field; see
  `ccf.replay.engine.ReplayEnvironment` below

*Recorded by `15.6-06-debt-evidence.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.record.ContentGenerationPath

- Surface: ccf.replay.record.ContentGenerationPath
- Public surface: true
- Before: eight fields, ending at 15.5-13's `request`
- After: one further field, `debt_evidence: DebtEvidence`, defaulted to
  `NO_DEBT_EVIDENCE`
- Change type: reshaped
- Why: this member is **the price of the ruled placement**, not a symmetry.
  Because the carrier is top-level on the vessel rather than nested inside the
  assessment, it does not ride `debt` back through replay; `ProjectionOutput`
  carries exactly `(plan_id, payload)`, so without this field no implementation
  of the `(record, projection)` signature can produce the value and every replay
  of every product raises `ReplayDivergence`. Dimension 5 and not a sixth
  dimension: `INV-CH7-80` holds, asserted by field enumeration in
  `test_the_evidence_lands_on_dimension_five_and_mints_no_sixth`. Beside the
  `debt` it explains, for that field's own reason — the value a rehydrator reads
  in order to reproduce the address sits next to the address it must reproduce
- Migration: none for construction. `NO_DEBT_EVIDENCE` is the positive
  assertion *this decision pointed at nothing*, so a record written before this
  seam parses and verifies unchanged

*Recorded by `15.6-06-debt-evidence.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.DebtProfile

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.DebtProfile
- Public surface: true
- Before: three declared values, none with a default — `risk_sensitivity`,
  `forced_llm_threshold`, `fail_closed_threshold`
- After: one further field, `signal_obligations: SignalObligations`, defaulted
  to `NO_SIGNAL_OBLIGATIONS`; plus one method,
  `obligation_for(signal) -> str`, returning the reference bound to that state
  or the sentinel
- Change type: reshaped
- Why: the calibration half belongs here because the calibration already is
  here. This artifact owns the two bands that decide *which* state a decision
  lands in; it is a `VersionedArtifact` with a published schema; and it is
  already pinned per-agent as `halflife_debt_profile: VersionedRef`, which
  `D-IMPL-97` named as *"axis 5 … the home for a product's debt configuration
  already exists, on the agent, declared and version-pinned."* Binding elsewhere
  would split one calibration across two documents and two version lanes.
  `D-IMPL-118`'s Q-A fork does not reach this half: a Control Plane artifact is
  not the product vessel. `obligation_for` resolves off the **resolved,
  version-pinned instance** the caller already holds and never reaches the
  Control Plane for a `@latest` (`D-IMPL-26`)
- Migration: **none to keep an existing profile working** — the sentinel default
  means every `debt-profile.yaml` in existence parses and means what it always
  meant. To bind obligations, add a `signal_obligations` list of
  `{signal, obligation_ref}` entries; bind only the states you have an answer
  for. `docs/schemas/ccf.debt-profile.json` was regenerated in the same commit

*Recorded by `15.6-08-obligation-binding.md`.*

  here than for either sibling. Because the value is top-level on the vessel it
  does not ride `debt` back through replay; and unlike the other two, a

#### ccf.halflife.assessment.SourceRefreshRecency

- Surface: ccf.halflife.assessment.SourceRefreshRecency
- Public surface: true
- Before: the annotated carrier type plan 15.6-07 shipped — a
  `tuple[SourceRefresh, ...]` validated to be source-unique and canonically
  ordered
- After: `SourceRefreshWindow | None`. The ordering and uniqueness validators
  are gone with the collection: a two-field contract has no declaration order,
  so there is nothing to canonicalise
- Change type: reshaped
- Why: `D-IMPL-124`. **Replace, not parallel** — the ruling's operative
  sentence is *"no `source_ref` strings leave the vessel"* and *"the inference
  channel is closed"*, and a parallel shape would leave the per-source map on
  the vessel, which is the rejected Shape A wearing a second field. It keeps
  its **name** for `ccf.halflife.assessment.DebtEvidence`'s recorded reason:
  the carrier lands in **two** places, the vessel and replay dimension 5, and
  one named shape is what keeps those two from drifting apart
- Migration: nothing is required to keep constructing products — the sentinel
  default covers every existing site. An adopter who **populated** this alias
  under 15.6-07 supplies a `SourceRefreshWindow` instead of a
  `tuple[SourceRefresh, ...]`; the change on your side is the one-line
  reduction `min`/`max` over the instants you were already declaring

#### ccf.halflife.assessment.NO_SOURCE_REFRESH_RECENCY

- Surface: ccf.halflife.assessment.NO_SOURCE_REFRESH_RECENCY
- Public surface: true
- Before: the empty tuple, as a named module-level constant
- After: `None`, under the **same name** and making the **same positive claim**
  — *this product records nothing about when its sources last refreshed*
- Change type: reshaped
- Why: the value moved because `()` is the empty case of a *collection*, and a
  window is not a collection. There is no empty window that does not require
  inventing an instant, and an invented instant is the silent default
  `D-IMPL-38` forbids. The same-vessel precedent is exact:
  `ContextProductContract.debt` and `.request` are both optional *structures*
  whose `None` is documented as a positive assertion in the same voice. What
  the **name** buys, and why it is kept rather than replaced by a bare `None`
  at each site, is unchanged: every construction site and every default still
  spells the claim, so a deployment that knows nothing about its sources'
  publication schedule and one whose recency somebody dropped in transit remain
  different artifacts
- Migration: none for construction — it remains the default on both fields that
  carry the alias, so every existing construction site asserts exactly what it
  always asserted. If your code compared this constant to `()`, compare it to
  `None`, or better, compare against the named constant

*Recorded by `15.6-13-source-refresh-window.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.http_api.dependencies.ApiDependencies

- Surface: ccf_runtime.http_api.dependencies.ApiDependencies
- Public surface: true
- Before: a frozen keyword-only dataclass with **eight required** fields
- After: the same eight fields, with `workflow_factory`, `workflow_engine`,
  `hitl_transport`, `skill_catalog` and `grant_ledger` typed `X | None` and
  defaulting to `None`. `control_plane`, `artifact_store` and `product_resolver`
  stay required — measured, they are read by four routers, two and one, and no
  useful app omits them. The class docstring now names, for each of the eight,
  the routes that handle gates
- Change type: reshaped
- Why: `D-IMPL-126` item (3). The spike measured a first-time adopter serving a
  single Context Product and finding the bundle demanded a workflow factory, a
  workflow engine, a HITL transport, a skill catalog and a grant ledger before
  `GET /product/...` would answer — five fabricated stubs to expose one noun. On
  the five, `None` is the positive assertion *this deployment exposes no such
  route*, and the route refuses with a typed 503 rather than serving
  half-wired. `kw_only=True` means the defaults required no field reordering
- Migration: **nothing is required.** All seventeen shipped construction sites
  pass eight keyword arguments and are unaffected; a deployment that wires all
  eight sees no change on any route. What *did* change is the posture: a
  deployment that forgot a handle used to fail at boot with a `TypeError` and
  now fails at first call with a typed 503. If you were relying on that boot
  failure as your wiring check, assert your bundle explicitly at your
  composition root, or smoke-test one route from each family you intend to
  expose

*Recorded by `16-22-consumption-papercuts.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.mcp.egress.build_egress_server

- Surface: ccf_runtime.mcp.egress.build_egress_server
- Public surface: true
- Before: six required keyword-only arguments — `server_name`, `resolver`,
  `token_verifier`, `auth_settings`, `entitlement_source`,
  `perspective_resolver`
- After: the same six, unchanged and still required, plus two keyword-only
  arguments **both defaulting closed**: `expose_product_tool: bool = False` and
  `product_tool_description: str | None = None`. Supplied together or not at
  all — either without the other raises `ValueError` naming both, in the `D-03`
  requiredness-conditional-on-exposure voice. With the pair supplied the builder
  registers exactly one Tool, named by `PRODUCT_REQUEST_TOOL`, whose advertised
  `inputSchema` requires exactly `tenant`, `product_class`, `version`,
  `subject`, `intent`, `freshness` and `asked_at` and carries no `role`, no
  `schema_name`, no `schema_version` and no `parameters`. The resource, the URI
  template and the resolver Protocol are untouched
- Change type: reshaped
- Why: `D-IMPL-126` item 5, gating HW-2 in `D-IMPL-127`. A resource-only door
  cannot refute the reading that a Context Product is a precalculated static
  object, because a caller with no way to ask a question cannot demonstrate that
  the answer depends on one. Opt-in rather than on-by-default because a
  deployment that reviewed and approved a resource-only door must not acquire a
  parameterised authorization surface it never assessed merely by upgrading —
  deny-by-default, this module's stated posture, applied to exposure. Two
  arguments rather than one because *is this door open?* must be answerable by
  grepping for a boolean. No `DEFAULT_*` description constant ships, unlike the
  Experience server's tools: the prose an adopter's consumers read is not the
  SDK's to write (CINV-01)
- Migration: **none required.** Every existing call site keeps working
  unchanged and registers no tool, which is the whole point of the default. To
  open the door, pass `expose_product_tool=True` **and**
  `product_tool_description="…"` together; see *Adopter migration* above,
  including the measured constraint that a class declaring any required request
  parameter is not reachable through the tool

*Recorded by `16-23-mcp-product-tool.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.mcp.experience.build_experience_server

- Surface: ccf_runtime.mcp.experience.build_experience_server
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: nine optional seam/prose parameters plus the two required governance seams; the gated consumption tools had no declared address, so `get_context` and `get_lineage` registered handlers that reached their providers with no entitlement decision
- After: two additional keyword-only parameters, `entitled_product_class` and `lineage_tenant`, both `str | None = None` and both required conditionally on exposure — and refused when supplied for a tool the allowlist does not admit
- Why: `D-IMPL-128` limb 1 ruled the ungated `get_context` a defect in the SDK's external front door, and an entitlement decides on a *(tenant, class)* pair the deployment must supply — the class because check and serve would otherwise be free to diverge, the tenant because `get_lineage` carries no wire arguments at all and inventing one would be the SDK holding a deployment value (CINV-01). A second entry for this name after `15.5-06-egress-entitlement.md` is deliberate: that fragment recorded the arrival of the governance seams, this one records the declared address the two now-gated tools are decided against
- Migration: supply `entitled_product_class` when either gated tool is allowlisted and `lineage_tenant` when `get_lineage` is; both are ordinary deployment values and the shipped example binds them to `assembled.address.product_class` and `assembled.address.tenant`. A deployment whose entitlement policy does not name the class its context provider serves will now be refused at its own front door — that is the gate working

*Recorded by `16-24-experience-door-entitlement.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.mcp.experience.ContextProviderProtocol

- Surface: ccf_runtime.mcp.experience.ContextProviderProtocol
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `__call__(*, tenant: str, entity: str) -> str` — the provider received no conditioning, because the door that calls it computed none
- After: `__call__(*, tenant: str, entity: str, perspective: Perspective) -> str` — required, no default, carrying the perspective the door's entitlement authorized
- Why: `D-IMPL-108`'s reasoning applied to the newly gated tool: a door that computes an authorized perspective and discards it is `DI-15.5-03` reintroduced one layer down, where nothing on the wire would show it. No default, because a defaulted governance argument is one an implementor can be written without noticing. A second entry for this name after `15.6-10-protocol-call-capture.md` is deliberate: that fragment recorded a different change to the same Protocol
- Migration: add `perspective: Perspective` to your provider's signature — and **use it**. Compare its `role` against the capacity your product was assembled under and refuse a mismatch; a provider that accepts the conditioning and serves regardless has moved the defect one layer down rather than closing it. `LineageProviderProtocol` is unchanged and needs no edit

*Recorded by `16-24-experience-door-entitlement.md`.*

#### ccf.products.contract.ProductDiscovery

- Surface: ccf.products.contract.ProductDiscovery
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: three axes — `domain: str`, `intents: frozenset[str]`,
  `capabilities: frozenset[str]`
- After: a fourth axis — `audiences: frozenset[str] = frozenset()` — on the
  same footing as the other three: opaque, deployment-declared, uninterpreted
- Why: `D-IMPL-135` (Dave, 2026-08-14) — a product's intended audience
  (`analyst`, `investment_banker`) maps onto none of the existing three axes;
  calling a role an *intent* or a *capability* corrupts both meanings, and
  the two come apart the moment one role serves several intents
- Migration: none. Every existing `ProductDiscovery` construction, every
  committed `ccf.product-class` document, and `NOT_DISCOVERABLE` itself keep
  working unedited. A deployment that wants an audience declares
  `audiences=frozenset({...})`, or an `audiences:` list on its
  `ccf.product-class` document's `discovery:` block

*Recorded by `16-27-discovery-audiences.md`.*

#### ccf.products.contract.register_product_class

- Surface: ccf.products.contract.register_product_class
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `discovery: ProductDiscovery = NOT_DISCOVERABLE`, a keyword-only
  parameter defaulting to the three-axis
  `ProductDiscovery(domain='not-discoverable', intents=frozenset(),
  capabilities=frozenset())`
- After: the same keyword-only parameter, at the same type, with the same
  default name `NOT_DISCOVERABLE` — but the sentinel's own printed
  representation now carries `audiences=frozenset()`, so the function's
  captured shape moves even though no parameter was added, removed,
  retyped, or reordered
- Why: `ProductDiscovery` is a `contract`-kind export, and
  `tests/test_surface_shape_capture.py`'s committed-manifest disturbance
  check (`test_no_committed_plain_class_or_function_shape_is_disturbed`)
  only walks `class`- and `function`-kind entries — so `ProductDiscovery`'s
  own shape move is structurally invisible to that check. Measured rather
  than assumed, per `D-IMPL-138`(c): `pytest -k surface` is the arbiter, and
  it caught the move **here**, not on `ProductDiscovery`.
  `register_product_class` is a `function`-kind entry whose keyword-only
  `discovery` parameter defaults to `NOT_DISCOVERABLE`, and a
  `function`-kind entry's captured shape is `str(inspect.signature(obj))`,
  which renders a default argument's own `repr()` inline — so the fourth
  axis's move surfaces transitively, on the one function that carries the
  sentinel as a default, rather than on `ProductDiscovery` directly
- Migration: none. Every call site that does not pass `discovery=` continues
  to receive `NOT_DISCOVERABLE`, whose *value* is unchanged (still the class
  not offered for lookup on any axis) — only the sentinel's own printed
  representation grew a field

*Recorded by `16-27-discovery-audiences.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.http_api.routes.canonical_response

- Surface: ccf_runtime.http_api.routes.canonical_response
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `canonical_response(payload) -> Response` — the canonical body plus
  the content-address header, and nothing else
- After: `canonical_response(payload, *, replay_ref: str | None = None) ->
  Response`. One keyword-only parameter, defaulting to `None`, which publishes
  `REPLAY_REF_HEADER` when supplied and sets no header when not
- Why: the product door is the one call site with a replay reference to publish;
  the other four serve Control Plane and replay artifacts rather than a
  delivered Context Product and pass nothing. A sibling channel on the response
  is the only place the reference can go without changing the bytes the body
  claims an address for
- Migration: none. Additive and defaulted — all five existing call sites read
  identically and behave identically, and the response is byte-for-byte
  unchanged when the argument is omitted

*Recorded by `17-10-both-doors-carry-the-replay-reference.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.BindingDocument

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.BindingDocument
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `root`, `config`, `resolver: str`, `question: str`, `connection: str |
  None` — three reference strings and one path
- After: `root`, `config`, `plan: str` (the one reference), `question:
  QuestionDeclaration`, `perspective: Perspective`, the eight resolver data
  fields, `artifact_store: Path`, `debt: DebtDeclaration`, `connection:
  Mapping[str, object] | None` (already composed, paths already contained)
- Why: the document changed from *three pointers at deployment code* to *the
  data the SDK builds a resolver from, plus one pointer at a projection*
- Migration: `document.resolver` and `document.question` no longer exist.
  Callers that loaded the resolver factory now read the fields directly; the SDK
  builds the resolver

*Recorded by `17-12-binding-document-data-only.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.inprocess.InProcessAdapter

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.inprocess.InProcessAdapter
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: booted, then called the deployment's `resolver` factory and its
  `question` factory, and passed whatever the latter returned straight to the
  former
- After: boots, resolves the one `plan:` reference, and **builds
  `GovernedResolver` itself** from the document's data — selecting `17-11`'s
  `AssemblyDebtScorer` (with `DeclaredFactor` for `C` and `S` at the declared
  values) and the file-backed `ArtifactStore`, because `artifact_store:`
  declares a path and a path is what a file-backed store takes. The typed
  `ProductRequest` is assembled per question from the declared vocabulary, with
  `schema_version` read off the registered product class rather than restated
- Why: `D-IMPL-141`'s ruling. The deployment supplies the projection and
  nothing else
- Migration: a deployment on the rejected shape deletes its resolver factory,
  its question factory, its connection factory and its debt scorer, and keeps
  its projection. The client's own API is unchanged — `ProductClient.for_product`
  and `ask` are byte-identical, and the binding address is unchanged
- Note: this composition is the thing `17-13` is expected to lift into a
  `build_governed_resolver(...)` helper, so a second transport does not grow a
  second copy of it. It lives in the adapter today because there is exactly one
  transport today

*Recorded by `17-12-binding-document-data-only.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.BindingDocument

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.BindingDocument
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: fifteen fields. A deployment could say where its config, its
  projection, its artifact store and its graph were, and could say nothing at
  all about the program a transport might have to launch to reach it
- After: a sixteenth field, `server: Path | None` — this deployment's own server
  program, resolved absolute against the directory **holding** the deployment
  and `None` when the document states none. Contained but deliberately **not**
  existence-checked: whether the program has to be on disk is the transport's
  question (`mcp+stdio` asks, because it launches it; an in-process deployment
  never does), and the `artifact_store` precedent rather than the `config` one
  is the right one
- Why: a deployment already declares how it is *assembled* — its projection, its
  Control Plane document, its graph. How it is *reached* is the same kind of
  fact and was the one thing a client had to be told out of band, in its own
  binding string. The base is the deployment root's **parent** rather than the
  root because a server program is not deployment content: the ordinary case is
  a remote server reached at an endpoint, with this key **absent entirely**, and
  a rule forcing the program inside the root would teach that a server belongs
  in a deployment, which is false
- Migration: none for a reader — the field is `None` on every document that does
  not declare one, and no existing document did. A deployment that wants to be
  reachable over `mcp+stdio` must now declare `server:`; see the adapter entry
  below

*Recorded by `18.1-04-the-deployment-declares-its-server.md`.*

#### ccf.client.codec.decode_outcome — returns the pair

- Surface: ccf.client.codec.decode_outcome
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `decode_outcome(raw: bytes, *, sent: WireRequest) -> WireOutcome`,
  with **no shipped call site** — both adapters built the outcome directly,
  so the one path that parses `request` and `content_address` out of
  sender-produced bytes was reached only by this package's own tests
- After: `decode_outcome(raw: bytes, *, sent: WireRequest) -> DecodedOutcome`.
  The body is otherwise unchanged: the parse, the malformed-bytes raise and
  the check order are exactly as they were. `ProductClient.ask` now calls it
  on what the adapter returned, so `CLNT-08` stops being a claim about an
  unshipped function
- Why: `C-05`. See `DecodedOutcome` above for why the vessel has to travel out
  with the outcome rather than be re-parsed by the caller
- Migration: a caller reading an outcome field off the return now reads it off
  `.outcome`; the vessel it would otherwise re-parse is `.vessel`. Every
  in-tree call site is updated in this plan

*Recorded by `19-02-the-adapter-seam-carries-bytes.md`.*

### Not public Python API

#### tools/lints/__main__.py

- Surface: tools/lints/__main__.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `LINTED_TREES = ("src", "runtime", "examples")` and
  `RULE_TREE_SCOPE["no-hardcoding"] = ("src", "runtime", "examples")` — the
  No-Hardcoding Rule did not reach test modules
- After: both tuples gain `"tests"`. Two edits, not one: `run()` iterates
  `LINTED_TREES` and *then* filters each rule by `RULE_TREE_SCOPE`, so a tree
  named only in the scope map is never walked and the widening would be a
  no-op. `harness-imports`, `algorithmic`, and `orchestration-imports` stay
  `("src", "runtime")` — test modules exercising the harness adapters import
  harness SDKs, and CLAUDE.md §7.3 words that invariant over core
- Why: D-IMPL-78 (Dave, 2026-08-04). D-09 relocates the example CI assertions
  into `tests/`, and those assertions carry domain expectations read from
  `expected:` artifact blocks. Without this scope the relocation would move
  domain values out of a structural invariant's reach with every CI gate still
  green — a CI-invisible weakening arrived at as a side effect of an
  ergonomics change. The hole is closed before any relocation happens
- Migration: downstream forks that add test modules with inline domain
  literals — prompt-shaped strings, numeric threshold tables, direct `Agent` /
  `Task` / `Workflow` construction — will now see CI findings under
  `tests/`. The fix is the same one `/examples` already takes: read the
  expected value from the artifact the test asserts about, and construct
  agents through the Agent Factory from fixture artifacts. The exemption
  surface for a whole tree is this config file, never an allowlist entry

*Recorded by `15-04-lint-scope.md`.*

#### tools/lints/no_hardcoding.py

- Surface: tools/lints/no_hardcoding.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `cinv01a-prompt-shape` scored every prose-shaped literal that
  survived the SQL/URI/short-string filters. Developer-facing diagnostics were
  indistinguishable from prompts to the rule, so a multi-sentence CI failure
  message naming its own fix scored as a system instruction
- After: `is_developer_diagnostic()` runs before scoring. A literal that
  carries at least one `DIAGNOSTIC_MARKERS` signal — a backticked command, a
  repo-relative filename, a `callable()` reference — and does not
  `addresses_a_reader()` (role-preamble opener, second-person pronoun, or
  template-brace density) is a diagnostic, and is not scored
- Why: Dave's ruling of 2026-08-04, recorded in `15-03-audit-baseline.md` and
  binding on this plan: resolve the two prose-diagnostic false positives by
  refining the rule, **not** by adding allowlist entries. Two allowlist entries
  today becomes friction on every future diagnostic message, and this phase's
  conventions actively encourage diagnostics that name their own fix (15-01
  ships `test_the_drift_message_names_its_own_fix` asserting exactly that).
  The distinction is mechanically detectable: a prompt is a string sent to a
  model, a diagnostic is a string shown to a developer, and the old rule
  detected "long English string literal" — a proxy for the wrong thing
- Migration: a CINV-01a finding that was previously suppressed by an allowlist
  entry on a diagnostic message can have that entry deleted; none existed in
  this repo, and none were added. A genuine prompt is unaffected — the reader
  test means it cannot escape by mentioning a filename, proven by
  `test_a_prompt_wearing_diagnostic_markers_is_still_flagged`

*Recorded by `15-04-lint-scope.md`.*

#### src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py

- Surface: src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the module docstring and the `DeclaredArtifact` class docstring both
  named "operator parameter sets" — unqualified — as a shape whose schema belongs
  to a deployment
- After: both say **deployment-authored** operator parameter sets, and both state
  the rule D-01 actually states: the SDK owns the schema of what it ships and
  never invents one for what it does not. The four categories the SDK ships have
  typed classes; everything else takes the open envelope
- Why: D-01. The unqualified claim was no longer true once this plan shipped the
  four typed classes, and amending only one of the two sites would leave the
  module contradicting itself. The policy-bundle rationale is kept intact — it
  was sound for policy bundles and remains the reason `DeclaredArtifact` exists;
  what changed is its scope, not its truth. mkdocstrings publishes both
  docstrings, so Phase 16's rendered reference changes with this edit, which is
  why it is a recorded surface change and not a comment tidy
- Migration: none — no name, signature or behavior changes; the docstrings are
  the deliverable

*Recorded by `15-08-operator-parameters.md`.*

#### ccf.signal-batch

- Surface: ccf.signal-batch
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the event document used flat keys the SDK contract does not declare —
  `source_system_id:` for what the contract nests as `source: {system_id: ...}`,
  and `payload:` for what the contract calls `raw_payload:`. `tenant_id` was not
  in the event document at all; it was read from `ontology.yaml`
- After: the document is in the SDK's own envelope vocabulary — nested
  `source: {system_id: ...}` and `raw_payload:` — with `tenant_id` declared once
  on the batch, alongside the batch's `semver` lane
- Why: the translation between the two vocabularies is **removed rather than
  relocated**. The SDK does not grow a second field vocabulary for a shape it
  already owns, and D-15B permits the churn. The change is to the *container*
  keys only: `raw_payload`'s contents stay opaque to L1 and its keys stay the
  source system's vocabulary, exactly as `SignalEnvelope`'s docstring states
  (T-15-09-04, accepted). This entry is `Public surface: false` because an
  artifact-document format change can never appear in an `__all__`-derived
  manifest diff — see `docs/surface-changes/README.md`
- Migration: transform each event document mechanically —
  1. `source_system_id: X` becomes `source:` / `  system_id: X`
  2. `payload:` becomes `raw_payload:` (contents unchanged)
  3. add `tenant_id:` at the batch root, carrying the value previously read from
     the ontology document, and add `tenant_id:` on each envelope — the batch
     refuses a disagreement rather than stamping over one
  4. add the artifact's own `semver:` lane at the document root

*Recorded by `15-09-signal-perspective-productclass.md`.*

#### examples/00-quickstart/artifacts/resolution-spec.yaml

- Surface: examples/00-quickstart/artifacts/resolution-spec.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `ontology.yaml`, carrying `schema_version`, `artifact_version`, `tenant_id`, `entity_rules`, `relationship_rules`, `classification_attribute_by_class` and `similarity_corpus_attribute_by_class`
- After: `ccf.resolution-spec` carrying `semver`, `entity_rules` and `relationship_rules` and nothing else
- Why: D-03 makes the class closed-world over resolution rules only; the two attribute maps moved to the operator-parameters documents that consume them, and `tenant_id` to the manifest
- Migration: rename the file, replace both root version keys with a single `semver`, move `tenant_id` to the manifest, and move each attribute map to its operator's parameters document

*Recorded by `15-13-example-00.md`.*

#### examples/00-quickstart/artifacts/events.yaml

- After: a `ccf.signal-batch` with `semver`, `tenant_id`, and envelopes using nested `source: {system_id: ...}` and `raw_payload:`
- Why: the document adopts the SDK's own envelope vocabulary, so the loader has no translation to perform and none to drift; the batch carries its tenant because every event in a batch belongs to one, and it disagrees loudly rather than being repaired
- Migration: nest `source_system_id` under `source: {system_id: ...}`, rename `payload:` to `raw_payload:`, add `tenant_id` at batch level and on each envelope

- After: a `ccf.operator-parameters.policy` document carrying both, and deliberately no `permitted_policy_bundle_versions`

#### examples/00-quickstart/artifacts/operator-parameters-similarity.yaml

- Surface: examples/00-quickstart/artifacts/operator-parameters-similarity.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `operator_parameters.similarity:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, with `similarity_corpus_attribute_by_class` in `ontology.yaml` and `index_version` alongside the query
- After: a `ccf.operator-parameters.similarity` document carrying query, threshold, ranked classes and the corpus attribute map; the index version moves out
- Why: the corpus attribute map governs index *construction* and belongs with the artifact that declares it. The index version does not: it identifies a corpus state at a named boundary, not a parameter set, so it is supplied to the builder per run
- Migration: move `similarity_corpus_attribute_by_class` out of the ontology; pass `similarity_index_version` to `build_operator_registry`

*Recorded by `15-13-example-00.md`.*

- Migration: lift the block to a document root and add `semver`

#### examples/00-quickstart/artifacts/product-class.yaml

- Surface: examples/00-quickstart/artifacts/product-class.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `product_class:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, carrying `plan_id` and `product_ttl_seconds` and declaring no operator set
- After: a `ccf.product-class` document declaring `required_operator_keys` and `max_ttl_key`; `plan_id` and `product_ttl_seconds` move to the deployment's own governance document
- Why: `required_operator_keys` is the authored declared side of the lineage gate. Without it the projection returned the operator registry's own keys and Gate 2 compared a value against itself. `max_ttl_version` stays absent because registering the ceiling mints it
- Migration: add `required_operator_keys` naming the operator keys your projection depends on, and read it in your projection instead of the registry's keys

*Recorded by `15-13-example-00.md`.*

- Why: the SDK never invents a policy-bundle schema, but it does own the envelope, and the envelope is what gives a deployment-authored document a version lane and a checkable class identity

- After: a document in the `DeclaredArtifact` envelope, registered by the manifest under the key the definition's axis-4 ref resolves to

#### examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/product-class.yaml

- Surface: examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/product-class.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `product_class:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, with no declared operator set — `run.py` passed `operators.keys()` to the projection plan
- After: a `ccf.product-class` document carrying `max_ttl_key` and `required_operator_keys`, and deliberately no `max_ttl_version`
- Why: `required_operator_keys` is the authored declared side of Gate 2 (INV-CH7-47); a plan returning the registry's own keys made the gate compare a value against itself. `max_ttl_version` is minted by *registering* the ceiling, so an artifact declaring it would be authoring a hash of something it does not contain
- Migration: lift the block to the root, add `semver`, add `max_ttl_key` and `required_operator_keys`, drop `plan_id` and `product_ttl_seconds` (they moved to the governance document), and stop passing the registry's keys to your plan

*Recorded by `15-15-examples-03-07.md`.*

#### examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/product-governance.yaml

- Surface: examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/product-governance.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `max_ttl_seconds`, `product_ttl_seconds`, `product_class.plan_id`, `operator_parameters.similarity.index_version` and `artifacts.replay_record_class`, scattered through `controlplane.yaml`
- After: one `DeclaredArtifact`-envelope document, `class_id: atlas-commerce.product-governance`, carrying the four governance values the SDK declines to own
- Why: these are values the SDK deliberately holds no default for; grouping them in one deployment-authored document keeps each one's independence visible (the TTL ceiling and the product TTL are separately authored, which is what leaves Gate 3 a real comparison)
- Migration: collect the values under `declaration:` and name the document `role: register`

*Recorded by `15-15-examples-03-07.md`.*

#### examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/decision.yaml

- Surface: examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/decision.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `decision:` and `instruction_parameters:` blocks inside `controlplane.yaml`
- After: one `DeclaredArtifact`-envelope document, `class_id: atlas-commerce.decision-inputs`, **`role: load`**
- Why: what instant one run asked about, and what parameters it bound, are not part of what the fabric was *permitted to do* — registering them would put them in replay dimension 2, where re-running at a different boundary would read back as a governance change
- Migration: collect the values under `declaration:` and name the document `role: load`

*Recorded by `15-15-examples-03-07.md`.*

#### examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/expected.yaml

- Surface: examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/expected.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `expected:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, asserted against inside `run.py`
- After: one `DeclaredArtifact`-envelope document, `class_id: atlas-commerce.expected-product`, `role: load`, read by `tests/examples/test_harness_portability_output.py`; it additionally declares `harness_leg_count`
- Why: an expectation is not a permission, so it has no business in Control Plane state; and the declared leg count closes the vacuity hole in "all legs agree", which is trivially true of one leg
- Migration: collect the values under `declaration:`, name the document `role: load`, and read them from `booted.artifacts` in your test rather than inlining them

*Recorded by `15-15-examples-03-07.md`.*

#### examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/harnesses.yaml

- Surface: examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/harnesses.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: three top-level per-harness blocks, read with `yaml.safe_load`
- After: one `DeclaredArtifact`-envelope document, `class_id: atlas-commerce.harness-bindings`, **`role: load`**, with the three blocks under `declaration:`
- Why: a harness binding is how a harness is stood up *outside* the fabric, so it is not a fabric permission and has no business in replay dimension 2 — which harness ran is recorded per leg on the `HarnessTrace` instead
- Migration: nest the per-harness blocks under `declaration:`, add `semver` and `class_id`, name it `role: load`, and pass `artifact.declaration` where you passed the parsed document

*Recorded by `15-15-examples-03-07.md`.*

#### examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/live.yaml

- After: one `DeclaredArtifact`-envelope document, `class_id: atlas-commerce.live-runner`, with both blocks under `declaration:`
- Why: it needed an honest `$schema` header like every other document in the directory. It is deliberately **not** a manifest entry: it configures the opt-in live legs, which are excluded from `make ci`, so naming it in the manifest would make the CI leg parse configuration for a mode it never enters
- Migration: nest the two blocks under `declaration:`, add `semver` and `class_id`, and load it with `load_artifact` rather than `yaml.safe_load`

#### examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/agents/agent-definition.yaml

- Surface: examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/agents/agent-definition.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `agents/order_insights.yaml`, one file carrying three things — a `keys:` block, a `referenced_artifacts:` block with two artifacts inlined, and the definition itself under `agent_definition:`
- After: the `ccf.agent-definition` at the document root, with a real `$schema` header; the keys moved to `agent-registration.yaml` and the two referents to `agents/policy-regime.yaml` and `agents/debt-profile.yaml`
- Why: one class, one document — a file carrying three classes cannot be annotated, because each header marks the others' keys as errors. Inlining the referents also gave one document several version identities, which is what `VersionedRef` exists to prevent
- Migration: lift `agent_definition:` to the root, move the `keys:` block to a registration document, and split each `referenced_artifacts` entry into its own envelope document whose `semver` equals the version its ref pins

#### examples/07-artifact-store-mcp/artifacts/product-governance.yaml

- Surface: examples/07-artifact-store-mcp/artifacts/product-governance.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `max_ttl_seconds`, `product_ttl_seconds`, `product_class.plan_id`, the similarity `index_version`, and the whole `artifacts:` block (replay record class, product class and name, policy-bundle class and name), scattered through `controlplane.yaml`
- After: one `DeclaredArtifact`-envelope document, `class_id: nordhaven-logistics.product-governance`, carrying all of them — with the artifact class/name values renamed `product_artifact_class`, `product_artifact_name`, `policy_bundle_artifact_class`, `policy_bundle_artifact_name`
- Why: these are values the SDK declines to own. Grouping them here also makes the store claim legible: both runs read the same class and name values from this same document, which is what makes "the same artifacts landed at the same versioned URIs" a statement about the two *stores* rather than about two configurations
- Migration: collect the values under `declaration:`, rename the artifact class/name keys, and name the document `role: register`

*Recorded by `15-15-examples-03-07.md`.*

- Why: what instant one run asked about is not part of what the fabric was permitted to do — and here it is what makes both store-backed runs replay the *identical* decision, so the byte-equality assertion is about the stores rather than about the wall clock
- Migration: collect the values under `declaration:` and name the document `role: load`

*Recorded by `15-15-examples-03-07.md`.*

- Why: an expectation is not a permission. The declared role set closes a vacuity hole: "the two runs' persisted roles agree" is trivially true of two empty sets, so the roles are also checked against what the deployment says it files

#### examples/08-e2e-demo/artifacts/operator-parameters-similarity.yaml

- Public surface: false

- Before: the `operator_parameters.similarity:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, with `similarity_corpus_attribute_by_class` in `ontology.yaml` and `index_version` alongside the query
- After: a `ccf.operator-parameters.similarity` document carrying query, threshold, ranked classes and the corpus attribute map; the index version moves out

#### examples/08-e2e-demo/artifacts/product-class.yaml

- Surface: examples/08-e2e-demo/artifacts/product-class.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `product_class:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, carrying `plan_id` and `product_ttl_seconds` and declaring no operator set
- After: a `ccf.product-class` document declaring `required_operator_keys` and `max_ttl_key`; `plan_id` and `product_ttl_seconds` move to the deployment's own governance document
- Why: `required_operator_keys` is the authored declared side of the lineage gate. Without it the projection returned the operator registry's own keys — `PaymentNetworkPlan(required=operators.keys())` — and Gate 2 compared a value against itself

#### examples/08-e2e-demo/artifacts/policy-bundle.yaml

- Surface: examples/08-e2e-demo/artifacts/policy-bundle.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `policy_bundle:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, registered as a bare mapping under the key `meridian-bank/policy-bundle/financial-crime-analyst`
- After: a document in the open `DeclaredArtifact` envelope — `semver`, `class_id`, `declaration` — registered under the short manifest key `policy-bundle`
- Why: the SDK never invents a policy-bundle schema, but it does own the envelope, and the envelope is what gives a deployment-authored document a version lane and a checkable class identity
- Migration: wrap your bundle in `declaration:` and add `semver` and `class_id`. **Note that both the content and the Control Plane key change, so the pinned version changes and any recorded replay reference over this bundle is regenerated** — see the narration note below

- After: a `ccf.agent-definition` document whose **root is the artifact**, annotated with that class's committed schema

#### examples/08-e2e-demo/artifacts/skill-dispatch.yaml

- Surface: examples/08-e2e-demo/artifacts/skill-dispatch.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `grant:`, `actors:` and `dispatch:` blocks inside `skills.yaml`
- After: one deployment-authored `role: load` document in the `DeclaredArtifact` envelope, carrying `grant_request`, `requesting_actor` and `dispatch`
- Why: **this is deliberately not a `ccf.skill-grant`, despite that class having a committed schema.** `SkillGrant` requires `agent_definition_hash`, which is derived identity computed by mechanism at request time — a hash is never an artifact value, so a standalone document of that class cannot be authored and a `ccf.skill-grant` header would be one its own class rejects. What *is* authorable is the grant request, and `run.py` completes it by supplying the hash
- Migration: rename your `grant:` block to `grant_request:` inside a declared document, and leave the derived hash out

*Recorded by `15-16-example-08.md`.*

- Why: a key is where a document is *filed*, not part of what it says, and the author-versioned documents' roots had to become their artifacts. The skill catalog entry's key is deliberately absent — `SkillCatalog` derives it from the entry's own tenant and name, so authoring one would be a second, drifting statement of a value mechanism already owns
- Migration: collect the keys your driver reads into a declared document

- Before: the `artifacts:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`
- After: a deployment-authored `DeclaredArtifact`, `role: load`, carrying the store's class names and the two negative probes' values; `replay_record_class` moved to `product-governance.yaml`
- Why: a probe is not a permission — putting it in force would say the fabric was permitted to resolve an unregistered version, which is the opposite of what the probe proves
- Migration: split governance values from probe values and read both off `booted.artifacts`

#### examples/06-decision-replay/run.py

- Surface: examples/06-decision-replay/run.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: 1,320 lines, 175 mechanical (five marshalling helpers, a 77-line operator-construction function, a 41-line registration prologue, an 8-line boot), 130 relocatable assert/print lines, and a `record()`/`replay_decision()` split whose assertions ran only under `make example-06`
- After: 1,075 lines, 12 mechanical, 0 asserts, 0 prints — one `run(*, root)` returning a typed result, with the guarantee relocated to `tests/examples/test_decision_replay_output.py`
- Why: `make examples` proves a spine runs; `pytest` proves it is correct (D-09). What stays in adopter code stays deliberately: the MaxTTL registration, `register_product_class`, the product's own lineage, `run_gates` with its independent `engine_resolved`, and — the one that matters most in this file — the explicit `pins.version_for` lookup behind every `PolicyBinding` handed to `build_replay_record`
- Migration: split `main()` from a `run()` that returns its state; move assertions to a test module; note that `run()` leaves the fabric handle OPEN because the replay leg reads through its reader

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/producer-manifest.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/producer-manifest.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: absent — `producer_child.py` registered artifacts by hand in a 32-line prologue, including a loop that registered artifacts out of another artifact's body
- After: a `ccf.deployment-manifest` naming sixteen documents by `path`, `key`, `class_id` and `role`, plus the tenant and the backend kind
- Why: the set of artifacts in force is replay dimension 2, so it must be one enumerated, content-hashable document rather than a sequence of registration calls no artifact records
- Migration: write a manifest and call `load_and_boot`; the hand-written prologue plus `boot()` still works unchanged

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-manifest.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-manifest.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: absent — `run.py` built the consumer's resolution spec from a nested `federation.entity_rule` block inside `a2a.yaml` and registered the rest by hand
- After: a second `ccf.deployment-manifest` naming eight documents, including the consumer's own resolution spec, policy bundle, admitted product class and peering entitlement
- Why: the consumer is a deployment in its own right; a shared manifest would assert that one artifact set was in force for both sides, which is exactly the thing the A2A trust boundary exists because it is not true
- Migration: give each process its own manifest rather than one bundle read twice

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/resolution-spec.yaml

- After: a `ccf.resolution-spec` carrying `semver`, `entity_rules` and `relationship_rules` and nothing else
- Why: D-03 makes the class closed-world over resolution rules only; the two attribute maps moved to the operator-parameters documents that consume them, and `tenant_id` to the manifest
- Migration: rename the file, replace both root version keys with a single `semver`, and move each displaced block to the document named below

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-resolution-spec.yaml

- After: a `ccf.resolution-spec` at the standard nesting, loaded by the consumer's manifest like any other
- Why: a resolution spec is a resolution spec whichever deployment authors it; the nesting variant existed only because the consumer had no manifest of its own, and it cost a 19-line translation helper
- Migration: lift the rule to a document root under `entity_rules:` and name it from your manifest

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/policy-bundle.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/policy-bundle.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `policy_bundle:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, registered as a bare mapping under the long key `larkfield-estates/policy-bundle/situation-desk`
- After: a document in the open `DeclaredArtifact` envelope — `semver`, `class_id`, `declaration` — registered under the short manifest key `policy-bundle`
- Why: the SDK never invents a policy-bundle schema, but it owns the envelope, and the envelope is what gives a deployment-authored document a version lane and a checkable class identity
- Migration: wrap your bundle in `declaration:` and add `semver` and `class_id`. Both the content and the key change, so the pinned version changes

*Recorded by `15-18-example-04.md`.*

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-policy-bundle.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-policy-bundle.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: absent — the consumer registered the *producer's* `policy_bundle` block from the same `controlplane.yaml` and separately recomputed its content hash as `producer_policy_version`
- After: the consumer's own envelope document, content-identical to the producer's and registered from the consumer's manifest
- Why: the pre-rewrite `assert policy_version == producer_policy_version` compared two reads of one file. As two documents the agreement is falsifiable, and it is exactly what the trust grant's `permitted_policy_regimes` entitles
- Migration: author the regime you entitle a peer to claim as your own document rather than reading the peer's

*Recorded by `15-18-example-04.md`.*

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/operator-parameters-similarity.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/operator-parameters-similarity.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `operator_parameters.similarity:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, with `similarity_corpus_attribute_by_class` in `ontology.yaml` and `index_version` alongside the query
- After: a `ccf.operator-parameters.similarity` document carrying query, threshold, ranked classes and the corpus attribute map; the index version moves to the governance document
- Why: the corpus attribute map governs index *construction* and belongs with the artifact that declares it. The index version does not: it identifies a corpus state at a named boundary, not a parameter set

- Before: the `perspective:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`
- After: a `ccf.perspective` document
- Why: a parsed artifact of this class **is** a `Perspective` and passes straight into projection invocation with no field translation
- Migration: lift the block to a document root and add `semver`

*Recorded by `15-18-example-04.md`.*

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/product-class.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/product-class.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `product_class:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, carrying `plan_id` and `product_ttl_seconds` and declaring no operator set
- After: a `ccf.product-class` document declaring `required_operator_keys` and `max_ttl_key`; `plan_id` and `product_ttl_seconds` move to the deployment's own governance document
- Why: `required_operator_keys` is the authored declared side of the lineage gate. Without it the projection returned the operator registry's own keys — `SituationPlan(required=operators.keys())` — and Gate 2 compared a value against itself
- Migration: add `required_operator_keys` naming the operator keys your projection depends on, and read it in your projection instead of the registry's keys

*Recorded by `15-18-example-04.md`.*

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-product-class.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-product-class.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: absent — the consumer registered its admitted product class from the producer's `product_class:` block
- After: the consumer's own `ccf.product-class` document, naming the schema it will admit and its own TTL key
- Why: a consumer that read its admission criteria off the producer's declaration would be verifying a value against itself; `permitted_schema_names` in the trust grant is built from this
- Migration: author the classes you admit rather than inheriting the peer's

*Recorded by `15-18-example-04.md`.*

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/product-governance.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/product-governance.yaml

- Before: `max_ttl_seconds`, `product_ttl_seconds`, `index_version` and `plan_id` scattered through `controlplane.yaml`
- After: one deployment-authored document in the `DeclaredArtifact` envelope carrying all of them
- Why: these are values the SDK deliberately declines to own; the TTL ceiling and the product's own lifetime in particular stay two separately authored numbers, which is the whole reason Gate 3 is a check
- Migration: collect the equivalents from your Control Plane bundle into a declared document

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-product-governance.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-product-governance.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: absent — the consumer registered `max_ttl_seconds` from the producer's bundle
- After: the consumer's own envelope document carrying its own ceiling
- Why: the ceiling a consumer enforces is the consumer's policy; a peer does not get to raise it by declaring a longer one
- Migration: author your own ceiling for every class you admit

*Recorded by `15-18-example-04.md`.*

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/producer-peering.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/producer-peering.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `producer:` block and parts of the `consumer:` block inside `a2a.yaml`, read by BOTH processes
- After: the producer's own envelope document, `role: register`, naming who it is on the wire, which key id it signs under, which producing agent it asserts, and where it emits
- Why: which identity a fabric emits under is a standing statement of what it was permitted to do. Split from the consumer's entitlement so the federation relationship is stated from both sides independently — one shared document would make the signature check pass for a reason unrelated to trust
- Migration: split your federation bundle along the trust boundary: what you assert goes in your Control Plane, what you entitle a peer to assert goes in theirs

*Recorded by `15-18-example-04.md`.*

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/peering-trust.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/peering-trust.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `consumer:` and `producer:` blocks inside `a2a.yaml`
- After: the consumer's own envelope document, `role: register`, carrying the trust-policy REQUEST — peer ids, the key identifier the peer may sign under, the producing agent it may assert, the ceilings, and the receive path
- Why: **this is deliberately not a `ccf.peering-trust-policy`, despite that class being real SDK mechanism.** `PeerTrustGrant` requires `signing_keys` — key MATERIAL, generated per run and never committed — so a standalone document of that class cannot be authored and a header naming it would be one its own class rejects. What is authorable is the request; `run.py` completes it with the run's public key. Same shape as `max_ttl_version`'s deliberate absence from a product-class document
- Migration: author the policy half of your trust entry and supply key material at registration time

*Recorded by `15-18-example-04.md`.*

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/federation-ingest.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/federation-ingest.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `federation:` block inside `a2a.yaml`, mixing the L1 mapping with the entity resolution rule
- After: a deployment-authored `role: register` document carrying the mapping only; the entity rule moved to `consumer-resolution-spec.yaml`
- Why: D-03 makes a resolution spec closed-world over resolution rules, so the rule and the mapping cannot share a document. What shape of peer claim a fabric turns into signal is a standing permission, hence `register`
- Migration: split the block: rules to your resolution spec, aliases and the source system id to a declared document

*Recorded by `15-18-example-04.md`.*

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-lineage.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-lineage.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `consumer.lineage:` block inside `a2a.yaml`
- After: a deployment-authored `role: load` document
- Why: the consumer's lineage at admission is a statement about its own state at one instant, not about what it was permitted to do. Its operators stay deliberately disjoint from the producer's, so the merged set proves union rather than replacement
- Migration: lift the block into a declared document and mark it `role: load`

*Recorded by `15-18-example-04.md`.*

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/decision.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/decision.yaml
- Public surface: false

- Before: the `decision:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, with unquoted timestamps parsed by a bespoke `_instant` helper
- After: a deployment-authored `role: load` document with quoted instants
- Why: what instant one run asked about is not part of what the fabric was permitted to do. Quoting the timestamps removes the helper: the artifact loader suppresses PyYAML's timestamp resolver, so no zone-attaching translation is needed at the boundary
- Migration: quote your instants and mark the document `role: load`

- Why: a translation removed is one that cannot drift. `role: load` is FIXED by the SDK for this class: registering a batch would content-hash raw event data into replay dimension 2

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/agent-definition.yaml

- After: 781 lines, 0 mechanical — one `load_and_boot` of the same manifest at the top of `main()`, whose result supplies both the artifact values every assertion reads and the graph PASS 8 replays against; `build_operator_registry` rebuilds the operators
- Why: the replica must be rebuilt from the artifacts the original ran on, and reaching into another module's private helpers to do it was a second, drift-prone description of the same deployment. - Migration: boot your replica from the same manifest; do not import another module's private loaders
