## Removed

Surfaces that no longer exist. Each entry names what replaces it.

4 entries — 4 public Python surface, 0 not public Python API.

### Public Python surface

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.REQUIRED_KEYS

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.REQUIRED_KEYS
- Public surface: true
- Change type: removed
- Before: `("config", "resolver", "question")` — three required keys, two of
  them `<module-path>:<attribute>` factory references
- After: absent. Replaced by `DATA_KEYS` and `REFERENCE_KEYS`, which are
  separate constants because the distinction between *data* and *the one
  permitted reference* is the whole ruling. A single `REQUIRED_KEYS` tuple would
  have made the exception invisible at the constant that defines the schema
- Why: `D-IMPL-141`'s rejection removed `resolver:` and turned `question:` into
  a data mapping, so the tuple named nothing that still exists
- Migration: a caller enumerating required keys reads `(*DATA_KEYS,
  *REFERENCE_KEYS)`. `tests/client/test_binding_document.py` does exactly that,
  so a new required key cannot arrive without an absence-refusal test covering it

*Recorded by `17-12-binding-document-data-only.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.ResolverCall

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.ResolverCall
- Public surface: true
- Change type: removed
- Before: a frozen dataclass carrying the `Perspective` and `ProductRequest` a
  deployment's question factory returned
- After: absent. The adapter builds the `ProductRequest` from the document's
  declared question and reads the `Perspective` off the document
- Why: there is no question factory any more, so there is no factory return
  value to type
- Migration: none for a client developer — this type never appeared in the
  client's own surface

*Recorded by `17-12-binding-document-data-only.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.QuestionFactoryProtocol

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.QuestionFactoryProtocol
- Public surface: true
- Change type: removed
- Before: a `@runtime_checkable` Protocol describing the deployment callable
  that turned a client's question into a `ResolverCall`
- After: absent
- Why: `question:` is data. A Protocol describing how a deployment computes
  three strings and two more strings describes a computation that does not
  happen
- Migration: delete the factory; state its values in `question:` and
  `perspective:`

*Recorded by `17-12-binding-document-data-only.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.conforming_resolver

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.conforming_resolver
- Public surface: true
- Change type: removed
- Before: refused a resolver factory whose product did not satisfy
  `ServedProductResolverProtocol`
- After: absent, replaced by `conforming_plan_factory`
- Why: the SDK builds the resolver, so there is no deployment-returned resolver
  to structurally check. The check moved to the one thing a deployment still
  hands over as code
- Migration: none. A deployment substituting a whole resolver does so by not
  using the in-process adapter's binding-document path

*Recorded by `17-12-binding-document-data-only.md`.*

## Behavior changes

Surfaces whose **signature did not move** but whose observable behavior did. A generated signature diff is structurally incapable of seeing these, which is why D-15 asks for a curated log alongside the manifest rather than instead of it.

88 entries — 68 public Python surface, 20 not public Python API.

### Public Python surface

#### ccf.deployment.operators.build_operator_registry

- Surface: ccf.deployment.operators.build_operator_registry
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `def build_operator_registry(*, parameters, view, pins, policy_bundle_key,
  similarity_index_version=None) -> OperatorRegistry` — the builder dispatched on artifact
  TYPE through an `isinstance` chain and raised `ValueError` on anything it did not
  recognise, so a deployment's own operator had no route into the registry the operator plan
  reads
- After: the same signature plus one defaulted, keyword-only parameter,
  `deployment_operators: Sequence[Operator] = ()`, whose members are registered FIRST and
  sorted by `operator.key`, through `register(operator)` with **no category argument**
- Why: `D-59`. The registry beneath the builder was already open —
  `src/ccf/operators/registry.py::OperatorRegistry.register` reads category and parameter
  schema off the operator — but the composition path in front of it was not, so
  `examples/cp-00-institutional-holders/deployment.py` had to register into a private
  registry the projection plan never sees. That workaround is exactly why cp-00's recipe
  witness for step 1 is `payload_key_present` rather than `operator_in_lineage`
- Migration: none — additive and keyword-only with a default

*Recorded by `24.1.1-02-the-deployment-operator-reaches-the-plan.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.build_operator_registry

- Surface: ccf.deployment.build_operator_registry
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the package re-export of the same function, carrying the same signature
- After: the same re-export, carrying the reshaped signature above
- Why: the same change, visible at the package boundary because `ccf.deployment` re-exports
  the symbol; recorded as its own entry because the manifest records it as its own entry and
  a reader who imports from the package should not have to infer it from the module
- Migration: none — additive and keyword-only with a default

*Recorded by `24.1.1-02-the-deployment-operator-reaches-the-plan.md`.*

#### ccf.agents.assembler.DeterministicAssembler

- Surface: ccf.agents.assembler.DeterministicAssembler
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `(agent: 'Agent', projection: 'ProjectionOutput', acquisitions:
  'tuple[AcquiredContent, ...]' = ()) -> None` — the reference assembler could only succeed
  or raise, so the SDK's own worked example demonstrated *raise-or-succeed* while the
  codebase taught refusal-as-data
- After: the same constructor plus one optional field, `request_contract:
  'ProductRequestContract | None' = None`. With it absent the assembler is byte-for-byte
  what it was; with it present, `assemble` makes ONE call into
  `src/ccf/products/request.py::refuse_request` and RETURNS the resulting
  `ProductRequestViolation`
- Why: `D-IMPL-214`, ruling on criterion 6 — *"`DeterministicAssembler` is what deployments
  diff against. If only a test-local assembler can refuse, the reference impl still
  demonstrates 'raise or succeed' and criterion 6 becomes theater."* The conformity check is
  ordered AFTER the two identity checks, because a question asked of the wrong agent is not
  a question about a contract's conformity
- Migration: none — additive and keyword-only with a default

*Recorded by `24.1.1-03-a-refusal-returns-as-data.md`.*

#### ccf.agents.DeterministicAssembler

- Surface: ccf.agents.DeterministicAssembler
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the package re-export of the same class, carrying the same constructor shape
- After: the same re-export, carrying the reshaped constructor above
- Why: the same change, visible at the package boundary because `ccf.agents` re-exports the
  symbol. Recorded as its own entry because the manifest records it as its own entry — and
  because the plan predicted ONE reshaped entry where the tree has TWO, which is the reason
  a fragment claims what was measured rather than what was expected
- Migration: none — additive and keyword-only with a default

*Recorded by `24.1.1-03-a-refusal-returns-as-data.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.record.ContentGenerationPath

- Surface: ccf.replay.record.ContentGenerationPath
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: dimension 5's field map without an assembler-seam member
- After: the same map plus `assembly_refusal: str = NO_ASSEMBLY_REFUSAL`. **Dimension 5 rather
  than dimension 2** because dimension 5 is *how the delivered content came to be* and a
  refusal is that path's terminus; a refusal is not a policy binding, which is what dimension
  2 records
- Why: `D-IMPL-214` Reading B — a named sub-structure inside an existing dimension, on the
  `hitl_actions` / `workflow_step_sequence` precedent this module already carries four times
- Migration: none — sentinel-defaulted

*Recorded by `24.1.1-04-a-refusal-has-an-identity-in-the-record.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.record.build_replay_record

- Surface: ccf.replay.record.build_replay_record
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the same signature without an assembler-seam keyword
- After: one added keyword, `assembly_refusal: 'str | None' = None`, threaded with the
  `if X is not None else SENTINEL` expression the three precedents use, and admitted only
  when it is the sentinel or a value that round-trips as a
  `src/ccf/products/request.py::ProductRequestViolation` — a hand-written block that merely
  looks like a refusal is the flattening `src/ccf/products/refusal.py::envelope_for` exists
  to forbid
- Why: the block has to reach the record from the caller that minted it, and the builder
  records it verbatim rather than reconstructing it
- Migration: none — sentinel-defaulted; all 40 call sites unchanged

*Recorded by `24.1.1-04-a-refusal-has-an-identity-in-the-record.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.load_artifact

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.load_artifact
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: parsed with `yaml.safe_load`, so PyYAML's timestamp resolver ran and a
  timestamp-shaped scalar arrived as a **naive** `datetime` — which
  `ControlPlaneRegistry.register` then refused as non-canonicalizable, forcing
  every caller to hand-normalize (six distinct helper implementations had
  drifted across eleven sites)
- After: parses through a private `_ArtifactLoader` with the
  `tag:yaml.org,2002:timestamp` resolver removed, so the scalar arrives as
  `str`; a contract field annotated `datetime` parses it into an **aware**
  value, and a date-time naming neither `Z` nor a `±HH:MM` offset is refused at
  parse by `_reject_naive_timestamps` with a message naming D-IMPL-38
- Why: D-02. `Z` is the ISO-8601 UTC designator, not an implicit timezone — the
  author stated UTC explicitly and PyYAML's resolver is what drops it. Refusing
  the author's document punished them for a rule they did not break, because the
  naive value was a *parser* defect rather than an authoring one. The SDK now
  reads timestamps as text and lets the contract parse them, exactly as it
  already refuses floats rather than repairing them. The resolver table is
  copied rather than mutated in place, so YAML parsing elsewhere in the process
  is unaffected.
- Migration: a caller relying on `load_artifact` handing back a `datetime` for a
  field its contract does not annotate as one now receives a `str` — annotate
  the field `datetime` on the contract and Pydantic parses it, aware. A document
  carrying a zone-less date-time is now refused rather than silently read as
  naive; state the zone as `Z` or as an explicit offset. No public
  timestamp-parsing helper is offered, deliberately: one would invite adopters
  to keep hand-normalizing the thing the loader now gets right.

*Recorded by `15-05-timestamps.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.registry.ControlPlaneProtocol

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.registry.ControlPlaneProtocol
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the Protocol declared `register`, `register_typed`, `resolve`,
  `resolve_latest` and `audit_events`. `register_versioned` — the D-05 hash-verified
  bump check — existed only on the reference `ControlPlaneRegistry`, so a conforming
  implementation could omit it entirely
- After: the Protocol also declares
  `register_versioned(key, artifact, artifact_class, *, actor=None, reissuable=False) -> str | RegistrationRefusal`,
  with a signature identical to the reference implementation's
- Why: D-IMPL-82 (Dave, 2026-08-05). The bump check is a governance obligation, and
  the Protocol's own docstring already argues that the obligations sit on the surface
  "precisely so a remote plane cannot conform while quietly dropping them" — a remote
  Control Plane that cannot run the bump check is exactly such a plane, and
  `load_and_boot` must be able to dispatch author-versioned classes to it to close
  CR-1 at all (T-15.1-01-01). Rejected: a narrow second
  `VersionedRegistrationProtocol`, which preserves the posture at the cost of a
  two-Protocol mental model in the phase whose subject is one mental model; and
  narrowing `load_and_boot` to the concrete `ControlPlaneRegistry`, which breaks the
  Protocol-over-inheritance posture CLAUDE.md §11 mandates. Recorded as
  `Change type: behavior` rather than `reshaped` because
  `docs/surface/v0.2-surface.json` records a Protocol's shape as `(*args, **kwargs)`:
  a member addition changes what conforms without moving any signature the generated
  manifest can see, which is the case the `behavior` class exists for
  (`docs/surface-changes/README.md`)
- Migration: a non-reference Control Plane implementation must now provide
  `register_versioned` — this is a FABR-07 remote-plane implementation obligation.
  The in-process `ControlPlaneRegistry` already satisfies it and needs no change; no
  caller's code changes. An implementation that omits the member no longer type-checks
  as conforming, and `isinstance(x, ControlPlaneProtocol)` returns `False` for it

*Recorded by `15.1-01-projection-requirements-and-protocol.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.classes.resolve_artifact_class

- Surface: ccf.deployment.classes.resolve_artifact_class
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `resolve_artifact_class(class_id)` returned the SDK constant for an id in
  `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` and the open `DeclaredArtifact` envelope for **every** other id,
  including every `ccf.`-prefixed one. The module docstring justified that with "refusing
  it would be the SDK claiming ownership of a namespace that is not its own"
- After: the name and the signature are unchanged and no manifest in the tree resolves
  differently. Two behavior changes: the six author-versioned ids now resolve to their
  **real contracts** instead of the envelope, and a `ccf.`-prefixed id in neither SDK
  mapping now raises `ValueError` naming D-IMPL-84 instead of resolving to the envelope.
  The docstring's namespace sentence is rewritten: the SDK claims exactly the `ccf.`
  prefix and nothing else, and the rejected four-id alternative is recorded with the
  reason
- Why: D-IMPL-84. The envelope path was reachable *under an SDK class name*, which is
  what made the squat work — arbitrary content content-hashed into Control Plane state,
  pinned into `ResolvedPins` and therefore into Decision Replay dimension 2, bound to
  `ccf.workflow-definition` with no `artifact_version` at all. The reservation is one
  prefix wide and no wider: every adopter namespace stays on the envelope path unchanged,
  and `tests/deployment/test_class_lookup.py` asserts that half too, so an implementation
  that refused *every* unrecognized id would fail
- Migration: **a deployment naming a `ccf.`-prefixed class id it does not own must rename
  it into its own namespace.** Concretely: a manifest entry whose `class_id` starts with
  `ccf.` and is neither in `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` nor in
  `SDK_VERSIONED_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` now fails at load with a message naming D-IMPL-84 and
  saying what to do. Two cases land there and both are the same instruction: an id that
  was a typo for a real SDK class (correct the spelling), and a deployment-authored class
  that borrowed the SDK's prefix (rename it — `acme.workflow-definition`, not
  `ccf.workflow-definition`). Nothing in this repository required the edit. Note also
  that `ccf.hitl-policy` and `ccf.scope-mapping` — SDK classes registered by adopter code
  against their real contracts, never through a manifest — are refused here rather than
  enveloped, which is a strict improvement on the hazard `examples/05-workflow-hitl`
  previously documented as something the adopter had to know to avoid

*Recorded by `15.1-03-namespace-reservation.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ManifestEntry

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ManifestEntry
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: one validator on `path`, rejecting only the glob characters `*`, `?` and `[`.
  An absolute path and a `..` segment both validated, so a manifest could name any file
  on the host
- After: a second validator, `_path_stays_beside_the_manifest`, refusing an absolute
  path, a rooted or drive-qualified path, and any `..` segment — asked of both
  `PurePosixPath` and `PureWindowsPath`, so one content-hashed document is admissible on
  every platform or on none. No field is added, removed or retyped
- Why: CR-01. `ManifestEntry.path`'s stated contract is "relative to the manifest file's
  own directory" and the contract did not hold it. D-IMPL-84 had already established in
  this same phase that a manifest is *not* assumed benign — the whole `ccf.` namespace is
  reserved precisely because a manifest can be authored to squat an SDK class id — and a
  document trusted that little does not also get to name `/`-rooted paths. The check is
  syntactic and therefore belongs at the boundary (CLAUDE.md §11: validation is at the
  boundary, never deferred); it is deliberately not the whole answer, because a plain
  name can still be a symlink out, which is what the `load_and_boot` entry below covers
- Migration: **a manifest naming a path outside its own directory now fails to parse**,
  with an `ArtifactLoadError` naming the offending path. No manifest in this repository
  was affected — every entry across the eleven example manifests and the deployment
  fixtures is already a plain relative name, and subdirectory-relative paths such as
  `agents/agent-definition.yaml` are unaffected because the rule is *inside the
  manifest's directory*, not *flat*. An adopter whose manifest reached outside must move
  or copy the document into the deployment, where it is versioned with the rest of the
  artifact set

*Recorded by `15.1-11-manifest-path-containment.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.ManifestEntry

- Surface: ccf.deployment.ManifestEntry
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the `ccf.deployment` re-export of the contract above, with the same
  glob-only validation
- After: the same re-export, carrying the added containment refusal
- Why: the package re-export is a distinct fully-qualified name and the phase-close
  cross-check matches at that granularity, so the behavior change is recorded against
  both paths an adopter can import. There is no separate mechanism here — it is the same
  class object
- Migration: identical to `ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ManifestEntry` above; the import
  path an adopter used does not change what is refused

*Recorded by `15.1-11-manifest-path-containment.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.manifest.load_and_boot

- Surface: ccf.deployment.manifest.load_and_boot
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `document = (root / entry.path).read_text(...)`, with nothing between the
  manifest's declaration and the open. Any YAML-parseable file on the host landed on
  `BootedFabric.artifacts`, and on `role: register` was content-hashed into Control Plane
  state and therefore into Decision Replay dimension 2; on a non-parseable file the
  `ArtifactLoadError` carried the file's contents back to the caller
- After: each entry's document is resolved against the manifest's own directory in
  **preflight** — before any document is opened and before anything is registered — and
  an entry resolving outside it raises `ValueError` naming the key, the declared path and
  the resolved path. `resolve()` follows symlinks, so a plain name that links out is
  refused; a link resolving back inside is admitted. The refusal names the resolved path
  so an operator can locate it and never the file's contents, which is the disclosure the
  check exists to stop
- Why: CR-01, and the reason this half exists separately from the contract half is
  measured rather than assumed — with only the contract validator in place, a symlinked
  plain name still booted and returned a file from outside the deployment
  (`tests/deployment/test_manifest.py::test_a_symlink_out_of_the_deployment_is_refused_at_the_read`).
  The preflight placement follows the ordering `_preflight_backend` already establishes
  in this function: a manifest whose seventh entry escapes must not have read its first
  six, and must certainly not have registered them
- Migration: **a manifest whose entry resolves outside its own directory now raises
  `ValueError` instead of booting.** Nothing else changes: the signature, the return
  type, the pin set and its content hash are all untouched, and every manifest already
  naming files beside itself behaves identically. A deployment relying on a symlink out
  of its own tree replaces the link with the document

*Recorded by `15.1-11-manifest-path-containment.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.load_and_boot

- Surface: ccf.deployment.load_and_boot
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the `ccf.deployment` re-export of the function above, with no containment check
- After: the same re-export, carrying the preflight containment refusal
- Why: the package re-export is a distinct fully-qualified name and is the import path
  the examples and the deployment tests actually use (`from ccf.deployment import
  load_and_boot`), so it is the one most adopters will read. Recorded against both for
  the same name-granularity reason as `ManifestEntry` above
- Migration: identical to `ccf.deployment.manifest.load_and_boot` above

*Recorded by `15.1-11-manifest-path-containment.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.protocols.GraphReader.query

- Surface: ccf.graph.protocols.GraphReader.query
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: absent — the only question the read seam could carry was
  `as_of(tenant_id, at)`; a predicate-bearing question had no wire into any
  reader
- After: `query(self, q: GraphQuery) -> GraphView` on the Protocol and on
  every conformer in the tree. Tenant scope arrives inside the frozen value
  (D-IMPL-05 satisfied by the contract, `GQ-01`); a conformant answerer
  answers any well-formed query, degrading internally to scan-and-filter
  where it holds no native translation (`GQ-29`, D-04); an empty answer is
  an answer, never an error (`GQ-23`)
- Why: D-04 rules that adapters always answer — no capability negotiation,
  no typed pushdown-refusal path — so the seam must exist on the Protocol
  before any pushdown translation does, and sqlite/postgres must answer any
  GraphQuery (including `allowed_entities`-bearing ones) from this plan on
- Migration: external `GraphReader` implementers must implement `query()`
  (and `attestation`, below). The reference degrade path is available to
  them: materialize records, then apply `ccf.graph.fallback.answer` for the
  query's predicate families and `allowed_entities`, then order per `GQ-22`.
  `as_of()` MAY be derived as
  `self.query(GraphQuery(tenant_id=tenant_id, as_of=at))` (D-07, `GQ-21`)

*Recorded by `15.2-05-query-seam-open.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.protocols.GraphReader.attestation

- Surface: ccf.graph.protocols.GraphReader.attestation
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: absent — a reader had no typed way to state how its answers
  acquired policy binding and lineage
- After: a property returning `SourceAttestation` (SDK-defined per CLAUDE.md
  §11). The live graph reports tier 1 (`assembly-stamped` /
  `graph-supplied` / `peer=None`); `SnapshotReader` delegates to the wrapped
  reader — a pin narrows scope, not provenance
- Why: the D-09 ruling makes the three trust tiers three well-known values
  of one schema; the seam-level property is what lets the engine read the
  attestation in force and pin it into replay with zero new mechanism
- Migration: external implementers return their source's modes; a live-graph
  wrapper returns the tier-1 value

*Recorded by `15.2-05-query-seam-open.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.reader.BaseGraphReader

- Surface: ccf.graph.reader.BaseGraphReader
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `as_of()` acquired rows via the two `SELECT_*_AS_OF` statements
  and materialized them; no predicate-bearing question existed
- After: `query(q)` is the template method — the `_translate_query(q)` hook
  answers the whole query or returns `None` (`GQ-32`; safe narrowing under a
  declined translation per `GQ-33`; no partial-family bookkeeping exists);
  the baseline path acquires tenant+as-of rows, materializes through the
  15.2-03 substrate-neutral core, applies `ccf.graph.fallback.answer`, and
  freezes through the interface-owned ordering. `as_of()` is now
  `self.query(GraphQuery(tenant_id=tenant_id, as_of=at))` — the D-07
  derivation, byte-identical, zero call-site churn. `attestation` reports
  tier 1. `translated_answers` / `fallback_answers` are per-instance
  monotonic telemetry counters, deliberately outside the replay record
  (D-04 — performance is never a replay input)
- Why: adapters supply dialect and rows; everything above the rows —
  materialization, semantics, ordering — must exist once, or the
  differential suite would be comparing accidents
- Migration: SQL backend subclasses (sqlite, postgres) inherit everything;
  no subclass change. A subclass MAY override `_translate_query` to earn
  native pushdown, under the whole-query-or-None contract

*Recorded by `15.2-05-query-seam-open.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.snapshots.SnapshotReader

- Surface: ccf.replay.snapshots.SnapshotReader.query
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the pinned reader scope-checked `as_of` / history reads only; a
  `GraphQuery` had no path into replay
- After: `query(q)` scope-checks `q.tenant_id` / `q.as_of` against the
  pinned snapshot before delegating to the wrapped reader's `query()`,
  raising `SnapshotScopeViolation` on mismatch — the deliberate
  raise-vs-empty-view contrast of `GQ-27`: a source answers questions; a
  pin enforces one. `attestation` delegates to the wrapped reader. The
  `as_of` delegation path is byte-identical to before
- Why: replayed executions will phrase reads as queries from plan 15.2-08
  on; a replay that could query outside its pin would silently import the
  future into a record of the past
- Migration: none — existing replay callers are unchanged

*Recorded by `15.2-05-query-seam-open.md`.*

#### ccf.operators.categories.PolicyFilter.read_intent

- Surface: ccf.operators.categories.PolicyFilter.read_intent
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: absent — the policy operator had no declared substrate rendering
- After: returns the policy-family `QueryFragment`
  (`permitted_policy_bundle_versions`, `classification_attribute_by_class`,
  `permitted_classifications`), a field-for-field projection of its
  parameter contract (`GQ-02`/`GQ-08`)
- Why: D-03 — the pushed admissibility predicate must mirror `_admits`

  `tests/conformance/test_substrate_properties.py` pins the mirror
- Migration: none — `apply()` is byte-identical

*Recorded by `15.2-07-read-intent-segmentation.md`.*

- Before: absent
- After: returns the temporal-family `QueryFragment`
  (`require_valid_at_boundary`); the as-of cut itself rides the mandatory

#### ccf.operators.categories.SimilaritySelect.read_intent

- Surface: ccf.operators.categories.SimilaritySelect.read_intent
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: absent
- After: returns `None`, full stop — Similarity consults an injected
  `SimilarityIndexProtocol` the substrate does not hold and is never
  pushable (D-03; the Phase 15.2 Deferred-Ideas ruling)

#### ccf.replay.record.build_replay_record

- Surface: ccf.replay.record.build_replay_record
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: dimensions 2 and 5 carried no query pins and no cap value
- After: signature unchanged — the new facts arrive on `execution` itself:
  `execution.executed_queries` becomes the dimension-5 pin tuple (hashed
  here, in order) and `execution.survivor_cap` becomes the dimension-2 cap
  pin
- Why: execution facts already travel to the builder on `ExecutionResult`;
  a new parameter would have been a second channel for the same facts
- Migration: none — executions built without the fields yield both sentinels

*Recorded by `15.2-08-replay-query-pins.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.engine.replay

- Surface: ccf.replay.engine.replay
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: re-execution ran with no survivor cap and compared the step
  sequence, the snapshot id, and the product address
- After: re-execution runs under the **recorded** dimension-2
  `survivor_cap`, and after the step-sequence check the re-executed query
  pins — one `content_hash` per issued `GraphQuery`, in order — are compared
  against the recorded `graph_query_sequence`; a mismatch is
  `ReplayDivergence`. A record carrying the pre-seam `NO_GRAPH_QUERIES`
  sentinel asserts it predates the query seam and the pin comparison stands
  down — the snapshot and product checks still bind it to the same answer
- Why: D-11 — replay recomputes each question and compares hashes, so a
  doctored pin tuple or a drifted question sequence is caught non-determinism;
  D-10 — the split depends on the cap, so re-executing under the deployment's
  *current* cap would be a re-computation wearing the decision's identifier
- Migration: none — pre-phase records replay exactly as before

*Recorded by `15.2-08-replay-query-pins.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.engine.resolve_artifact

- Surface: ccf.replay.engine.resolve_artifact
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: fetched bytes were validated with the **current** record class and
  the rebuilt model was re-hashed as the tamper check — so
  sentinel-defaulted fields added by later contract versions changed the
  re-derived hash, and honest pre-extension records raised
  `ReplayDivergence` (the false A5 assumption, empirically reproduced on
  `PRE_CHANGE_CANONICAL`: stored `26cdaded…`, re-derived `ad21e6ab…`)
- After: verifies `sha256(raw fetched bytes) == content_address` **before**
  model validation; the re-validated-model re-hash is gone
- Why: D-IMPL-89 (option a) — the put-time address *is* the sha256 of the
  stored canonical bytes and the store's `get` contract returns those exact
  bytes, so byte fidelity carries identical tamper-detection strength
  (T-04-17) while being model-class-independent — which is what lets every
  sentinel-default record extension (I.2, I.3, I.4, 15.2-08) keep its
  compatibility promise
- Migration: none — every artifact a conforming store holds verifies as
  before; pre-extension records additionally verify again

*Recorded by `15.2-08-replay-query-pins.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.record.build_replay_record

- Surface: ccf.replay.record.build_replay_record
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: dimension 1 carried no attestation
- After: signature unchanged — the new fact arrives on `execution` itself:
  `execution.source_attestation` becomes dimension 1's
  `source_attestation`, exactly as the 15.2-08 facts arrived
- Why: execution facts already travel to the builder on `ExecutionResult`;
  a new parameter would have been a second channel for the same fact
- Migration: none — executions built without the field yield the `None`
  sentinel

*Recorded by `15.2-11-attestation-enforcement.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.record.PolicyBindingsSnapshot

- Surface: ccf.replay.record.PolicyBindingsSnapshot
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: dimension 2 recorded the bindings, the HITL actions block and the
  D-10 survivor cap, and said nothing about which adapter served the read
- After: gains three sentinel-defaulted fields — `graph_adapter: str =
  NO_ADAPTER`, `graph_adapter_version: str = NO_ADAPTER` and
  `graph_secret_refs: tuple[str, ...] = ()`. `graph_secret_refs` holds
  reference **names**, stored sorted, and can hold nothing else
- Why: the bindings in force at decision time now include which adapter was
  bound and which credential references it ran under, because from D-IMPL-93
  onward that adapter can be third-party code — the same reason a policy
  bundle version is a dimension-2 fact. Sorted because declaration order is
  not a fact about the decision, and an unsorted tuple would make replay
  comparison order-sensitive on something that has no order
- Migration: none — the three defaults preserve existing records byte-for-byte,
  and a record written before the fields existed still resolves under
  D-IMPL-89's content-address-before-validation ordering

*Recorded by `15.4-08-replay.md`.*

#### ccf.boot.boot

- Surface: ccf.boot.boot
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: two gaps. A `secrets` entry was keyed by config field and its
  `ResolvedSecret.field` was never read, so a key and the record under it could
  disagree with no complaint. And a failure inside the resolved adapter's
  writer constructor propagated with the store never closed and the graph still
  marked as having a live writer, so a corrected retry over the same graph was
  refused as a second writer for the life of the process
- After: a credential whose record names a field other than the key it is
  supplied under raises `ValueError` naming both. And a boot that fails at any
  point closes the store and releases the graph before re-raising the adapter's
  exception unchanged, so a corrected retry boots
- Why: `ResolvedSecret` justifies its existence on the field and the reference
  name travelling together — a pin cannot record a reference the value did not
  come from — and nothing performed that reconciliation, so the store could
  receive a credential under one field while the replay pin recorded a
  reference resolved for another. The writer constructor is a callable an
  installed distribution supplies as of D-IMPL-93, and it sat between the
  mint guard above it and the worker guard below it, protected by neither
- Migration: none for a caller whose `ResolvedSecret.field` already equals its
  mapping key — which is every caller going through
  `ccf.deployment.load_and_boot`, since `_resolve_secret_references` writes
  both from one source. A direct `boot()` caller that built the two
  independently must make them agree; the refusal names the key and the field
  its record carries. Nothing to do for the writer-constructor guard: it turns
  a stuck process into a retryable failure and adds no new exception type

*Recorded by `15.4-13-review-fixes.md`.*

#### ccf.boot.FabricHandle.close

- Surface: ccf.boot.FabricHandle.close
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: unguarded — every call ran the shutdown closure, so
  `with handle:` around a body that also called `close()` reached the adapter's
  store twice
- After: idempotent. The second and later calls return without touching the
  store
- Why: `close()` is public *and* the class is a context manager, so two calls
  is an ordinary caller shape rather than a defect. Every in-tree store happens
  to delegate to a driver that tolerates a second close, so nothing was broken
  and nothing said so — while the out-of-tree adapter fixture's author added a
  guard of their own after reasoning about the bug it causes
- Migration: none. A caller that was closing twice was relying on adapter
  tolerance and now does not have to

*Recorded by `15.4-13-review-fixes.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.registry.BackendStore

- Surface: ccf.graph.registry.BackendStore
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `close()` carried no contract beyond its signature
- After: `close()` is documented as **required to be idempotent**
- Why: this Protocol is what a third-party adapter implements, and the
  requirement was previously discoverable only by hitting the bug — a second
  close double-decrementing a handle count and dropping a graph another handle
  was still using. A Protocol docstring exists to prevent exactly that
  discovery route
- Migration: if your store's `close()` is not safe to call twice, add a
  `_closed` guard. Note that `ccf.boot.FabricHandle.close` no longer calls it
  twice, so this is a contract you should be able to satisfy cheaply rather
  than a behaviour anything now depends on

*Recorded by `15.4-13-review-fixes.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.registry.declared_names

- Surface: ccf.graph.registry.declared_names
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: three refusals, all reachable, but two states passed through them.
  Two declarations of one adapter name were accepted as "the same code declared
  twice" whenever the distribution *names* matched — so two metadata
  directories for one distribution at different versions pointing at different
  modules were accepted, and so were two declarations with no distribution
  metadata at all, since both collapsed to the `unknown-distribution` sentinel.
  Separately, an unreadable adapter package reported an empty built-in set, and
  the refusal an operator then read told them to install a distribution. The
  refusal messages carried distribution names and their versions
- After: a duplicate is passed over only when it is provably one declaration —
  one distribution, one version, one target — and never when the declaring
  distribution could not be identified. An unreadable adapter package raises
  `RuntimeError` saying so. Every refusal message names distributions and never
  a version
- Why: the fabric was choosing an adapter by metadata iteration order in both
  accepted states, and the winner's version then landed in Decision Replay
  dimension 2 as fact — the hazard this function's fail-closed ruling exists to
  close. The version disclosure is separate: this is the listing function, the
  HTTP front door serves it with no credential under `create_app`'s documented
  default, and an uninstall names a distribution rather than a version
- Migration: an environment with a stale `dist-info` left by a failed uninstall
  now refuses instead of picking one — reinstall or remove the stale
  directory, which the refusal says. A caller that parsed a version out of a
  refusal message must stop; refusal prose was never a contract and the
  `error_code` on the HTTP refusal body is

*Recorded by `15.4-13-review-fixes.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.registry.resolve

- Surface: ccf.graph.registry.resolve
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the third-party branch called `EntryPoint.load()` bare, so a bad
  module target, a missing attribute, or anything the adapter's module-level
  body raised reached the caller untyped and in neither this function's nor
  `ccf.boot.boot`'s documented `Raises`
- After: a declaration that cannot be loaded raises `ValueError` naming the
  adapter, the declaring distribution, and the underlying failure's type and
  message — and stating that the distribution is installed and broken, which is
  a different problem from an adapter that is not installed
- Why: the built-in branch beside it already refused a missing registration
  record by name, with a test asserting no `AttributeError` escapes. The
  careful treatment was on the half the SDK wrote and the half a stranger wrote
  got none
- Migration: a caller catching `ModuleNotFoundError` or `AttributeError` around
  `resolve` should catch `ValueError` instead — which is what every caller
  refusing a bad manifest already catches. The refusal is raised outside the
  handler, so the third-party exception is not reachable on `__context__`

*Recorded by `15.4-13-review-fixes.md`.*

#### ccf.skills.catalog.SkillArgumentSchema

- Surface: ccf.skills.catalog.SkillArgumentSchema
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: a flat declaration; `to_input_schema()` rendered one level with
  `additionalProperties: false` at the root
- After: **the same two fields, unchanged** — which is why this is classified
  `behavior` rather than `reshaped`: the signature diff cannot see it, because
  the change is entirely in what `properties`' member type may now declare and
  in what the renderer emits. `to_input_schema()` renders nesting losslessly
  with `additionalProperties: false` at **every** object level, not only the
  root. `docs/schemas/ccf.skill-catalog-entry.json` and `openapi.json` both
  regenerate accordingly, in the same commit, under their bytes-equality gates
- Why: this is also the declared payload schema of a Context Product class
  (D-IMPL-97 ruling 1), reached through the `ProductPayloadSchema` alias. A
  nested object left open would be a hole in exactly the surface the widening
  creates, which is why the closed world extends downward with it
- Migration: **none.** A depth-1 declaration validates unchanged, hashes
  unchanged, and renders byte-identically — asserted directly rather than
  assumed, on the rendered surface every harness adapter consumes rather than
  on the model. All three harness adapters (`adk`, `claude_agent`,
  `langgraph`) reach this type through the one shared derivation in
  `ccf.skills.toolsurface`, so none of them changes

*Recorded by `15.5-07-contract-elements.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.engine.ReplayEnvironment

- Surface: ccf.replay.engine.ReplayEnvironment
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `rehydrate_product(*, record, projection)` rebuilt the product's
  envelope from the deployment's product class and its payload from the
  re-executed projection
- After: **the same signature** — which is why this is classified `behavior`
  rather than `reshaped`, and it is the one place this plan came in under the
  cost D-IMPL-100 authorized. The ruling priced a signature change; none was
  needed, because `record` is already a parameter and the debt is on the record.
  What changed is the **contract**: a conforming implementation must now carry
  `record.content_generation_path.debt` onto the product it returns.
  **This classification is NOT reclassified by plan `15.6-10`, and the reason is
  worth having on the record.** `D-IMPL-110` anticipated that a later phase
  would reclassify this entry once the manifest could see a Protocol's shape.
  `15.6-10` taught it to read a Protocol's `__call__` — but `ReplayEnvironment`
  defines no `__call__`; its whole contract is a method set, and `D-IMPL-122`
  descoped method-set capture on a measured cost (65 new `reshaped` diff lines
  against 11). So the manifest still shows nothing for this name, `behavior`
  remains the only honest classification available, and the reclassification
  `D-IMPL-110` anticipated needs the wider fix that has not been taken. This
  entry is one of 39 Protocols in that position
- Why: `ContextProductContract.debt` is inside the hashed body, so an
  implementation that omits it returns a product the engine's address check at
  `src/ccf/replay/engine.py:449-455` rejects with `ReplayDivergence`
- Migration: **required for any adopter with a custom `ReplayEnvironment`,
  and it is one line.** Add `debt=record.content_generation_path.debt` to the
  `ContextProductContract(...)` your `rehydrate_product` returns. All seven
  implementations across six files in this repository were updated in the same
  commit (`src/ccf/replay/engine.py`, `tests/replay/test_engine_rehydration.py`,
  `tests/replay/test_query_sequence_pin.py`, `perf/suites/replay/conftest.py`,
  `examples/06-decision-replay/run.py`,
  `examples/external/00-hello-context/run.py`).
  **Do not re-assess the debt at replay.** `D_c` is computed from observations
  and a `now` that neither the record nor the projection re-derives, so a fresh
  assessment would be a different decision wearing this one's identifier — and
  the address check would say so. An adopter who omits the line does not get a
  silent wrong answer; they get `ReplayDivergence`, which is the failure mode
  this fabric prefers

*Recorded by `15.5-11-debt-delivery.md`.*

#### ccf.projection.engine.ProjectionEngine.execute

- Surface: ccf.projection.engine.ProjectionEngine.execute
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `execute(*, plan, perspective, tenant_id, temporal_boundary,
  recorded_acquisitions=None)`. Every compiled segment query carried
  `seeds=None`, a hardcoded literal
- After: one further keyword-only parameter, `seeds: frozenset[str] | None =
  None`, threaded to **every pushable segment that carries a traverse family and
  no other**. `GQ-03` refuses seeds with no walk to start, so a segment that
  walks nothing is passed `None`; where a survivor set is also in force the two
  compose under `GQ-17` — the anchor starts a walk only if it survived, and the
  walk still may not cross outside the set
- Why: **classified `behavior` rather than `reshaped` under the `DI-15.5-06`
  precedent**, and the classification is a tooling limit rather than a judgement
  about the change: `tools/dump_surface.py` records module-level names, so a
  **method** signature is invisible to the drift gate and a `reshaped` claim
  here has no diff line to match. The 15.5-11 and 15.5-15 fragments classify
  `ReplayEnvironment.rehydrate_product` and the two resolver Protocols the same
  way for the same reason; Phase 17 should expect to reclassify all of them once
  the manifest can see methods. This is a **parameter addition** in substance.
  D-IMPL-111 limb (a). Note the rule is *every walking segment*, not *the
  opening segment*: in the shipped reference example the traverse family lands
  in a **post**-in-process segment, so an opening-segment-only rule would seed
  nothing at all. This is deliberately the **opposite** of the pitfall
  `tests/invariants/test_mcp_role_separation.py` pins this signature against —
  the anchor is *what* was asked about, never *who* asked, and
  `ProductRequest` has no role, principal or tenant field by construction
- Migration: **none.** The parameter is keyword-only with a default, so every
  existing call site is byte-for-byte unchanged in behaviour and in the queries
  it issues

*Recorded by `15.5-13-request-in-replay.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.engine.replay

- Surface: ccf.replay.engine.replay
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: re-executed the recorded plan with the recorded perspective, tenant,
  boundary and D-10 survivor cap
- After: **the same signature** — which is why this is `behavior` rather than
  `reshaped` — re-executing additionally with the seeds the recorded question
  implies, through `request_seeds(record.content_generation_path.request)`
- Why: the walk starting points are compiled into the query, so a replay under
  different seeds would ask a different question wearing the decision's
  identifier. It is the `survivor_cap` argument one line above it, applied to
  the other half of what shapes a query: replay resolves what the decision ran
  under, never what the deployment is configured with now
- Migration: **none.** A record carrying no request yields `None` and replays
  exactly as it did before the ruling. A deployment whose serving path seeds by
  a rule other than `request_seeds` will see `ReplayDivergence` on the pin
  comparison — loudly, which is the intended failure mode: the record must
  contain everything needed to reproduce the decision

*Recorded by `15.5-13-request-in-replay.md`.*

#### ccf.products.request — module import order

- Surface: ccf.products.request
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `ccf.products.payload` and `ccf.skills.catalog` were imported at the
  top of the module
- After: both are imported at the **bottom**, on the D-IMPL-56 pattern
  `ccf.skills.catalog` itself documents, with an explicit
  `ProductRequestContract.model_rebuild()`. `ccf.replay.record` defers its
  import of this module the same way
- Why: limb (b) makes `ccf.replay.record` name `ProductRequest`, and
  `ccf.skills.catalog` reaches `ccf.agents.agent`, which reads
  `NO_GRANTS_SET_HASH` and `PerspectiveConditioning` back out of
  `ccf.replay.record`. With the imports at the top, first-import order would
  decide whether the SDK imports at all. Deferring them makes `ProductRequest`
  exist before anything here can reach the replay plane, so the cycle closes in
  every order — asserted by importing each of the four modules first in turn
- Migration: **none.** Every name this module exported still exports from it,
  and no import statement anywhere else changes

*Recorded by `15.5-13-request-in-replay.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.http_api.routes.product — `GET /{tenant}/{product_class}/{version}`

- Surface: ccf_runtime.http_api.routes.product.build_router
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: four query-free path parameters plus optional `?role=`; served
  whatever the resolver returned, with entitlement enforcement **inert** when
  the seams were unbound or no grant was present
- After: four new optional query parameters — `subject`, `intent`, `freshness`,
  `asked_at` — and the enforcement is no longer inert. The route refuses
  (503, `product-door-ungoverned`) when it cannot produce an authorized
  perspective, and refuses (400, `product-request-incomplete`) when a caller
  supplies some but not all four address axes
- Why: two reasons, one per half. The refusal is D-IMPL-108 (above). The query
  axes are whitepaper §6.3 **Addressability**
  (`docs/CCF_Whitepaper_1-2.md:1465`), whose worked pattern is literally
  `/context-products/credit-risk/counterparty/{counterparty-id}?intent=…&freshness=…`
  — path for *what*, query for *what is being asked of it*. This is the one
  door with a query surface, so it is the one door that can carry a request,
  which is where `15.5-08`'s must-have lands
- Migration: an existing caller that passes none of the four query parameters
  sees no change to the request it sends; what changes is whether the door it
  reaches is governed. `asked_at` is ISO-8601 and is the caller's instant —
  the door reads no clock, so replay feeds the *recorded* boundary through the
  same code path

*Recorded by `15.5-15-resolver-seam.md`.*

#### ccf.operators.planner.IntermediateCache

- Surface: ccf.operators.planner.IntermediateCache
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `run` built the key `(operator.key, content_hash(operator.parameters), content_hash(view), content_hash(upstream))` on **every** invocation, then probed the memo with it. On the shipped engine path every one of those keys was built for a lookup that could not hit
- After: the same four components, in the same order, built **only when a lookup could hit** — that is, when the cache already holds an intermediate for the same `operator.key`, which is the key tuple's first component and therefore a necessary condition for any match. An intermediate produced for an operator this cache has not seen is held un-keyed until a second call for that operator makes a key necessary. The value returned, and the `hits` accounting, are unchanged
- Why: `D-IMPL-36` rules the key's **composition** — *"a deterministic key, so a cache hit is by construction the same value replay would produce"* — and says nothing about **when** the key is computed. So what narrows here is the guarantee's *witness*, not the guarantee. Until now the property *"the key is a total function of everything `Operator.apply` can observe"* was witnessed by execution on every call; it is now true by construction and witnessed by `tests/operators/test_planner.py`, whose two cache tests exercise the reachable path — a repeat invocation that hits, and two distinct upstream chains that must not collapse. Both pass unmodified, as do `tests/properties/test_composition.py` and `tests/conformance/test_similarity_index.py`, which construct `compose(..., cache=IntermediateCache())` and are the composition-preservation suites this change sits closest to. The instrumented unreachability probe reports the identical figures before and after — 192 engine-created caches, 640 `run` calls through them, 0 hits — so control flow did not move either. One consequence follows from the same narrowing and is stated rather than left to be discovered: a view with no canonical rendering (`ccf.contracts.canonical::CanonicalizationError`) used to surface on its first pass through this cache and now surfaces only where the key is actually built
- Migration: **none.** No signature moves, no constructor argument appears, the key's composition is byte-for-byte what it was, and a hit still returns the value recomputation would produce with its provenance. An adopter constructing an `IntermediateCache` — directly or through `ccf.operators.planner::compose` — changes nothing and observes the same values and the same `hits` count. What an adopter may observe is that the fabric got faster: `perf/suites/micro/test_operators.py::test_composed_chain` moved from a 0.386391 s median to 0.178888 s on the `local` runner class, and `test_composed_ab` from 0.191451 s to 0.077345 s, against a `perf/baselines/local.json` this plan left byte-identical

*Recorded by `15.6-02-intermediate-cache-key.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.record.build_replay_record

- Surface: ccf.replay.record.build_replay_record
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the record's dimension 5 said nothing about what a debt pointed at
- After: **the same signature.** Dimension 5's `debt_evidence` is *derived from
  the `product` argument the builder was already handed* — no new parameter
- Why: derivation rather than threading is what keeps every
  `build_replay_record` consumer byte-for-byte unchanged, and it makes a record
  pinning evidence its product does not carry **unrepresentable** rather than
  merely discouraged. It is exactly the rule 15.5-11 applied to `debt`: a fact
  already present is read, never re-passed
- Migration: **none.** No call site changes. A caller that wants evidence on the
  record puts it on the product

*Recorded by `15.6-06-debt-evidence.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.engine.ReplayEnvironment

- Surface: ccf.replay.engine.ReplayEnvironment
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: a conforming `rehydrate_product(*, record, projection)` had to carry
  `record.content_generation_path.debt` onto the product it returned (15.5-11)
- After: **the same signature** — which is why this is `behavior` rather than
  `reshaped`. What changed is the **contract**: it must now *also* carry
  `record.content_generation_path.debt_evidence`
- Why: `ContextProductContract.debt_evidence` is inside the hashed body, and it
  is **not** reachable through `debt` — the ruled placement made it a sibling of
  the assessment, not a member. `canonical_bytes` walks a model field-by-field
  over `type(value).model_fields`, so an implementation that omits it returns a
  product `ccf.replay.engine::replay`'s address check rejects with
  `ReplayDivergence`
- Migration: **required for any adopter with a custom `ReplayEnvironment`, even
  if you never populate the field, and it is one line.** Add
  `debt_evidence=record.content_generation_path.debt_evidence` to the
  `ContextProductContract(...)` your `rehydrate_product` returns. All seven
  implementations across six files in this repository were updated in the same
  commit (`src/ccf/replay/engine.py`, `tests/replay/test_engine_rehydration.py`,
  `tests/replay/test_query_sequence_pin.py`, `perf/suites/replay/conftest.py`,
  `examples/06-decision-replay/run.py`,
  `examples/external/00-hello-context/run.py`). **Do not re-derive the pointers
  at replay** — they are a deployment's judgement about a particular assembly,
  not a function of the record, so a rehydrator that rebuilt them would be
  returning a different decision wearing this one's identifier. An adopter who
  omits the line does not get a silent wrong answer; they get
  `ReplayDivergence`, which is the failure mode this fabric prefers

*Recorded by `15.6-06-debt-evidence.md`.*

  `tests/replay/test_engine_rehydration.py`,
  `tests/replay/test_query_sequence_pin.py`, `perf/suites/replay/conftest.py`,
  `examples/06-decision-replay/run.py`,
  `examples/external/00-hello-context/run.py`). **Do not resolve the obligation
  from your current `DebtProfile`** — that is the one substitution this field
  exists to make impossible, and it is the failure a plausible-looking
  implementation will produce. An adopter who omits the line does not get a
  silent wrong answer; they get `ReplayDivergence`, which is the failure mode
  this fabric prefers

*Recorded by `15.6-08-obligation-binding.md`.*

#### ccf.halflife.decay.NO_SUBJECT_IDENTITY

- Surface: ccf.halflife.decay.NO_SUBJECT_IDENTITY
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the marker string `"no-subject-identity"` — un-namespaced — and
  `Observation` carried **no validator refusing it as a declared subject**. The
  panel confirmed by execution that
  `Observation(..., subject=NO_SUBJECT_IDENTITY).subject` was indistinguishable
  from an omitted subject
- After: the marker string `"ccf.no-subject-identity"`, SDK-namespaced in
  `ccf.halflife.assessment.NO_OBLIGATION_IN_FORCE`'s style, plus a validator on
  `Observation.subject` that refuses **a blank or whitespace-only subject** and
  refuses **any subject inside the reserved `ccf.` namespace other than the
  sentinel itself**
- Why: `D-IMPL-120` ruled the identity optional and ruled out expressing its
  absence as `None`, in its own words because *"an omitted identity and a
  dropped one are not the same artifact."* A deployment whose real opaque
  subject happened to be spelled `no-subject-identity` produced exactly the
  artifact that ruling forbids — a fidelity defect against a ruling made in this
  same phase. Namespacing alone makes an accidental collision unlikely;
  **reserving the namespace makes impersonation unavailable**, which is the
  property that was missing. The blank refusal closes the half-finished-edit
  case, for the reason `SignalObligation.obligation_ref` already states
- Migration: **none** for a deployment that populates `subject` with its own
  identifiers, and none for one that omits it — the default moved, but a
  defaulted `Observation` is still a defaulted `Observation` and still says
  exactly what it always said. The one behaviour to know about: `subject` values
  beginning `ccf.` are now refused, and the previously-sentinel spelling
  `"no-subject-identity"` is now an **ordinary deployment string** carrying no
  SDK meaning, admitted verbatim. A deployment that hardcoded that literal to
  mean *absent* must import `NO_SUBJECT_IDENTITY` or omit the field — it will
  not fail, it will simply be saying something else. **No product content
  address moves**, measured rather than assumed: `Observation` is an input to
  debt computation and not a member of `ContextProductContract`, and
  `examples/08-e2e-demo`'s pinned replay address is byte-identical across the
  change at `75365e18f4a1a675…`

*Recorded by `15.6-12-close-panel-refusals.md`.*

#### ccf.halflife.assessment.SourceRefreshWindow

- Surface: ccf.halflife.assessment.SourceRefreshWindow
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: both members accepted an `int`, a `float`, a `Decimal` or a numeric
  string as the instant. Pydantic's lax mode turned each into a timezone-aware
  UTC `datetime` **before** the class's `_the_instant_names_its_zone` validator
  ran, so a naive ISO string was refused for carrying an *implicit* zone while
  `1767225600` — which carries none at all — was accepted with UTC supplied by
  the framework. `1767225600.5` therefore became a hashed instant in the module
  whose headline discipline is *Decimal, never float*
- After: a `mode="before"` validator on both members refusing a `float` through
  the module's own `_refuse_float` — the same helper wired onto `DebtFactors`,
  `DebtEvidencePointer.handle`, `SignalObligation.obligation_ref` and
  `DebtAssessment.debt` — and refusing any bare number, in any spelling, as an
  instant that named no zone
- Why: this value is inside the delivered product's content address
  (`D-IMPL-118`), so a zone the SDK supplied rather than the deployment
  declared is a silent default (`D-IMPL-38`) that reproduces exactly, forever
- Migration: **none** for a deployment supplying a timezone-aware `datetime` or
  a string carrying an explicit offset — the two spellings
  `examples/s1-01-hello-context-product` uses, both asserted. A deployment
  passing an epoch must name the zone: `datetime.fromtimestamp(ts, tz=UTC)`

*Recorded by `15.6-12-single-agent-review-fixes.md`.*

#### ccf.halflife.decay.Observation

- Surface: ccf.halflife.decay.Observation
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `observed_at` had **no validator**, while this class's own attribute
  docstring said *"Timezone-aware; a naive instant is rejected"*. A naive
  observation was constructible, serialisable and storable, and the failure
  arrived at whatever call site first computed decay. `subject`'s namespace
  reservation compared the raw string, so `" ccf.no-subject-identity"`,
  the non-breaking-space spelling and `"CCF.no-subject-identity"` all walked
  past a reservation whose entire purpose is that a reader cannot tell them
  from the marker
- After: `observed_at` refuses a naive instant and a bare epoch at
  construction, which is where the docstring always said the refusal was;
  `subject`'s reservation reads the value as a reader sees it — NFKC, Unicode
  `Cf` format characters dropped, stripped and case-folded
- Why: CLAUDE.md §11 — validation is at the boundary, never deferred; and
  `D-IMPL-120`'s *"an omitted identity and a dropped one are not the same
  artifact"* is not held by a check a leading space defeats
- Migration: a deployment constructing `Observation` with a naive `datetime`
  now fails at construction rather than at first decay computation — the same
  failure, moved to the boundary. A deployment whose subjects begin `ccf.` in
  any rendering was already refused for the unspaced spelling and is now
  refused for the rest. Script confusables are **not** folded and are admitted;
  the limit is documented at the helper and asserted

*Recorded by `15.6-12-single-agent-review-fixes.md`.*

#### ccf.halflife.assessment.DebtEvidencePointer

- Surface: ccf.halflife.assessment.DebtEvidencePointer
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `handle='ccf.fabricated-marker'` was accepted
- After: refused. The field has no sentinel of its own — an assessment that
  points at nothing carries the empty `NO_DEBT_EVIDENCE` tuple — so the whole
  `ccf.` namespace is reserved with nothing carved out of it
- Why: the close-panel's B1 reserved the namespace on `Observation.subject` on
  the argument that a deployment must not be able to write a value that
  impersonates a fabric marker. Three sibling opaque strings shipped in the same
  phase, all inside the same content address, none of them reserved. A property
  worth enforcing on one field of four is either a rule or a coincidence
- Migration: none unless a deployment's evidence handles begin `ccf.`

*Recorded by `15.6-12-single-agent-review-fixes.md`.*

#### ccf.halflife.assessment.SignalObligation

- Surface: ccf.halflife.assessment.SignalObligation
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `obligation_ref` refused the **exact** sentinel string only, so
  `" ccf.no-obligation-in-force"` was declarable in a profile and travelled to
  the vessel through `DebtProfile.obligation_for` unchanged
- After: the whole `ccf.` namespace is refused, with nothing carved out — a
  binding is not a carrier and has no sentinel of its own
- Why: as above; the exact-match refusal held the distinction against one
  spelling of the impersonation and not the others

#### ccf.halflife.assessment.ObligationInForce

- Surface: ccf.halflife.assessment.ObligationInForce
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the alias `15.6-12`'s first round added refused a blank and admitted
  everything else, including `ccf.`-namespaced values that render identically to
  the marker asserting that no obligation was bound
- After: the sentinel `NO_OBLIGATION_IN_FORCE` is admitted, compared **exactly**
  because it is this field's own default; nothing else in the namespace is
- Why: one string, two positions, two rules — and both rules exist to keep the
  same two claims apart. That argument only holds if neither position can be
