#### ccf.replay.engine.ReplayEnvironment

- Surface: ccf.replay.engine.ReplayEnvironment
- Public surface: true
- Before: `rehydrate_product`'s contract required the per-source tuple to be
  read off `record.content_generation_path.source_refresh_recency` and carried
  onto the rebuilt product verbatim
- After: **the same signature**, and the same requirement over the reshaped
  value. A conforming implementation copies the window exactly as it copied the
  map
- Change type: behavior
- Why: the Protocol's contract text is where the read-back obligation is
  stated, and the obligation's *subject* changed type even though its
  *signature* did not — which is the class of change a signature diff cannot
  see, and the reason the `behavior` change type exists
- Migration: **none — the read-back line is unchanged in spelling** and is
  required exactly as before. Its necessity was re-measured against the
  reshaped fixture in both directions: deleting it over a sentinel-carrying
  fixture costs nothing (0 red), and over a populated window it turns 13 of
  `tests/replay/test_engine_rehydration.py`'s 44 cases red with
  `ReplayDivergence`. Both figures are recorded in a comment at the read-back
  line, because reporting only the populated one would hide the reason the
  measurement is taken twice

*Recorded by `15.6-13-source-refresh-window.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.mcp.egress — the `request_context_product` tool

- Surface: ccf_runtime.mcp.egress.build_egress_server
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the handler took seven arguments — three address segments plus
  whitepaper §6.3's four axes — and hard-wired `parameters={}` at
  `mcp/egress.py::build_egress_server`. The measured consequence, recorded in
  the handler's own
  docstring: *a product class whose request contract declares **any** required
  parameter is not reachable through this tool*, and a resolver discharging its
  `refuse_request` obligation refused every such call
  `missing-required-parameter`
- After: an eighth argument, `parameters: dict[str, Any] | None = None`,
  forwarded verbatim to `ProductRequest.parameters`. **Optional, not required** —
  the seven above are the *address* and stay required together so a partial
  address remains unrepresentable, while the declared remainder is not part of
  the address. Omitting it is the empty mapping, which says *this question needs
  nothing beyond its four axes*
- Why: `D-IMPL-133`. The half of the recorded objection that said a *"flat
  tool-argument map cannot carry `D-IMPL-103`'s declared nested types without
  the door guessing at coercions"* is **false as measured** — an MCP tool
  argument map is JSON, not flat text, so a presented `3` arrives an `int`, a
  presented `"3"` arrives a `str`, and one level of declared nesting crosses
  intact. Nothing is left to guess at. The half that said a declared-parameter
  surface *"would make this tool wider than the HTTP door"* is still binding and
  is honoured by repairing **both** doors, not neither
- Migration: none. The argument is optional and omitting it reproduces the
  previous behaviour exactly. A deployment whose product class declares no
  required parameter is unaffected; one whose class declares a required
  parameter becomes reachable through this tool for the first time

*Recorded by `17-10-both-doors-carry-parameters.md`.*

#### GET /product/{tenant}/{product_class}/{version} — `?parameters=`

- Surface: ccf_runtime.http_api.routes.product.build_router
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the route took `role`, `subject`, `intent`, `freshness` and
  `asked_at`, and hard-wired `parameters={}` in
  `http_api/routes/product.py::build_router` for the
  identical recorded reason. Those two sites were the **only** two in `runtime/`
  constructing a `ProductRequest` for a door
- After: an optional `parameters` query parameter carrying **one JSON object**.
  The door parses it and carries what it found; it inspects nothing inside it.
  Text that is not JSON, or JSON that is not an object, is refused 400 with the
  new `ERROR_PRODUCT_PARAMETERS_MALFORMED` code. Supplying `parameters` with
  none of the four §6.3 axes is refused 400 with the existing
  `ERROR_PRODUCT_REQUEST_INCOMPLETE`: the declared remainder qualifies a
  question, and there is no question to qualify
- Why: `D-IMPL-133`'s *"both, not one"*. **JSON text in one parameter rather
  than flattened query keys**, and that shape is the whole of how the recorded
  objection is honoured rather than overridden: a flat, string-valued query
  genuinely cannot carry declared types or nesting without the door guessing at
  a coercion, whereas JSON is self-describing and carries both. It is also what
  makes the two doors exactly as wide as each other, which is the property
  `D-IMPL-133` gives as the reason for repairing both
- Migration: none — a new optional query parameter. Existing callers are
  byte-identical in behaviour. `openapi.json` regains one parameter entry and
  is regenerated under the committed-drift gate

*Recorded by `17-10-both-doors-carry-parameters.md`.*

#### ccf.client.wire.WireRequest — `temporal_boundary` must be timezone-aware

- Surface: ccf.client.wire.WireRequest
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: any `datetime` was accepted, naive included, and the failure surfaced
  differently on every transport. Measured on the two commands
  `examples/hw1-companies-over-revenue/README.md` documents, with the offset
  stripped off `--as-of`: `inprocess:` raised
  `ccf.contracts.canonical.CanonicalizationError` out of the SQLite graph
  backend, and `mcp+stdio:` raised `ccf.client.errors.TransportError` because the
  far door refused the naive `asked_at` by name and a *raised* door refusal
  reaches a client as a wire error
- After: a field validator refuses a naive instant at construction, so
  `ProductClient.ask` raises `pydantic.ValidationError` before any transport is
  reached and the failure is identical on every binding. `None` is unchanged and
  still means *defer to the server's decision instant* (`D-04`); any UTC offset
  is accepted, not only `+00:00` — `D-IMPL-38` refuses an *ambiguous* instant,
  not a non-UTC one
- Why: `D-IMPL-151` finding `B0b`. The `mcp+stdio` path reported a **transport
  failure for a caller's malformed argument**, which is `D-10`'s inversion: the
  natural response to a `TransportError` is a retry, and this question can never
  succeed. The guard for the hazard already existed one field over at
  `WireRequest._parameters_survive_the_wire`; `temporal_boundary` had no
  equivalent
- Migration: pass an aware instant — `datetime(..., tzinfo=UTC)`,
  `value.replace(tzinfo=UTC)`, or an ISO 8601 string carrying an offset. The
  refusal message names all three. A caller already passing an aware instant or
  `None` is unaffected; a caller passing a naive one was already failing, and now
  fails at the seam with a message that names the repair

*Recorded by `18-99-close-review-blocking-fixes.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.mcp.egress — the product tool refuses `"parameters": null`

- Surface: ccf_runtime.mcp.egress.build_egress_server
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the tool argument was `parameters: dict[str, Any] | None = None` and
  the handler read `dict(parameters or {})`, so a supplied JSON `null` arrived as
  `None`, was defaulted to the empty mapping and reached the resolver — while the
  HTTP route refused the identical payload `400
  ERROR_PRODUCT_PARAMETERS_MALFORMED`
- After: the argument is `parameters: dict[str, Any] = EMPTY_PARAMETERS` and the
  handler reads `dict(parameters)`. The generated tool schema is `{"type":
  "object", "default": {}}`, so an **omitted** argument still arrives as the
  empty mapping and a supplied `null` is refused by the argument model before the
  handler runs — the same place a supplied array or string was already refused
- Why: `D-IMPL-151` finding `B2`. `or {}` was a default under a docstring
  stating the handler *"coerces nothing, defaults nothing and inspects
  nothing"*, and the result was a width difference `D-IMPL-133` forbids. The
  HTTP route's refusal is the correct half: the empty mapping is the *positive*
  statement `this question needs nothing beyond its four axes`, so reading a
  supplied `null` as that statement invents an assertion the caller never made
  — `D-08` — on the argument the resolver selects a plan from. Narrowing MCP
  satisfies "no wider than the other door" in the direction that does not add a
  silent default
- Migration: a client that omitted `parameters` is unaffected. A client that
  sent `"parameters": null` explicitly must omit the argument or send `{}`; both
  reproduce the previous behaviour exactly. The shipped
  `ccf.client.adapters.mcp_stdio` adapter always sends a mapping and is
  unaffected

*Recorded by `18-99-close-review-blocking-fixes.md`.*

#### ccf.client.codec — the integrity checks' claims corrected, not their behaviour

- Surface: ccf.client.codec
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the module docstring argued that the echo is *cross-checked, never
  reconstructed*, and both `verify_content_address` and `check_outcome`
  documented a `TransportError` for a transport that answered a different
  question or altered a vessel in transit
- After: the argument is kept whole and the measured state of the code is
  recorded beside it: `DoorOutcome` carries no request identity, so both shipped
  adapters fill `WireOutcome.request` from the request object the client also
  passes as `sent`, making `check_outcome`'s comparison an object against itself;
  and the "claimed" address is computed by the adapter from the same
  `product_json` it emits, making `verify_content_address` `sha256(x) !=
  sha256(x)`. Both `Raises:` blocks now say which failures are reachable from a
  shipped adapter and which are not. **No behaviour changed.** The same
  correction is carried on `WireOutcome.request` and `.content_address`, which
  point a reader at exactly this argument
- Why: `D-IMPL-151` finding `B0`, and Dave's instruction — *"Do not claim the
  current checks provide that protection."* The fix requires a wire-shape change
  across both doors, the adapter and the codec, and is deferred to its own phase;
  the false claim is not deferred with it
- Migration: **until that phase lands, this SDK must not be described as
  detecting a transport that answered a different question, or as detecting a
  vessel altered in transit.** No code change is required of an adopter; what
  changes is what may be claimed of the seam

*Recorded by `18-99-close-review-blocking-fixes.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.OPTIONAL_KEYS

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.OPTIONAL_KEYS
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `("connection",)` — one member. The strict unknown-key refusal
  enumerated `connection` as the whole of what a document may optionally state
- After: `("connection", "server")`. The enumeration in the unknown-key refusal
  grew with it, so a document misspelling `server:` is now told that `server` is
  a key it could have meant rather than being told nothing
- Why: the tuple is the single register of what a `binding.yaml` may say, and
  the refusal message is generated from it rather than written beside it —
  adding the key anywhere else would have left the refusal describing a document
  format that no longer exists
- Migration: none — additive. A document that stated no `server:` loads exactly
  as before

*Recorded by `18.1-04-the-deployment-declares-its-server.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.load_binding_document

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.binding_document.load_binding_document
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: no `server:` value existed to refuse. Once the key shipped with its
  base at the deployment root's parent, containment passed for a value reaching
  back **inside** the root — `server: deployment/anything.py` resolved, was
  contained, and loaded
- After: a value resolving inside the deployment root is refused **by name**, in
  the `RULED_OUT_KEYS` register's voice: the message says the value resolves
  inside the deployment root, that `D-IMPL-141` Amendment A.3 grants that
  directory **one** Python module (its projection), that a server program is not
  deployment content, and that the remedy is to name it beside the deployment
  rather than inside it
- Why: `D-18` (Dave, 2026-08-18), and it is **the one piece of new mechanism
  this phase ships** — deliberately, because it makes `D-09`'s argument
  mechanical rather than documentary. The base one level out is correct for the
  ordinary case and opens a hole on the way: a third-party deployment could
  quietly hold two Python modules where Amendment A.3 grants one. Without this,
  the only thing holding that line was
  `assert document.server.parent != root` in `test_hw1_two_transports`, which
  covers the shipped example and no adopter. Note it is a **value** refusal, not
  a key refusal: `"server"` is in `OPTIONAL_KEYS` and deliberately **not** in
  `RULED_OUT_KEYS`, because a deployment declaring an outward `server:` is
  legitimate and `hw1` does exactly that
- Migration: none, unless a deployment already declared a `server:` value
  resolving inside its own root — impossible before this phase, since the key
  did not exist. Such a value must be moved beside the deployment root and the
  declaration repointed at it

*Recorded by `18.1-04-the-deployment-declares-its-server.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.mcp_stdio.McpStdioAdapter

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.mcp_stdio.McpStdioAdapter
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `mcp+stdio:<program> <root>` — **two** tokens, the server program then
  the deployment root, neither inferable from the other, parsed with
  `shlex.split`. The client's own binding string named the program it was about
  to spawn
- After: `mcp+stdio:<root>` — **one** token, the deployment root. The program is
  read from that deployment's `server:` key. Three ordinary authoring mistakes
  are refused by name rather than half-resolved: a deployment declaring no
  `server:` (the message names the transport and the key, since the requirement
  belongs to this transport and not to the document), the pre-migration
  two-token address (the message names the migration — drop the program, it is
  the deployment's `server:` key now), and a single token that resolves to a
  file rather than a directory (the message says it names a program, and that an
  `mcp+stdio` address names the deployment root that declares the program). A
  fourth refusal, for a declared program that is not on disk, moved here from
  the loader
- Why: `D-05`, and the phase's reason for existing (Dave, 2026-08-18): *"the
  client is not responsible for setting up the MCP server, yet when the MCP
  example is called the client code references the server.py."* The spawn is
  inherent to stdio and is not the defect; the defect was that the spawn was the
  *client's* business in the reader's field of view. Where the program sits is
  the deployment's business, so the deployment is where it is declared
- Migration: replace the two-token address with the deployment root alone, and
  declare `server:` in that deployment's `binding.yaml` — a path relative to the
  directory holding the deployment. The two-token form is **replaced**, not kept
  alongside, and an address still stating two tokens gets the migration message
  rather than a parse failure; that branch is permanent grammar, not a
  time-limited shim

*Recorded by `18.1-04-the-deployment-declares-its-server.md`.*

#### ccf.products.door.DoorOutcome — two required members, five validator rules

- Surface: ccf.products.door.DoorOutcome
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: three members — `product_json`, `replay_ref`, `refusal` — and a
  three-rule validator. `ccf.client.wire.WireOutcome` was a **separate class**
  with five members and a four-rule validator, and the two were kept in step by
  hand across a transcoding step in each adapter
- After: one class with exactly five members, in `WireOutcome`'s declaration
  order — `request`, `product_json`, `content_address`, `replay_ref`,
  `refusal`. `ccf.client.wire.WireOutcome` is an **alias**, so
  `ccf.client.wire.WireOutcome is ccf.products.door.DoorOutcome` is true. The
  merged `_exactly_one_half` carries **five** rules, not four: the two
  originals raised seven times between them and exactly two of those were the
  same rule in different words. The rule with no counterpart on the other side
  — *a refused outcome carrying a content address* — had no coverage anywhere
  in the tree and now has its own assertion
- Why: `C-06`. A door must emit precisely what a client decodes, because the
  transcoding step between two shapes is exactly where an adapter re-sources
  the echo from client state — which is what `B0` measured on both shipped
  adapters. `request` is required on **both** halves (`C-04`), so one
  comparison covers a served answer and a refusal: a crossed refusal is a
  governed "no" attributed to a question nobody asked, and must be as
  detectable as a crossed vessel
- Migration: a caller constructing a `DoorOutcome` directly must now supply
  `request` and `content_address`. In-tree there is no such caller left —
  `ccf_runtime.mcp.egress` constructs none. `DoorOutcome.model_validate_json`
  against bytes emitted by a pre-Phase-19 door fails, by design: the two new
  members are required, and an optional member with a reconstruction fallback
  is the bypass this phase exists to close

*Recorded by `19-01-one-outcome-shape-and-the-encoded-question.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.mcp.egress — `request_context_product` gains a **required** argument

- Surface: ccf_runtime.mcp.egress.build_egress_server
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: eight tool arguments, seven required. The handler resolved parameters
  from the `parameters` argument and constructed `DoorOutcome` at two sites
- After: nine arguments, eight required. `request` carries the UTF-8 text of
  `canonical_bytes` over the caller's `WireRequest`; `door_question` parses it,
  and the outcome echoes **what was parsed** rather than the address arguments
  beside it. The handler then refuses a call that asks two things, by name, on
  every axis where a second copy of the fact exists: `product_class` against
  the encoded `product_id`, `parameters` against the encoded declared
  remainder, and — **guarded on `temporal_boundary is not None`** — `asked_at`
  against the encoded boundary. The answer resolves from the **decoded**
  request's parameters, so there is one source for what is answered and the
  echo is that same source. Both `DoorOutcome(` construction sites are replaced
  by one `door_answer` call
- Why: `C-16` and `C-18`. Required rather than optional on the tool
  docstring's own argument for the four §6.3 axes — required-together makes a
  partial address unrepresentable rather than merely refused — and because an
  optional argument with a reconstruction fallback is precisely the bypass this
  phase closes. The cross-check exists because two independent sources for one
  fact is the bypass one level up: a caller could encode question A, address
  question B, and have A echoed while B is answered. `C-18` extends that to the
  temporal axis because `C-15` keeps the clock client-side, so a **concrete**
  `as_of` crosses the pipe twice
  `as_of=None`, the client resolved the instant and there is **no second copy**
  to compare, so a transport altering `asked_at` alone is **undetected**. No
  fallback, tolerance window or reconstruction is added — a reconstructed
  operand is the bypass this phase exists to shut. The residual belongs in
  `C-10`'s named undetected list and in the phase's `D-IMPL` entry
- Migration: **a wire-compatibility break with no deprecation window** (`C-16`;
  pre-1.0). Any non-SDK consumer calling `request_context_product` must add
  `request`, carrying `canonical_bytes` of a `WireRequest` whose `product_id`
  and `parameters` agree with the address arguments beside it, and whose
  `temporal_boundary` is either `None` or exactly the `asked_at` being sent.
  The shipped `ccf.client.adapters.mcp_stdio` adapter sends it and is
  unaffected

*Recorded by `19-01-one-outcome-shape-and-the-encoded-question.md`.*

#### ccf.client.wire — the two shapes are re-exports now

- Surface: ccf.client.wire
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `WireRequest` and `WireOutcome` were defined here
- After: both are imported from `ccf.products.door`, and `WireOutcome` is a
  module-level alias rather than a subclass. `__all__` is unchanged, and the
  module docstring gains a section recording the collapse and its direction —
  appended, with every existing paragraph kept
- Why: `C-06`, plus the layering constraint above
- Migration: none for an importer. `isinstance` against either name admits the
  other, which is the point of an alias rather than a subclass

*Recorded by `19-01-one-outcome-shape-and-the-encoded-question.md`.*

#### ccf.client.wire.TransportAdapter — the seam returns bytes

- Surface: ccf.client.wire.TransportAdapter
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `def ask(self, request: WireRequest) -> WireOutcome` — an adapter
  constructed the outcome object the client then re-checked. `D-11` permitted
  it, and that permission is what let both shipped adapters set
  `outcome.request` from the request they were handed while
  `ProductClient.ask` passed that same object as `sent`, making
  `outcome.request != sent` an object compared with itself
- After: `def ask(self, request: WireRequest) -> bytes`. The request stays
  typed because an adapter genuinely needs its fields to build a tool call or
  a URL; only the answer becomes bytes
- Why: `C-05`, `C-08`. The object return is removed **by type change** rather
  than by convention, so an adapter has nothing to construct and therefore no
  way to source the echo from client state. `mypy --strict` binds this over
  `src` and `runtime`; an out-of-tree adapter is reached by `19-05`'s AST gate
  (`C-17`), because a type change is invisible to code the type checker never
  reads
- Migration: **a source break with no deprecation window** (pre-1.0). An
  out-of-tree adapter — which can only exist by a third party assigning into
  `SCHEME_REGISTRY`, since `D-05` forbids plugin discovery — returns
  `encode_outcome(outcome)` where it returned `outcome`, or hands the
  producer's bytes through unexamined, which is the better shape. The two
  shipped adapters are updated in this plan

*Recorded by `19-02-the-adapter-seam-carries-bytes.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.mcp_stdio.McpStdioAdapter — `_decode` is gone

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.mcp_stdio.McpStdioAdapter
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `ask` returned the door's text through a private `_decode` that
  parsed the `DoorOutcome`, re-built a `WireOutcome` with `request=request`
  from its own argument, and recomputed the content address from the very
  text it passed on
- After: `ask` returns
  `asyncio.run(self._call(...)).encode("utf-8")`. `_decode` is deleted
  entirely, along with the module's now-unused `hashlib`, `ValidationError`,
  `WireOutcome` and `DoorOutcome` imports. `_call` is unchanged
- Why: `C-06`. The point is the **absence of a transcoding step** — a
  transcoding step is exactly where an adapter can re-source the echo from
  client state. The wire between the MCP door and this adapter stays text
  because raw bytes cannot be an MCP tool result without base64, and a base64
  envelope would be the second rendering of the vessel that `ccf.client.wire`
  rejects by ruling; encoding text this adapter never inspects is a
  pass-through, not a transcoding
  raised a `TransportError` naming the scheme and the program for malformed
  bytes. `decode_outcome` raises the same diagnosis in the same words without
  that context, and the generic message is taken. A thin adapter-level
  well-formedness check would parse and discard — not a transcoding step, but
  a place a future adapter grows one
- Migration: none for a caller. `_decode` was private

*Recorded by `19-02-the-adapter-seam-carries-bytes.md`.*

#### ccf.client.adapters.inprocess.InProcessAdapter — a wire in both directions

- Surface: ccf.client.adapters.inprocess.InProcessAdapter
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `ask` built a `ProductRequest` from the `WireRequest` **object** it
  was handed, called the resolver, and passed the result to a private
  `_encode` that built a `WireOutcome` with `request=request` — the same
  object. The answer half was a degenerate wire (`D-13`); the question half
  was not one at all
- After: `ask` is the **client** half: it boots, reads the clock exactly where
  it read it before, encodes the question with
  `canonical_bytes(request).decode("utf-8")`, and hands *text* to a new
  private `_door`. `_door` is the **producer** half: it parses that text with
  `ccf.products.door.door_question`, builds the `ProductRequest` from what it
  parsed, calls the resolver, and emits through `ccf.products.door.door_answer`
  — the one emission site, which serializes the vessel once and derives the
  content address from those same bytes. `_encode` is deleted
- Why: `C-07`. Without it the fix would be cosmetic on the transport every
  quickstart, fixture and dev path uses: two halves sharing one object make
  the echo cross-check an object compared with itself, which is `B0` surviving
  in-process while being closed over the wire. The door half takes **text and
  never a `WireRequest`**, for the reason `door_question` already states
  accepted with no benchmark owed (`C-14`). `D-13` already accepted *"a
  microsecond of serialization"* on the answer half, and the question is far
  smaller than the answer
- Migration: none for a caller. Both `_encode` and `_door` are private.
  `TransportError` is no longer imported: `_encode`'s wrong-shape raise is
  gone, and `served_answer` inside `door_answer` raises `TypeError` for a
  resolver answering with neither shape — the same diagnosis in the right
  place. No in-tree test asserted the old wording

*Recorded by `19-02-the-adapter-seam-carries-bytes.md`.*

#### ccf.client.client.ProductClient — one decode, no second check

- Surface: ccf.client.client.ProductClient
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `ask` returned `self._result_from(self._adapter.ask(request),
  sent=request)`, and `_result_from` ran `check_outcome` on the object an
  adapter had built
- After: `ask` returns
  `self._result_from(decode_outcome(self._adapter.ask(request), sent=request),
  sent=request)`. `_result_from` takes a `DecodedOutcome`, reads the six
  echo/address/replay fields off `.outcome` and the vessel off `.vessel`, and
  keeps its **one** `Result` construction. Its `check_outcome` call and the
  module's `check_outcome` import are both gone — running the checks twice
  would hash the vessel twice and validate the contract twice
- Why: `C-05`. The paragraph explaining why the request identity is read off
  the **echo** rather than off `sent` is unchanged and is now amended with one
  sentence recording where the comparison runs; both `:func:` cross-references
  to `check_outcome` in this module's prose are preserved for `19-07` to amend
- Migration: none — `_result_from` is private and `ask`'s signature and return
  are unchanged

*Recorded by `19-02-the-adapter-seam-carries-bytes.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.http_api.routes.canonical_response — a third sibling

- Surface: ccf_runtime.http_api.routes.canonical_response
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `canonical_response(payload, *, replay_ref=None)`
- After: a keyword-only `asked: WireRequest | None = None` after
  `replay_ref`, stamped under `REQUEST_ECHO_HEADER` when it is not `None`.
  `None` — the default — means **no question was posed**, and the header is
  then **absent rather than empty**, on exactly the reasoning `replay_ref`
  already carries: stamping an empty header would make *"no question was
  asked"* and *"the question is named by nothing"* the same observation
- Why: `C-09`. The docstring records the two facts a future HTTP adapter
  needs: that it reconstructs the echo by **decoding this header and never
  from its own state**, and the transport asymmetry — this door requires
  `asked_at` whenever any §6.3 axis is supplied, so an echo published here
  never carries a `None` `temporal_boundary`, whereas `mcp+stdio` carries
  `None` faithfully. It also records that `C-18`'s temporal cross-check has no
  subject on this door: the boundary is built **from** the one `asked_at` the
  route parsed, so a cross-check would compare a value with itself. That is a
  measurement, not an exemption
- Migration: none — additive and keyword-only with a default

*Recorded by `19-06-the-http-door-publishes-the-question.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.http_api.routes.product — the route states what it was asked

- Surface: ccf_runtime.http_api.routes.product.build_router
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `retrieve` called `canonical_response(answer.product,
  replay_ref=answer.replay_ref)`. The question it had assembled reached the
  resolver and went no further; a consumer had no producer-sourced statement
  of what had been answered
- After: `retrieve` builds the echo from `_asked`'s own return and the path
  segment it had already resolved, and passes it through as `asked=`. When
  `_asked` returns `None` — the positive assertion that the caller asked for
  the class's standing answer and posed no question of their own — it passes
  `None` and no header is stamped
- Why: `C-09`. Every field originates producer-side, which is the property a
  future HTTP adapter depends on
- Migration: none for a conforming caller
- Surface: ccf_runtime.http_api.routes.product.build_router
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: a `parameters` value that cannot survive a wire — a `float`, a
  `Decimal`, a `set` — was forwarded verbatim and the door served **200**.
  Measured against the tree: `?parameters={"x": 1.5}` reached the resolver as
  a Python `float`. Separately, an `asked_at` carrying no UTC offset was also
  served **200**
- After: both are refused with a `400`. A wire-unstable parameter carries
  `ERROR_PRODUCT_PARAMETERS_MALFORMED` and the guard's **own** message, which
  names the wire-stable spelling. A naive `asked_at` carries
  `ERROR_PRODUCT_REQUEST_INCOMPLETE` — an axis supplied without the part that
  makes it unambiguous, which is the *the door fills nothing in* that constant
  already records
- Why: `C-09` forces the question to be representable, and `C-10` forbids the
  alternative: publishing no header for a question that cannot be represented
  is a sibling that silently disappears for some questions, which is the exact
  pattern this phase exists to close. `D-08`'s posture — *the fabric never
  silently degrades a caller's value* — decides the direction. Both refusals
  move this door into **agreement** with the MCP product tool, which already
  refuses each of them at `WireRequest` construction; the shared width table
  in `tests/mcp/test_egress_parameters.py` contains no float case, so the
  divergence was real and invisible. This is the narrowing direction
  `D-IMPL-151` finding `B2` already chose for this pair of doors
  `ccf.products.door.WireRequest`'s temporal guard states that *"both product
  doors refuse a naive `asked_at` by name"*. Measured before this change: this
  one did not
- Migration: **a break with no deprecation window** (pre-1.0). A caller
  sending a `float` renders it with `format(value, 'f')`; a caller sending a
  bare local instant attaches an explicit offset. Both spellings are named in
  the refusal message rather than left to be guessed
  read better under a dedicated `product-asked-at-naive` error code.
  `runtime/ccf_runtime/http_api/errors.py` is outside this plan's declared
  file set, so the existing constant whose recorded meaning covers *an axis
  the door refuses to fill in* is reused. `19-08` should rule on whether the
  dedicated code is owed

*Recorded by `19-06-the-http-door-publishes-the-question.md`.*

### Not public Python API

#### tools/check_schema_headers.py

- Surface: tools/check_schema_headers.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: a header naming anything outside `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` was rejected, so deployment-authored documents were unannotatable
- After: a header naming `ccf.declared-artifact` is accepted when the envelope contract accepts the document, the document declares a `class_id`, and that class is not one the SDK owns; the manifest cross-check then compares the entry's `class_id` against the *document's* field rather than the header
- Why: the indirection must not become an escape hatch. An SDK-owned class pointed at the open envelope would silence every constraint it declares, so that is refused, and the two-independent-statements property the cross-check exists for is preserved rather than dropped
- Migration: none — existing headers are unaffected

*Recorded by `15-13-example-00.md`.*

#### tools/check_schema_headers.py

- Surface: tools/check_schema_headers.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: a `$schema` header could only name a member of `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES`, and the envelope route refused only class ids in that same set
- After: both the header lookup and the envelope route's refusal read the union of the manifest-addressable and author-versioned sets
- Why: without the second half, an agent definition could annotate itself as deployment-authored — its class id is absent from `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES`, which used to be the whole test — and have every constraint `AgentDefinition` declares go unchecked
- Migration: none — existing headers are unaffected

*Recorded by `15-14-examples-01-02.md`.*

#### tests/examples/test_ext00_hardening.py

- Surface: tests/examples/test_ext00_hardening.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: two private loader helpers read `tools` and `tool_allowlist` from the root of `experience.yaml`
- After: they read them from under `declaration:`, following the document onto the `DeclaredArtifact` envelope
- Why: unavoidable consequence of the reshape this plan mandates — the envelope route is the only honest `$schema` header available to a deployment-authored class. No assertion in the module changed; only where the two loaders index
- Migration: n/a

#### ccf.graph._writer.MemoryGraphWriter

- Surface: ccf.graph._writer.MemoryGraphWriter
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the typed write seam (`_GraphTxn`, plan 15.2-02) had only SQL
  implementations — `_SqlGraphTxn` driving dialect statements for SQLite
  and Postgres
- After: `_MemoryTxn` implements the nine typed operations natively over
  the store's per-tenant dicts — the first non-SQL proof of the seam —
  honouring the rowcount contract (0 for an ignored duplicate claim id, the
  writer's idempotency signal) and all-or-nothing transactions via lazy
  per-tenant snapshot with in-place rollback. `MemoryGraphWriter` inherits
  the whole `BaseGraphWriter` algorithm with no SQL ClassVars. Both live in
  `ccf.graph._writer` only (CINV-02 — write code outside that module is
  write code outside the guard)
- Why: D-05/D-06 lock that conformance writes go through booted L2, and a
  dict store cannot execute SQL text; the typed seam exists precisely so
  this class could be written without interpreting SQL constants
- Migration: none — the module is import-restricted to `ccf.assembly` /
  `ccf.boot`; deployments reach the writer only through
  `boot(memory=True)`

*Recorded by `15.2-06-memory-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph._writer.Neo4jGraphWriter

- Surface: ccf.graph._writer.Neo4jGraphWriter
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the typed write seam (`_GraphTxn`, plan 15.2-02) had one non-SQL
  implementation (`_MemoryTxn`, plan 15.2-06) beside the shared SQL
  adapter
- After: `_Neo4jTxn` implements the nine typed operations as parameterized
  Cypher over one explicit driver transaction — `MERGE` on the compound
  identity honours the rowcount contract (`nodes_created` is 0 for an
  ignored duplicate claim id, the writer's idempotency signal; for lineage
  the L-axis set-union itself, INV-CH7-17), reads inside the transaction
  see its own writes, and a per-partition `CcfMeta` counter renders the
  insertion-order tiebreak (`rowid`/`BIGSERIAL`). Write-time projections
  (`_neo4j_claim_projection`) store the predicate-relevant claim fields —
  validity window re-rendered through `canonical_timestamp`,
  `entity_class`, string-valued attributes under a prefixed key — so the
  read path needs no JSON parsing in Cypher. `Neo4jGraphWriter` inherits
  the whole `BaseGraphWriter` algorithm with no SQL ClassVars. All of it
  lives in `ccf.graph._writer` only (CINV-02; threat T-15.2-06 — no
  session or transaction construction exists in the reader module beyond
  reads)
- Why: D-05/D-06 lock that conformance writes go through booted L2, and a
  Cypher store cannot execute SQL text; the typed seam exists precisely so
  this class could be written without interpreting SQL constants
- Migration: none — the module is import-restricted to `ccf.assembly` /
  `ccf.boot`; deployments reach the writer only through
  `boot(neo4j_uri=…)`

*Recorded by `15.2-12-neo4j-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph._writer.MongoGraphWriter

- Surface: ccf.graph._writer.MongoGraphWriter
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the typed write seam (`_GraphTxn`, plan 15.2-02) had two non-SQL
  implementations — `_MemoryTxn` (15.2-06) and `_Neo4jTxn` (15.2-12) — beside
  the shared SQL adapter, and every one of them held an open substrate
  transaction
- After: `_MongoTxn` implements the nine typed operations as **buffered
  pymongo bulk operations** — the first *batched* implementation of the seam,
  and the case the `_GraphTxn` contract names in as many words (D-IMPL-88):
  mutating operations resolve their rowcounts before commit from committed
  state read before buffering plus the buffer's own contents (sound because
  INV-CH7-10 guarantees no concurrent writer), and `select_*_chain` merges the
  committed chain with the claim appended earlier in the same transaction.
  Buffering rather than a MongoDB multi-document transaction is deliberate: a
  plain `mongo:7` deployment is a standalone server and multi-document
  transactions require a replica set, which a GitHub Actions service container
  cannot be asked to start — so the adapter's conformance does not depend on a
  topology. Ordering inside a collection is preserved by
  `bulk_write(ordered=True)`, which is what lets the chain restitch update a
  claim the same transaction inserted; every operation is an upsert on its
  compound identity, so the writer's whole algorithm stays idempotent
  (INV-CH7-04). The doubled-edge traversal documents are maintained here, in
  the same buffered transaction as the relationship node they double, so the
  layout cannot drift from the edge it represents. Write-time projections
  (`_mongo_claim_projection`) store the predicate-relevant claim fields —
  validity window re-rendered through `canonical_timestamp`, `entity_class`,
  string-valued attributes whose names `attribute_field_path` admits as
  document field paths. All of it lives in `ccf.graph._writer` only (CINV-02;
  threat T-15.2-06 — no write operation exists in the reader module)
- Why: D-05/D-06 lock that conformance writes go through booted L2, and a
  document store executes neither SQL nor Cypher; the typed seam exists
  precisely so this class could be written without interpreting SQL constants
- Migration: none — the module is import-restricted to `ccf.assembly` /
  `ccf.boot`; deployments reach the writer only through `boot(mongo_uri=…)`

*Recorded by `15.2-13-mongo-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph._writer._FlureeTxn / FlureeGraphWriter

- Surface: ccf.graph._writer._FlureeTxn / FlureeGraphWriter
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: five `_GraphTxn` implementations existed — one shared SQL adapter, a
  dict-backed transaction, a Cypher-backed one and a buffered pymongo one
- After: a sixth, and the first whose commit is a **single HTTP POST**.
  `_FlureeTxn` buffers the nine typed operations as ground JSON-LD triples and
  commits one `delete` + `insert` body with **no `where` clause** — a shape
  probed before it was written (F-A4(a)) and ruled `approved` by Dave on
  2026-08-08, so D-02's letter holds and the SPARQL UPDATE multi-operation
  departure is not taken. Per-operation rowcounts resolve from committed-state
  reads plus the transaction's own buffer, exactly as `_MongoTxn` resolves them;
  no receipt field is consulted, because `flakes_added` does not exist on this
  build and could never have implemented a per-operation contract anyway.
  Supersession and observed-window widening are ground delete/insert pairs
  rather than overwrites, because RDF set semantics makes a bare re-assertion
  *add* a value beside the existing one instead of replacing it — the one place
  `_MongoTxn` is not a complete template. `_fluree_claim_projection` re-renders
  both validity bounds through `parse_timestamp` → `canonical_timestamp`
  (DI-15.2-03 non-reintroduction) and materializes a multi-valued
  `ccf_policy_key`, because this engine's dynamic property access `c[$key]`
  parses, returns `200` and evaluates to **null** — porting the Neo4j policy
  predicate verbatim would have excluded every claim while reporting success
- Why: this is the SUBQ-13 content. `_GraphTxn` states its contract in terms of
  *observable results* rather than mechanism, which is why one Protocol covers a
  held Postgres transaction, a buffered bulk write and a single JSON-LD POST.
  Landing the sixth backend without touching the Protocol — 864 lines added to
  `ccf.graph._writer`, zero deleted — is D-IMPL-88's hedge paying off, measured
  rather than narrated
- Migration: none — `ccf.graph._writer` is import-restricted to `ccf.assembly`
  and `ccf.boot` by CINV-02, so no adopter can hold a reference to either name.
  The write path is reached only through booted L2

*Recorded by `15.3-02-fluree-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.fluree.policy_key

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.fluree.policy_key
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `policy_key` was the *read* side's key construction only. The write
  side held its own copy of the separator (`_POLICY_KEY_SEPARATOR` in
  `ccf.graph._writer`) and joined the `(entity_class, attribute, value)` triple
  by hand, on the stated argument that importing the reader from the writer
  would break the CINV-02 confinement and create a cycle. The argument was
  false — the write path already imported the reader — and the duplicate is
  what made defect CR-01 reachable, because the injectivity guard lived on one
  copy only
- After: the duplicate constant and the hand-rolled join are deleted.
  `_fluree_claim_projection` calls `policy_key` and skips the triples it
  refuses, so a key that cannot be built on the read side is no longer stored
  on the write side. The observable answer does not move: an unencodable stored
  key was already unreachable from the read path (every pushed key carries
  exactly two separators; a smuggled one carries more), and a query naming such
  a triple still declines the pushdown so the fallback answers the whole thing
  (D-04). What moves is that the over-disclosure surface is now closed at both
  ends rather than at one
- Why: D-14 (Dave, 2026-08-09), review finding WR-01. Relocating a
  known-defective duplicate unchanged is worse than either fixing it or
  deferring it explicitly, and the writer split removes the last shred of the
  duplicate's justification
- Migration: none. `policy_key`'s own signature and behaviour are untouched;
  only its second caller is new

*Recorded by `15.4-03-write-modules.md`.*

#### tools/dump_surface.py

- Surface: tools/dump_surface.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `_shape_of` resolved every class with `inspect.signature` on the
  class, returning `__init__` — `(*args, **kwargs)` for every Protocol — and
  applied the same signature rendering to type aliases
- After: a Protocol's own `__call__` is resolved through the MRO and rendered
  with `self` elided; a type alias renders its type, scrubbed of the
  process-local memory address an `Annotated` alias would otherwise embed;
  everything else is untouched
- Why: `DI-15.5-06`, ruled by `D-IMPL-122`. The manifest could not see the
  three-to-five argument widening `15.5-15` shipped, so it was reporting a
  surface stability it had never verified
- Migration: none for adopters. Maintainers should expect `make surface` to
  produce a one-time 36-entry reshape on first run after this change, and
  should read it as the tool's field of view widening rather than as the
  surface moving

*Recorded by `15.6-10-protocol-call-capture.md`.*

#### tools/dump_surface.py

- Surface: tools/dump_surface.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `_shape_of` consulted `_is_type_alias` only inside the branch guarded
  by `kind in ("class", "function")`, so the alias rendering was unreachable for
  any alias `_kind_of` classified as a `constant` — which is every PEP-604 union
- After: the alias test is asked of every export. `kind` is untouched, so an
  alias keeps whatever kind it was recorded under and only the shape moves
- Why: `DI-15.5-06`'s blind spot was narrowed by `D-IMPL-122` and this is a
  piece of it the narrowing missed, in the same plan that measured the rest
- Migration: none for adopters. Maintainers should expect `make surface` to
  produce a one-time three-entry reshape on first run after this change

*Recorded by `15.6-12-single-agent-review-fixes.md`.*

#### src/ccf/products/resolver.py

- Surface: src/ccf/products/resolver.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the docstring asserted that the SDK ships no implementation of this
  Protocol and implied it should not — *"a resolver that scored debt itself
  would be choosing for every deployment, which is CLAUDE.md §6.4 firing"*
- After: it names `ccf.products.debt_scorer.AssemblyDebtScorer` as the shipped
  reference, states the row-selection choice made there, and keeps the argument
  about why the choice is semantically load-bearing. `ProjectionPlanFactoryProtocol`'s
  docstring likewise records Amendment A.2's ruling and the measurement behind
  it, and `GovernedResolver`'s "the debt scorer is deployment code and has no
  artifact form" paragraph was corrected
- Why: the Protocol's argument was good and its conclusion was wrong. Shipping
  no implementation did not spare any deployment the choice; it made every
  deployment write the adapter *and* make the choice unstated. Classified
  `Public surface: false` because no exported Python name changed shape — the
  Protocol's signature, return type and export are untouched, so this entry can
  never correspond to a manifest diff line
- Migration: none — no signature moved

*Recorded by `17-11-reference-debt-scorer.md`.*

#### src/ccf/client/adapters/inprocess.py

- Surface: src/ccf/client/adapters/inprocess.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `_boot` constructed `GovernedResolver` itself, composing
  `AssemblyDebtScorer` inline from two `_artifact` lookups and two
  `DeclaredFactor` wraps. `17-12`'s own docstring named this *"the composition
  `17-13` is expected to lift into `build_governed_resolver(...)`"*
- After: `_boot` calls `build_governed_resolver` with the document's data and
  selects only the artifact **store** itself — `FileArtifactStore`, because
  `artifact_store:` declares a path and a path is what a file-backed store
  takes. `ProductNotResolvable` raised while resolving the two debt keys is
  translated into a `BindingError` naming `binding.yaml`, because at boot every
  key the helper could miss was written there; the original is chained, so
  nothing about the manifest is lost
- Why: a second transport must not grow a second copy of a thirteen-argument
  composition, and the *"never has to know the Protocol exists"* property
  belongs to the seam rather than to one adapter. Classified
  `Public surface: false` because no exported Python name changed shape — the
  adapter's exports are `SCHEME`, `InProcessAdapter` and `reset_boot_cache`,
  all untouched
- Migration: none. The refusal an adopter sees for a mistyped `debt:` key is
  still a `BindingError` and therefore still a `TransportError`, so one
  `except` clause covers it exactly as before

*Recorded by `17-13-resolver-defaults.md`.*

#### binding.yaml (debt:)

- Surface: binding.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `17-12` added the `debt:` block — `half_life_key`,
  `debt_profile_key`, `completeness`, `semantic_consistency` — and flagged it
  as possibly relocatable once `17-13` shipped a default
- After: **unchanged, all four fields, and deliberately so.** What changed is
  what the deployment must *know*: the block declares `D_c`'s **inputs**, and
  the SDK now selects the **machinery** that consumes them. A deployment names
  no scorer, imports nothing from `ccf.products.debt_scorer`, and writes no
  `GraphView` → `DebtFactors` adapter
- Why: none of the four is something a default may supply. The two keys name
  **Control Plane artifacts**, and §6 of the ruling forbids *"new discovery or
  registration machinery in this change"* — a fabric holding two λ_c tables has
  no SDK-knowable right answer, and picking the only one present is a rule that
  silently changes behaviour the day a second is registered. The two factors
  have **no SDK measurement at all**: `DebtFactors` records that position in its
  own docstring, and `17-11` rejected each candidate default in turn (`C = 1` is
  a number the SDK chose inside a delivered product's content address; `C = 0`
  fail-closes every deployment; deriving `C` from the table's breadth couples
  completeness to how many observation types a deployment happens to calibrate)
- Migration: none — no key was added, removed or renamed

*Recorded by `17-13-resolver-defaults.md`.*

#### The two vacuous assertions, repaired

- Surface: tests/client/conformance/test_the_fixture_serves_a_replay_reference.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: added
- Before: `assert_served_parity` compares every declared `Result` field,
  `replay_ref` among them — so two subjects both answering `None` agreed
  perfectly while carrying nothing, and **no conformance leg asserted that a
  served delivery carries a replay reference at all**. Plan `17-10` Task 2
  recorded the precondition in prose and nothing enforced it
- After: a per-subject precondition, run over both real transports, requiring a
  served delivery to carry a reference. It lives at the **binding** rather than
  in `suite.py` because both-`None` is a legitimate deployment shape —
  `served_answer()` returns `None` for the narrower `ServableProduct` and
  `door.py` records that as the honest report — so a suite demanding a reference
  would reject a legitimate deployment on grounds unrelated to the transport.
  What must be true is a fact about *this package's fixture*
- Why: `D-IMPL-151` finding `B1`, the vacuity class surviving inside
  `D-IMPL-148`'s own fix. `D-22`: three controls, including an adapter that
  blanks the reference on the way back, passed into the **real** assertion
- Migration: none — test-tree only
- Surface: tests/mcp/test_egress_parameters.py::assert_same_verdicts
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: five cases, all well-formed JSON objects, and the two door callables
  **raised** when a door refused before the resolver — so a disagreement of the
  form *one door refuses this payload, the other serves it* was outside what the
  shared table could represent, which is why it could not see `B2`
- After: `REFUSED_AT_DOOR` is a verdict like any other and `EXPLICIT_NULL` is a
  case like any other, giving six. The non-vacuity guard is unchanged and still
  demands a **served** case rather than merely a non-empty table, so a pair of
  doors refusing everything is still refused
- Why: a disagreement the table cannot represent is the table's defect too
- Migration: none — test-tree only
- Surface: tests/contracts/test_canonical.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `set(carried) <= PERMITTED_CHILD_VARIABLES` — one-sided, so the
  `D-IMPL-150` `PYTHONPATH` repair going missing could not turn it red, while
  the module docstring claimed it *"holds the set closed"*
- After: `assert_child_environment_is_exactly` compares in both directions and
  names extra and missing separately, with two controls — the pre-`D-IMPL-150`
  environment (asserted to satisfy the old `<=`, so the control cannot silently
  stop being the control) and a leaked parent variable
- Why: `D-IMPL-151` finding `B3`, the same class as the leg `D-IMPL-149` split
- Migration: none — test-tree only

*Recorded by `18-99-close-review-blocking-fixes.md`.*

#### The new controls

- Surface: tests/products/test_door_answer.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. **No test in the tree constructed a `DoorOutcome` at all**,
  and `test_codec.py::test_control_a_wire_outcome_with_both_halves_or_neither_is_refused`
  parametrized only two of the validator's rules — which is why rules 3, 4 and
  5 shipped uncovered
- After: 18 tests. The as-asked echo with its vacuity guard; the
  serialize-once address with a byte-level sabotage that also asserts the
  tampered vessel still validates as a `ContextProductContract`; the refusal
  carrying the echo, captured from the fixture deployment's own gates rather
  than synthesized; `door_question`'s round trip over every parameter and
  temporal shape the wire guard admits; and the five validator rules with one
  assertion each
- Why: `CLNT-12` / `D-22` — every gate carries a named sabotage control. The
  control on the shipped code was run in a scratch copy **outside** the tree
  (`door_answer` patched to echo `resolved_request.asked_at`) and observed
  red on all three echo assertions; no tracked source file was edited and
  reverted
- Migration: none — test-tree only
- Surface: tests/mcp/test_egress_parameters.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the shared case table drove both doors on the parameters axis alone
- After: the MCP leg carries a per-case encoded request derived from the same
  case values the HTTP leg uses, so `assert_same_verdicts` still compares two
  doors on one table. Five legs added for the address cross-check: the
  `product_class` crossing and the `parameters` crossing, each with its vacuity
  guard, and `C-18`'s three regions — a one-microsecond temporal disagreement
  refused by name, agreement answered, and an absent boundary answered rather
  than refused (the guard, and the leg that would go red if the comparison were
  written unguarded)
- Why: `C-16`, `C-18`
- Migration: none — test-tree only
- Surface: tests/crossproc/test_mcp_egress.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `REQUIRED_TOOL_ARGUMENTS` held seven names
- After: eight, with the constant's own prose amended rather than replaced. One
  leg added over the **real** cross-process path asserting that a question
  asked with `temporal_boundary=None` is echoed `None`, and that
  `content_address` equals `sha256` of the vessel text beside it
- Why: the unit proof cannot see a transport that re-labels what it carries,
  and this is the transport where a crossed question is physically possible
- Migration: none — test-tree only

*Recorded by `19-01-one-outcome-shape-and-the-encoded-question.md`.*

#### The repaired and new controls

- Surface: tests/client/test_client.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: five stub adapters and two helpers returned `WireOutcome`; the module
  docstring described the seam as returning an object
- After: every stub returns bytes through the **shipped** `encode_outcome`; the
  object-returning helpers are kept and gain encoding siblings, so a test
  needing the object still has one. No test hand-rolls a wire encoding. The
  docstring is re-aimed at the bytes seam, keeping its reasoning about the two
  lying transports and recording where the lie is now caught. No assertion was
  weakened, retargeted or dropped, and the collected count is unchanged at 16
- Why: `C-05`
- Migration: none — test-tree only
- Surface: tests/client/test_codec.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `SwallowingAdapter.ask` returned an outcome, and four sites read an
  outcome field directly off `decode_outcome`'s return
- After: the stub encodes; the four sites bind `.outcome`. The three
  `decode_outcome` calls inside `pytest.raises` discard the return and are
  genuinely unaffected. Nothing any of them asserts changed; 34 collected,
  unchanged
- Why: `C-05`
- Migration: none — test-tree only
- Surface: tests/client/test_inprocess_adapter.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: 17 tests. The direct `adapter.ask(request)` asserted the **answer**
  half is a degenerate wire; nothing asserted the question half, because there
  was nothing to assert
- After: 19. The existing leg decodes the bytes and holds its `product_json`
  assertion unchanged. Two added: `C-07`'s question-half round trip — the echo
  equals the question sent **and** is not the same object, over a question
  carrying a nested mapping parameter, observed on a **refused** outcome so
  the echo's outcome-level placement (`C-04`) is what carries it — and
  `C-02`/`C-15`'s pair, `None` in and `None` out while the served vessel names
  a concrete decision instant
- Why: `C-07`, `C-02`, `C-14`. The round trip's vacuity guard was run in a
  scratch script outside the tree: the same assertion against a request
  differing in one parameter fails, so the equality is doing work
- Migration: none — test-tree only
- Surface: tests/client/conformance/test_the_suite_fires.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `Mutation = Callable[[WireOutcome], WireOutcome]`; `_Swallowing`
  synthesized a refusal outcome around a captured envelope
- After: `Mutation = Callable[[bytes], bytes]`, and every mutation is parse →
  edit → encode. Each records, in prose, that it parses with
  `WireOutcome.model_validate_json` and **not** with `decode_outcome`, and
  why: the decoder runs the client's own checks, so a control that parsed
  through it would attribute an observed failure to the decoder rather than to
  the leg under test. `_Swallowing` now replays the producer's captured
  **bytes verbatim**, and the leg is handed the refusing question as its
  broken-transport question so the captured echo is the echo of the question
  asked. That makes the control **stronger**: the forged refusal survives every
  codec check there is, so the leg has to catch it on `D-10` grounds alone. 44
  collected, unchanged
- Why: `C-05`, `C-08`
- Migration: none — test-tree only
- Surface: tests/client/conformance/test_the_mcp_binding_fires.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: both wrappers edited the outcome object `super().ask` returned
- After: both parse the wire, edit, and re-encode.
  `_AnswersAnotherQuestion`'s re-label survives as a `model_copy` and is
  marked as the line `19-03` deletes to produce the phase's single-transport
  control. It is worth noticing rather than passing over that the re-label is
  **not** a construction, so no construction counter can see it — a limit
  `19-05`'s AST gate has to state in its own docstring rather than imply
- Why: `C-05`
- Migration: none — test-tree only
- Surface: tests/client/conformance/test_the_fixture_serves_a_replay_reference.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `_DropsTheReplayReference.ask` rebuilt the outcome object
- After: it parses the wire, blanks the reference, and re-encodes — carrying
  `request` through from **`outcome.request`**, the producer's echo, and never

- Why: `C-05`
- Migration: none — test-tree only
- Surface: tests/client/test_wire.py
- Public surface: false

- After: `-> bytes`. Neither stub ever speaks; the repair is for the reader and

- Why: `C-08`
- Migration: none — test-tree only
- Surface: tests/client/test_binding.py

- After: `-> bytes`, on the same terms as above

#### The controls

- Surface: tests/http_api/test_content_address_header.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: 5 tests, all about the content address
- After: 10. Five added, each named against `C-09`: the echo round trip
  (base64url-decode, `model_validate_json`, **total** equality against a
  `WireRequest` built from what the caller sent, never read back off the
  response); its **named sabotage control**, which hands that same assertion
  function a question differing in one parameter and requires it to fail; the
  two refusal legs; and a shared **vacuity guard** asserting the
  representable form of both still serves, without which both refusal legs
  pass against a door that refuses everything
- Why: `C-09`, `CLNT-12`, `D-22`
- Migration: none — test-tree only
- Surface: tests/http_api/test_routes.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: 33 tests. The product door legs here run against the fixture's
  deliberately **ungoverned** app, which refuses and therefore never reaches
  `canonical_response`
- After: 35, plus a module-local governed app built for the same reason
  `test_content_address_header.py` and `test_product_entitlement.py` each
  build one. The absence leg asserts `header not in response.headers` —
  absence, never `== ""` — and its **named sabotage control** hands the same
  assertion a response to a request that *did* pose a question and requires it
  to fail. That control is the load-bearing half: on its own the absence
  assertion passes against a route that never stamps the header at all, which
  is precisely the vacuity this repository has shipped before
- Why: `C-09`, `CLNT-12`, `D-22`
- Migration: none — test-tree only

*Recorded by `19-06-the-http-door-publishes-the-question.md`.*
